Class 4 ⇒ `ActionLaneFreeze` recorded as DATA + a **flag-now** card under change class `terms` (a sanction withdrawn is a terms change, which on an ACTIVE lane routes flag-now through the same server-side S14.4 rule every other card uses). **An explicit negative test drives all four auth-shaped signals and asserts NO park action is ever produced** — a policy event that dies in a retry loop is a platform defect (P-T08-2's named worst case). `internal/scheduler/limits.go` is unchanged. A probe that could not COMPLETE proves nothing about sanction: it fails the canary as `unclear` and routes to the digest, never fabricating a revocation.
- **Behavioral canary (R25).** Scheduled fixed-prompt mini-eval per lane on ⚙ `canary.behavioral_interval`, alerting on a score drop past a structural tolerance. Its doc comment states plainly that it is **the ONLY drift detection available to the subscription lanes**. It REUSES the S14.8 runner seam and builds no second runner: the shell hook runs `evals.Runner` over the committed `bump-probe-v1` battery, so **no eval case content is invented** and promptfoo's CLI facts are not re-derived. A drop is a `models`-class card carrying **no** model-id subject — a subscription lane serves whatever the provider points it at, and guessing an id would flag the wrong assets — so the card RECORDS that no revalidation fired (OQ4(a)), which is the residual gap made visible. **Carried B5-5 dependency honoured:** the binary does not exist on the host until the B5-gate act, so at landing the leg is stub-verified only and composes to nil against a real binary's absence — `SANCTIONED SKIP (CONVENTIONS §10)`.
- **Logprob canary (R26) — LOCAL LANE ONLY, and the reason is CITED, not inferred.** Spec S03.7 verbatim: *"No logprobs on the Z.AI coding endpoint (`logprobs:true` → 200 but no `logprobs` key, endpoint-wide) → the Z.AI lane is behavioral-eval-only for drift detection; only the local lane gets the logprob canary"* [SPIKE P2-S1 Probe 8]; the wrapped Anthropic CLI exposes none either, so **both** subscription lanes are behavioral-eval-only. The canary **REFUSES** a non-local lane (`ErrLaneNotLocal`) — a checkable negative proven for `anthropic`/`zai`/an unknown lane/the empty lane, with a non-vacuity check that the local lane gets past the gate — and the layer never SCHEDULES a logprob job on any other lane. It invents no inference path: the probe is this package's OWN triage duty on its OWN schema against a FROZEN prompt, and the tracked scalar is `local.LabelMargin` at the `change_class` token (the S12.5 top1−top2 gap S12.9 calibration fits). It costs no allowance, so it is NOT gated by the arm — but it rides the `AdvisoryMeter` platform-run seam like the second pass, and with no meter it is **skipped honestly rather than issued unmetered**. Unlike a subscription lane it HAS a model id — the seat's — so it is the second producer that can carry a real model-id subject into the S14.8 runbook. No margin available is recorded as such, never as a margin of 0 (which would read as a coin-flip that never happened).
- **Model-list drift (R27) — the first real producer of a model-id subject.** Observed-vs-config diff per account (P-T17-3; S03.6), riding ⚙ `canary.auth_interval` because S14.6 ¶3 says it *"rides the same schedule"* as the other per-account cheap real request and inventing a constant S18 never ratified would contradict that sentence. **The `config` side is EMPTY at v0** — no per-account model configuration is registered anywhere — so the canary says so on its record and falls back to the previous OBSERVATION rather than pretending an empty config means every model was removed. Lists are normalized (trimmed, deduped, sorted) so a provider reordering its catalogue is not drift. Each added/removed model id becomes its own `drift.finding` with class `models` and that id as `Subject`, so **each fires the S14.8 revalidation runbook** while all of them share a fingerprint and fold into ONE card. **6A recorded that its own sources name no model id and that this layer would be the producer that does — that is now proven end to end.** The per-run subject fan-out is capped (25, structural) so a whole-catalogue rotation cannot fire unbounded flags; the card still names the full count. A non-2xx catalogue answer is an ERROR, never an empty list — reporting "no models" for a 403 would raise a removal finding for every model the account has.
- **Engine memory + compaction join the canary set as ROWS, not probes** [G2 §Follow-ups; S05.7]: two `study` rows (`canary-engine-memory-features`, `canary-engine-compaction-behavior`, group `S14.6-canary-set`), never polled, surfaced through `PendingReview` for the S16.7 quarterly pass. Neither has a wire signal a scheduled probe could read — engine memory is a config-and-behaviour surface of an adopted engine, and compaction behaviour shows up as the already-minted `compaction.anomaly` on REAL runs — so a probe here would fake coverage. The registration makes the gap visible instead.
- **Conformance canaries (R28) — coordinator disposition OQ5(a): a DUENESS SURFACER.** The adapter suites are Go tests and the release artifact is a static binary with no toolchain, so the daemon cannot run them, and re-implementing the tier-R checks in-process would give Sinet two implementations of one conformance claim that can disagree. So a due **weekly lane-affecting** adapter row (`adapter-anthropic`, `adapter-local`) raises a card and the RUN stays an operator/CI act recording through the existing `conformance.RecordResult` into the existing row — **no new registry row, no new event type** (results are the already-minted `eval.score_recorded`), and **`internal/conformance` is unchanged**. `RecordAdapterSuite` refuses any row that is not one of those two, so the canary path cannot write a result into an obligation it does not own. **This narrows B5-4's "dueness raises nothing at v0" to "dueness raises nothing EXCEPT the two weekly lane-affecting adapter rows"** — a deliberate narrowing, recorded here. The card's change class is **`unclear`**, the S14.2 abstain member, because that is the honest statement (nothing has been observed; the suite that would observe it is due and unrun), which routes it to the **daily digest** — a due suite is not a stall, a loop or a spend anomaly, and flag-now is budgeted at ⚙ `watchdog.flag_now_target` = 2/day for the things that are. One reminder per cadence period, gated off the log so a standing obligation cannot re-card on every sweep.
- **A canary failure raises a card, NEVER a kill (R29).** Mirrored from the watchdog's own wall: an AST test proves this package references none of `StateTombstoned`/`StateCrashed`/`StateDiedAtGate`/`StateCompleted`/`StateFinalized`/`Tombstone`/`Terminate`/`SIGKILL`/`SIGTERM`, carries none of those as string literals (so `run.State("tombstoned")` cannot slip past the ident check), and imports neither `os/exec` nor `syscall` — with a non-tautology probe that also checks the wall does NOT reject the vocabulary the layer legitimately uses (`lane-freeze` is a recorded scheduler ACTION, not a kill). The auth canary is the sharpest case precisely because Class 4's action is named `lane-freeze`: **honouring a frozen lane belongs to the dispatch path; the canary records the verdict and raises the card.**
- **Metering posture (R24), stated on the record itself.** Every `canary.result` payload carries `purpose_tag` = `probe` (`metering.PurposeProbe`) and `workload_class` = `probe` (`scheduler.ClassProbe`) — the EXISTING constants, coupled by identity in a test, never re-declared. Canary consumption is metered like everything and itemized on the lane owner's meters (D4/D7); both v0 paid lanes are flat-rate and `MeteredExceptions` is EMPTY (G1 P7), so a canary consumes **allowance and pressure, never dollars**, and is never built on a metered path. The auth and model-list probes are zero-token status/catalogue GETs, so no S10.1 usage row is due for them (S10.1 writes one row per **paid model call**). The behavioral leg DOES make paid model calls and promptfoo issues them itself, so Sinet sees no per-call usage stream — **itemizing that leg's consumption is an explicit ARMING PRECONDITION referred to the coordinator, not a decision this packet took.**
- **$0 POSTURE, composed at the root rather than asserted.** With no operator arm (`SINET_CANARY_ARM`) none of the three legs that would dial a provider — auth, behavioral, model-list — holds a **probe**, so the layer physically cannot spend; a disarmed sweep writes zero `canary.result` rows (proven by test at both the package and the composition root). The legs are still CONSTRUCTED and still scheduled, so each is counted and names its reason — see the drain-round-1 record below, which corrects two overstatements in this bullet's first landing. Arming alone conjures nothing: on this tree the arm activates the behavioral leg only, and only with the pinned runner installed. **Pre-registered projection at default cadences:** real dollars **$0.00** on every leg (flat-rate lanes, empty exception list); API-equivalent **$0.00/week** for auth + model-list (zero-token GETs) and **≈ $0.11/week** for the behavioral leg (2 lanes × 5 tasks, ~1 k tokens/task at Sonnet-class list rates) — **≈ $5.70/yr**, an ESTIMATE and not a table read, since the S10.3 price table ships EMPTY by design. **Stop line: any non-zero REAL-dollar total attributable to `purpose_tag=probe`.** On flat-rate lanes that figure must be exactly $0.00; a non-zero one means a lane left flat-rate or a metered exception was enabled, which is itself the alarm — disarm and raise flag-now. Secondary: probe-tagged consumption past **1 % of a lane's daily allowance** — the spec calls the canary "negligible by construction", and past 1 % it is not.
- **Dueness derives from STORED state**, as everywhere else: the last `canary.result` per (kind, lane) in the log — no side table, **no ticker driving any cadence**. Proven across a simulated restart (a brand-new layer does not re-run inside the cadence) and a wall-clock jump (it does run once the cadence has elapsed, which a ticker would have slept through). Both declared ⚙ intervals are read **by dotted key at evaluation time**, with live-apply proven for EACH by a mid-run tighten to its clamp floor that takes effect on the next sweep with no restart; neither default appears as a numeric literal anywhere in the package.
- **Structural constants, each with its reason** (S18 ratifies exactly two canary keys and none of these; the §7 `sseBatchSize` precedent, interim under the standing settings-tab directive): the **logprob cadence** (24 h — both ratified canary keys are named for other canaries and `canary.behavioral_interval`'s clamp is shaped for a paid weekly mini-eval, so consuming either here would misappropriate a ratified clamp; daily is the dead-man canary's coarse cadence and this probe costs no allowance); the **behavioral drop tolerance** (0.2 — a five-task battery moves in steps of 0.2, so anything smaller alerts on a single case flipping and 0.2 makes exactly one lost case the threshold); the **logprob margin tolerance** (1.0 nat — greedy decoding makes a frozen prompt reproducible, so small drift is quantization/build noise and a whole nat is a decode-path change); the **conformance dueness window** (7 d, matching the rows' own weekly cadence — one reminder per cadence period is the most a reminder can honestly be); the **canary subject cap** (25); the **probe timeout** (10 s) and **body caps** (8 KiB for policy-ban text, 1 MiB for a catalogue); and the **shell canary tick** (15 min — the TICK, never a cadence; the tightest possible cadence is ⚙ `canary.auth_interval`'s 6 h clamp floor, so a minute-grained tick would buy only wake-ups).
- **Coordinator dispositions, recorded:** **OQ4(a)** as 6A recorded it, now with a producer that satisfies its positive branch — the model-list canary carries a real model id into `FlagByModel`, and every non-`models` class plus every `models` class without a model-id subject records on its card that no revalidation was triggered, with the reason. **OQ5(a)** as above, including the narrowing of B5-4's dueness rule. **R31 held:** no new ⚙, no new migration, no new module, no new lock entry.
- **Files touched beyond the brief's §6 B list, declared:** `internal/watchlist/seed.go` + `watchlist.go` (the R27 canary-set rows and their group constant — R27 is a B requirement and rows are the only honest discharge), `internal/watchlist/{decay,drift,importwall}_test.go` (three 6A assertions that named `canary.result` as declare-only and the two ⚙ canary keys as "not consumed here" — both were true of 6A and are false now that the sibling half has landed; updated honestly rather than deleted), and `internal/api/projection_drift_test.go` (TEST ONLY — the added proofs that `canary.result` tags `inbox` and that a canary failure derives into one operator-only drift card; `internal/api`'s three production files are byte-unchanged, which is the point those tests exist to prove).

### §34-B drain round 1 (P3-B5-6B, 6 evaluator findings — all 6 actioned)

Architecture and every guarded invariant held under adversarial probing, and all three of the packet's referrals were ruled acceptable (the 6A test edits were confirmed as strengthenings, not weakenings). Six things drained. Two of them were **this section overstating what the code did** — recorded here rather than quietly edited away:

- **D1 — the armed path was incomplete and the record implied otherwise.** `buildCanaryLayer` composed only the behavioral leg under the arm; the auth and model-list legs had no composition path at all, and because `due()` skipped a nil leg they never even appeared in the sweep accounting — a gap visible nowhere. The first landing's "the three legs … are nil" also read as though the arm activated all three. **What is true:** arming is necessary but NOT sufficient. Both v0 paid lanes ride wrapped CLIs, so there is no per-lane HTTP endpoint and no broker credential accessor composed anywhere in this tree, and inventing either would be new structural config this packet has no mandate for. So every real-request leg is now **constructed either way**, holding either a probe or a NAMED reason it has none: unarmed, all three name the arm env; armed, auth and model-list name the gate-time material they still wait for and behavioral names the missing pinned runner. A leg that cannot run is **scheduled, counted and explained** on every sweep instead of vanishing, the armed log line names every leg it could not compose, and the arm activates exactly one leg today. Composing the auth and model-list probes is a **B5-gate item**, tracked alongside the already-recorded behavioral-metering arming precondition.
- **D2 — the logprob canary's measurement path was entirely untested.** Only the lane-refusal and never-scheduled negatives existed, so the half that actually measures anything was unproven. It now runs tier-F end to end on a `local.FakeServer` + a real `local.Duty`: baseline established quietly → a margin move past the tolerance raising a `models`-class card carrying the **seat model** as its revalidation subject → the tolerance boundary from the inside (a 0.9-nat move passes) → the honest no-margin-available branch (recorded as a failure with the abstain class, **no** fabricated measurement, and it does not poison the next baseline) → the unmetered-skip branch. The fixture builder is itself proven non-tautological against the production `local.LabelMargin`, and it uses the constructor `internal/local` already ships rather than inventing a second one. Verified by mutation: making the tolerance unreachable, dropping the subject, and inverting the baseline gate each fail the suite.
- **D3 — the no-kill wall's in-suite probe was tautological.** It re-ran the matchers against the banned lists, which is true by construction, and never exercised the AST walk. The walk is now factored out and run against **planted source** for each forbidden shape — a `Terminate` call, a `StateTombstoned` ident, `run.State("tombstoned")` built from a string literal, `os/exec`, `syscall`, a SIGKILL literal, a bare kill verb — asserting it trips on every one, plus a negative that it does **not** trip on the package's legitimate vocabulary (`lane-freeze`, `flag-now`, `policy-revocation-suspected`, `park-probe`).
- **D4 — this section overstated the disarmed sweep.** It claimed a disarmed sweep "raises zero cards", which is true only under the nil-registry test setup. With a wired conformance registry a disarmed sweep **does** raise the two dueness cards, by design (R28) — the conformance surfacer is not a real-request leg and is not gated by the arm. The bullet above now says exactly what is proven: zero `canary.result` rows.
- **D5 — the disarmed label was dishonest for the logprob canary.** `ErrCanaryDisarmed` names `SINET_CANARY_ARM`, but the logprob canary returned it for a nil `Duty`/`Meter` — a local-stack or metering absence, not an arming matter, and this canary is $0 and never gated by the arm at all. The two are now distinct sentinels (`ErrCanaryDisarmed` / `ErrCanaryStackAbsent`), each wrapping its named reason, and `CanarySweep` counts `Disarmed` and `StackAbsent` separately. A test asserts the logprob skip reason does not blame the arm env.
- **D6 — the recorded observed-model list was unbounded.** A degenerate catalogue marshalled into the `canary.result` payload trips the ⚙ `state.event_payload_cap` Append gate — probed live at 5,000 ids: **240,442 bytes > 65,536**, the append refused, and because the refusal happens before the record lands the lane would **never baseline** and the canary would silently stop working. The recorded list is now bounded (250 ids, structural, with its reason) with the **full count kept** and a truncation marker set. A truncated baseline cannot be diffed by id — naming ids from a bounded prefix would invent a removal for every id the cap dropped — so an oversized lane is compared by a **digest over the full list**: an unchanged catalogue passes, a changed one is still detected, and the finding claims **no** subject rather than a guessed one. Tested end to end, and mutation-verified: removing the bound reproduces the refused append.

## 35. Benchmark-practice conventions (P3-B5-7; S14.7, BENCH-REG, S10.4/S10.10, S14.2/§29, S18.4 R9)

`internal/benchmark` is the S14.7 machinery around **BENCH-REG** — `Spec/benchmark-preregistration-v1.md`, the signed pre-registration. **The protocol is BENCH-REG, not this package.** S14.7 is explicit: "none of its registered numbers is this spec's to change — amendments happen only via BENCH-REG §17, and silent drift between that text and the running platform is a platform defect (its own clause)." Every packet touching this area inherits that rule: implement the frozen text, never re-derive a threshold, never "improve" a registered label, never edit the registration. The package owns the LOGIC; the SHELL owns WHEN (one sampling goroutine, the §31/§34 precedent).

- **Registered numbers are MARKED READ-ONLY DATA, and the tests are the anti-drift instrument.** Every frozen value — gate limbs (`m ≥ 20`, `G ≥ 0.90`), the alarm threshold (`1 − G > 0.95`), the done-directly minimum (`≥ 10`), the §13/§5 labels verbatim, the §15 honest-claims table, the §6 arm-parity declaration, the §10 domain names — lives in `registered.go` as a named constant carrying `RegisteredMarker` ("registered — changing this value requires a re-registration commit") and its BENCH-REG §-ref, surfaced for display by `RegisteredValues()`. **`registered_test.go` asserts each value against the REGISTRATION FILE** (section-extracted from `Spec/benchmark-preregistration-v1.md`), so a constant edited without a §17 amendment fails the build. NONE of them is a ⚙ row (S18.4 R9: "only `benchmark.sampling_rate` is a live dial"); the tally stays **118/33** and `internal/settings/index.go` is byte-unchanged.
- **Import wall** (AST-pinned, exact-or-prefix, non-tautology probe, plus a FORWARD allowlist so a new edge is deliberate): `storage`/`eventlog`/`run`/`scheduler`/`metering` + stdlib ONLY. It never imports `evals`/`verify`/`intake`/`review`/`accept`/`api`/`settings`/`worker`/`stage`/`adapters`/`watchdog`/`watchlist`. ⚙ rides the package's own narrow `Settings` interface; the §10.1(d) floor read, the §2 frozen statement and both arms' texts ride func seams composed at the shell root (`internal/shell/benchmark_seams.go`) — the §33/§34 reason holds: the `evals→verify→intake→adapters` graph stays out of an observability leaf. `internal/api` imports nothing from it (reverse wall asserted).
- **NO-KILL / SET-NOTHING wall (AST-asserted, the §31 precedent applied to this scope):** the package references none of `Kill`/`Terminate`/`Tombstone`/`Cancel`/`Park`/`Transition(Tx)`/`StopAdmission`, none of the run-state VALUES as string literals, and neither `os/exec` nor `syscall`. §12's alarm is a flag-now card plus a **visible, queryable expansion freeze** — breadth is an operator act, so the freeze binds by visibility and by gate limb (c), never by killing or parking anything (D1.3). S14.7's "this section evaluates and displays; it sets nothing" is asserted as a PROPERTY: a full gate + platform-gate + readout pass changes no table but the benchmark bookkeeping row.
- **The statistic is stdlib integer arithmetic (§7, no dependency).** `G = P(Binomial(m+1, ½) ≤ w)` is computed exactly with `math/big` (a finite sum of binomial coefficients over `2^(m+1)`), converted to `float64` only at the display boundary; `GExact` returns the rational. The registration's own reference table is the pinned fixture set, asserted row for row — **13/20 = 0.905 clears, 12/20 = 0.808 does not**, the 0/4·2/10·4/15·6/20 alarm-zone `1−G` values, and the §15 "n=20 rejection needs ≥15/20" row proven from first principles by `FixedTestTailAtLeast`. Fixed-n views are READOUTS: `G` is the only decision input, and no fixed-n rule is wired into a decision.
- **Migration 0014** (immutable once committed; 0001–0013 byte-untouched): `benchmark_pairs` (immutable identity + the §3 working state, a `recorded` pair FROZEN by trigger per §17, no-delete because benchmark records are keep-forever), `benchmark_domains` (the OQ3 state surface — durable first-gate-opened marker, write-once by trigger, + current epoch id/started/identity/seq), `benchmark_sampler` (the one-row DURABLE cursor), and `ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN benchmark_opt_in` **DEFAULT 0** (the 0003 precedent). `benchmark_domains` gates NOTHING outside the package — it is bookkeeping OF the practice, and the SET-nothing clause is exactly that reading.
- **Sampling is DERIVE-FROM-LOG with a DURABLE cursor.** The hook tails `deliverable.accepted` rows (`internal/review`/`internal/accept` are neither edited nor imported; the canonical type STRING is read directly, the §31/§34 pattern). Unlike a detection tail, the cursor is persisted rather than bootstrapped at the head: sampling is uniform-random among ELIGIBLE tasks and that uniformity is FROZEN (§4.1), so a restart-skipped accept is a lost DRAW, not a neutral one. Each event's decision and its cursor advance commit in one tx. The frozen §4.2 five-limb conjunction is implemented with a concrete source per limb and the failing limb recorded (log level, not a card); limb 4 is satisfied by construction at v0 and the reading is documented, not faked. The draw is ONE independent uniform draw per eligible task against the live-read phase rate — **no stratification, prioritization or quota path exists** (asserted by code scan), and rate+phase are recorded on the pair.
- **Blind pairing (§3).** ONE uniform presentation template: format normalized (front-matter stripped, line endings, bullet glyphs, blank-line runs), **length NEVER truncated** and the confound reported alongside. Position is randomized per pair and STORED — and it is arm-identity data, so the pre-record read surfaces have no field for it: `PendingPair` and `InboxBenchmarkVerdict` carry no arm, position, run or model, and the queries filling them select no such column (asserted structurally in both packages). The verdict form's mandatory guess is **STRUCTURAL, not validated**: `Answer`'s fields are unexported, `NewAnswer` requires the guess, and the zero `Answer` is refused — a guess-less verdict is INEXPRESSIBLE (§3.3 frozen). **Reveal reads the committed record**, never a mutable flag: the §14 append and the row's move to `recorded` commit in ONE WriteTx, and `Reveal` queries the event log.
- **The direct arm is §2's, and nothing more.** ONE run row created and ONE `scheduler.Enqueue(..., ClassBackground)` under the REQUESTER's own user_id — the existing spawn gate, pressure admission and per-checkpoint gates throttle it like any background work, with **zero new admission machinery** (G2 Def.4; S10.4). The package contains no retry, no re-dispatch, no follow-up turn, no verification round, no worker template and no knowledge injection: the asymmetry IS the treatment under test (§6). Ordinary metering and ceilings apply as platform economics — but a ceiling that TRUNCATED the arm is recorded on the pair as a §6 parity note and flagged for the coordinator, never absorbed.
- **Epochs (§9).** `assignEpochTx` is the ONE site that opens an epoch: the DIRECT arm's observed model identity splits it, a platform-arm change never does (recorded per pair), and an unobserved identity is not a changed one. G, the gate and the alarm evaluate the CURRENT epoch only; §16 history views show epochs side by side; the §13 done-directly median deliberately pools all epochs (§13.2's letter — it is an honesty figure, not a decision statistic) and annotates them. **Nexus bench-02 is unpoolable by construction**: no import, no seed, no column, no code path could introduce a pre-Sinet pair.
- **§29 flip mechanics.** `benchmark.pair_recorded` and `decision.recorded` flipped declare→minted and `benchmark.alarm` was newly minted, all with real provenance + `producerTypes()` entries in the same packet, verdicts `CONFORMS`/`ADMIT`, `SchemaVersion` 1, and representative goldens joining the required-field conformance set. Inventory **82/11 → 85 minted / 9 declare-only (94 registered)**; families 11 and 14 are now fully minted. `eval.score_recorded` gained NO new producer: S14.7's v0 scope is pair-shaped, limb (d) READS existing floor/sweep records, and the standing questions are registrations. `FamilyBenchmarkEval` joined the INBOX branch of `internal/api/topics.go` (a DATA change proven by test) so a benchmark decision surface is live rather than snapshot-only; the four-topic set is untouched.
- **No benchmark number without its epistemics (§15).** The package exposes exactly ONE published-rate shape, `PublishedRate`, carrying win rate, n (=m), G, 1−G, tie rate, both-bad rate, decline rate, guess accuracy, the §5 "partially unblinded (guess accuracy g%)" label when due, the §15 table verbatim and the §6 declaration. **There is no bare-rate accessor** — an AST assertion refuses any exported rate-named function returning a lone float. Nothing is ever suppressed for blindness failure; it is LABELLED.
- **Cards DERIVE — no card table, no bare `asks` row.** `InboxSnapshot` gains `BenchmarkVerdicts` (from `benchmark_pairs`, owner-scoped to the **REQUESTER**, who is the voter — one of the few inbox cards a member owns and the operator does not) and `BenchmarkAlarms` (from `benchmark.alarm` rows, latest per domain, standing until a clear supersedes it; platform-scope ⇒ operator-only). HTTP verbs and React rendering are **B6's** (§31 precedent) — this packet ships projection data and package-level verbs.
- **Consent is not delegable.** The §4.2.1 opt-in defaults OFF, no boot step turns it on, and `SetOptIn` refuses to flip anyone's flag but the actor's own — including the operator's. The flip logs as `decision.recorded`.
- **The standing questions register (R25).** S14.7's four questions are `study`-class `watch_rows` in group `S14.7-questions` (`internal/watchlist/seed.go` — a sanctioned cross-package seed edit): data-only, never polled, immutable by trigger, removable only by a dated funeral edit, surfaced through `PendingReview` for the S16.7 quarterly pass. **No analysis machinery is built at v0** — the S06.9 hook already records the P-T05-2 data; the row records that it is owed an analysis.
- **Honest absences, recorded rather than faked.** No pair can complete until the dispatcher captures a single-shot direct-arm output and B6 wires the verdict form, so `benchmarkDirectTextSeam` returns a clear error naming the clause it cannot satisfy — a placeholder body would render a "blind pair" of the platform against itself and make every downstream statistic fiction. §11's low-accrual paragraph is a readout NOTE and no low-n alternate gate exists in code (pre-committing a weaker gate was considered and rejected as a built-in goalpost move). The §5 calibration pilot and §4.5 bring-up seed are operator intents; nothing is built for them.
- **No new things.** NO new ⚙ key (tally 118/33; `benchmark.sampling_rate` consumed by dotted key, live-read, live-apply proven by a mid-run mutation test), NO new dependency (`components.lock` 21, `go.mod` unchanged — the statistic is stdlib), NO edit to `internal/metering/receipt.go` (per-run receipts keep the §13.1 heuristic line forever; the stage-2 feed is this package's read API and both labels ship side by side), and **$0 spent** — every arm in the battery is a tier-F fake and real accrual is a bring-up act under BENCH-REG §4.1's own schedule. Structural constants, each with its reason: the sampling loop TICK (1 min — the sampler's cursor is durable, so the tick decides only how promptly a card appears, never how often a task is considered) and the tail batch (256, the `sseBatchSize` precedent), interim under the standing settings-tab directive.

### §35 drain round 1 (P3-B5-7 drain r1, D1–D10) — readings and corrections

- **The §2 statement is the CONFIRMED specification, not the arriving request (D1).** The direct-arm brief seam reads the S06 **artifact of record** (the spec markdown of record), hash-verified against its ref, and refuses a drifted or absent artifact. Intake's bounded revisions can move the confirmed text a long way from what the requester first typed, and §3.1 requires both arms answer the IDENTICAL frozen statement — sourcing the raw ask would have put the arms on different questions and made every later comparison a measurement of the intake pipeline. WHICH artifact was used is captured **at dispatch** on the direct arm's `run.created` detail (durable, no schema change) and lands on the §14 record as `statement_source`; a later revision can never rewrite which text a past pair answered. At v0 intake has no attachment channel separate from the specification (supplied inputs render INTO the markdown), so "identical attachments" holds by construction and the artifact ref is what travels.
- **Decline rate is declined ÷ SAMPLED (D2), per (domain, epoch)** — the denominator is every pair drawn, including those still awaiting an answer, because a requester who is asked and never answers is also selective participation (§4.2.5). In-flight pairs carry no epoch id (an epoch is decided at record time), so an epoch-scoped accrual counts them only for the CURRENT epoch, which is where they will land.
- **Guess accuracy is per VOTE, and publishes its own n (D3, declared reading).** §5's measurement is the per-vote guess, and a tie or both-bad vote still saw both rendered responses, so it still measures blindness. The accuracy therefore rests on a WIDER sample than the win rate's n (=m, non-tied only, §8). The brief's "at the same n" gloss is satisfied in the sense that binds — the two travel on ONE published shape and neither renders alone — and `GuessN` ships beside `n` so a reader sees which sample each figure rests on instead of assuming they match.
- **The dispatch→render driver is named follow-on scope (D4).** No production code calls `DispatchDirectArm`/`RenderBlind` yet, so a sampled pair rests at `sampled`. This is correct and honest at this phase — a pair cannot complete without single-shot direct-arm capture (the dispatcher's) and the B6 verdict form — but the DRIVER is its own obligation and must not fall between them: it lands as a **B5-gate-batch line** alongside those two, not as an assumed side effect of either.
- **Three recorded readings (D5).** (a) OQ3's "same tx" first-open is not reachable past an external limb-(d) seam and never could be; the conditional write plus migration 0014's write-once trigger achieve the disposition's intent (durable, opens once) without holding a write tx across an out-of-package call. (b) A DECLINED pair's row stays SQL-mutable while a recorded one is frozen: the record of record for a decline is the append-only EVENT, and the row is working state over it — B5-8's retention pass may harden the row alongside its keep-forever enforcement. (c) `Posterior` is decision-statistic INTERNAL, not a published-rate surface: the gate and alarm read it because G is the only decision input, and `PublishedRate` remains the one shape a rate renders in.
- **An alarm is cleared by the OPERATOR (D6).** `DisposeAlarm` checks the one S01.9 role bit against the users row in the data layer, exactly as the §4.2.1 consent flip enforces its own actor. §12 calls the disposition an operator act, and the alarm exists because the platform may be losing its own benchmark — letting any principal clear it would make the one signal designed to be hard to ignore trivially ignorable.
- **Comment truth (D7).** `internal/conformance` no longer claims `benchmark.pair_recorded` is declare-only (false since the B5-7 flip); the pre-record projection cites the assertions that exist (`TestRevealOnlyAfterTheRecord`, `TestVerdictCardRevealsNoArmIdentity`); and the entropy-fallback comment states what it actually does — `n-1` misses at every rate below 100%, and at 100% the rate mandates sampling regardless, so a broken source under-accrues and can never manufacture a sample.
- **The alarm boundary is pinned to exact fixtures, not to itself (D8):** 3/12 → G = 189/4096, 1−G ≈ 0.954, **trips**; 2/9 → G = 7/128, 1−G ≈ 0.945, **does not**. One record either side of the registered line, so a threshold edited in either direction breaks exactly one of them.
- **An epoch's identity is its first NON-EMPTY direct-arm identity (D9).** A declined pair records no direct arm, so grouping on the raw column could display an epoch as having no identity — misreporting the very thing §9 says an epoch IS. Ordering still follows the epoch's earliest recorded pair, so the timeline is unaffected; an epoch with only declines honestly reports no observed identity rather than borrowing one.
- **§6 ships VERBATIM (D10),** parentheticals included — "(e.g., its own web search)" and "(report 12 §2.6, negative finding)". §6 is registered text under §1: tightening it is precisely the "improving a registered label" §17 forbids, and the pin test now asserts each bullet against the registration file with only markdown emphasis stripped.

## 36. Retention, run-summary & history-index conventions (P3-B5-8A; S14.9, S02.1–S02.2, S13.10, S14.10 ¶4)

`internal/retention` is the S14.9 retention substrate: the run summary written at the run's terminal transition, the scheduled compaction pass, the keep-forever allowlist that makes the 11.3 exporter boundary structural, and the S14.10 ¶4 index layer (FTS5 + rollups) over what survives. It is a LEAF — `storage` + `eventlog` + stdlib, AST-pinned with a FORWARD allowlist and a non-tautology probe (`importwall_test.go`); the sanctioned allowlist is storage/eventlog/settings, and `internal/settings` is **permitted but unused** because ⚙ rides the package's own narrow `Settings` interface by dotted key (the §2 house pattern), so the actual import set is a strict subset. The run-end edge and the local-tier narrator ride func seams composed at the shell root (`internal/shell/retention_seams.go`). The package owns the LOGIC; the shell owns WHEN (two goroutines) and holds no clock inside the package (AST-asserted: no `time.NewTicker`/`NewTimer`/`Tick`).

- **OQ1(a) — the NARROWED append-only trigger (migration 0015; 0001–0014 byte-untouched).** 0001's blanket `run_events_no_update` made S14.9's mandated strip impossible to execute at all. 0015 DROPs it and creates two narrower triggers in its place: `run_events_identity_immutable` (`BEFORE UPDATE OF` event_seq/run_id/generation/user_id/type/schema_version/ts → ABORT) and `run_events_payload_compaction_only`, which permits EXACTLY ONE transition — an arbitrary body → the single compacted marker, ONCE, on a row older than the ratified clamp floor (⚙ `retention.compaction_horizon` `Min: 1` month, expressed as `OLD.ts > strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S','now','-1 month')` → ABORT). `run_events_no_delete` is UNTOUCHED; a row is never removed (event_seq is the sole ordering authority and `checkpoints.event_seq` FKs into the table). Both messages keep the phrase "run_events is append-only" so the standing `TestAppendOnlyTriggers` invariant still reads. **Recorded reading:** a SQL trigger cannot read the settings registry, so the trigger enforces the FLOOR of the clamp (structural, checkable) and the pass enforces the owner's own live per-user horizon on top (proven by test). Every refusal is tested — arbitrary payload rewrite, a marker-lookalike body, each identity column, delete, a strip inside the floor, and a second strip of an already-compacted row. The marker literal is byte-pinned to `retention.CompactedPayload` by a source scan of the migration.
- **The keep-forever set is DATA derived from the S14.2 FAMILY registry, total over all fifteen families.** `keepForeverFamilies` carries an explicit keep/strip decision AND its reason for every family, so an S00.9 family amendment fails the coverage test until someone decides — the default is never silently "strip". The seven S14.9 ¶2 classes map to `FamilyRunSummary` / `FamilyVerificationVerdict` / `FamilyHumanDecision` / `FamilyUsageLimits` (the receipt substrate) / `FamilyRouting` / `FamilyDriftCanary` / `FamilyBenchmarkEval`; the other eight are trace. Legacy aliases of a keep-forever family are allowlisted too (§29 R14) so a pre-rename row is not stripped for wearing its old name. B5-7's pre-marked `Retention: "keep-forever"` rows are a CROSS-CHECK asserted to agree; the family mapping is the authority. **Recorded reading:** S14.9's list is closed and `FamilyPlatform` is not on it, so `retention.compacted` is itself strippable past the horizon — its ROW always survives (deletes abort), so "the audit trail records its own compaction" holds as an existence record while a months-old pass's counts are elided.
- **OQ2 — the 11.3 exporter boundary is a VIEW, and it EDITS LANDED B4-3 CODE.** `run_events_export` (0015) left-joins the seeded `retention_keep_forever` allowlist and substitutes `ExportStrippedPayload` for every non-keep-forever body **at any age**; `internal/storage/backup.go` reads run_events through it and `StripPayloadBeforeSeq` is RETIRED from `DumpFrom` and `internal/backup`. A dump taken against a database without the view FAILS LOUD rather than falling back to the raw table (the F9 never-a-silent-drop precedent, inverted: never a silent leak). Every row still appears, so S02.9's gap-free `event_seq` restore check still passes. S13.10 asks for exactly this; S02.9's horizon phrasing is satisfied a fortiori; and a type boundary is the only one a PER-USER horizon can express as one view. The allowlist table is seeded idempotently at boot from the Go registry (`EnsureKeepForeverSeeded`, the §32 `SeedRows` precedent) and is **one-way** by no-delete trigger: a demotion in code preserves more data, never less.
- **The run summary is written at the run's terminal transition, in that transaction (S14.9 ¶1).** `run.Store` gained ONE additive seam — a nil-by-default `TerminalHook` invoked when `IsTerminal(to)` — because terminal transitions are driven from ten call sites across `stage`/`intake`/`adapters`/`recovery`/`watchdog`, and hanging the obligation on the one edge they all cross also covers a call site added later. A hook error ROLLS BACK the ending (a half-written ending is not a state the log can hold); the local tier is nowhere near that path, so a run never fails to end because the stack is down. The DETERMINISTIC AGGREGATE is the record — objective (tasks.title, with its source recorded so an absence reads as an absence), stages (the S02.3 lifecycle transitions at v0; `stage.started/finished` are B6's and are picked up forward-tolerantly), tool-call counts (the tolerant `tool`/`name`/`tool_name` key set §31 already reads, with unnamed calls counted rather than bucketed), verdicts, decisions, receipts (beside the checkpoint count, so an unmaterialized receipt is visible rather than rendered as zero cost) and final state — plus a sha256 **generation-inputs digest** folded over exactly the rows the aggregate saw. It lands in `run_summaries` (aggregate columns trigger-frozen, no-delete) because the summary must SURVIVE the compaction that strips the trace it was computed from; the EVENT carries the S14.2 contract minimum `{summary_ref, inputs_digest}` and nothing bulky.
- **The local tier ENRICHES, never gates.** `Narrator` is a func seam on duty alias `distill-summarize` (S12.4 "L1 distiller; run summary at run end"), composed at the shell with the S12.4 shaping that row mandates — grounding, extract-then-abstract, event-id citation forcing, a length cap and a section schema — as a DRAFTING duty (no `json_schema`, no logprobs, no abstain belt). It runs strictly AFTER the summary is committed, never inside the terminal transaction (a model call there would hold the single writer for an inference). Statuses are `pending`/`written`/`absent`/`failed`: an absent stack or a failure is RECORDED with its reason and the aggregate stands; nothing is ever faked. `internal/retention` cannot import `internal/local`, so it classifies an absent stack by `StackAbsentMarker`, which `internal/shell` pins to `local.ErrStackAbsent` at boot and in CI. Metering rides the `AdvisoryMeter` platform-run seam (§31 OQ4) because the summarized run is terminal and cannot take a D7 checkpoint. **A PLATFORM-scope run mints no summary** — platform machinery has no objective and no receipts, and summarizing the narrator's own advisory run would not terminate (asserted).
- **Incremental per-run only (S14.9 ¶1).** No batch or backfill summarizer exists: an AST scan asserts exactly ONE summary producer (`WriteAtRunEndTx`) and no `Backfill`/`Resummarize`/`SummarizeAll`/`SummarizeRange`/`SummarizeSince` verb, with a non-tautology probe. `EnrichPending` works over rows whose OWN run has already ended, one run at a time, never a window.
- **OQ5 — the compaction driver is the SHELL's, on structural-constant ticks.** `CompactionInterval` = 24h (daily-equivalent, run once at loop start so a restart-heavy host still compacts) and `IndexInterval` = 1 min; the enrichment batch is 4. None is a ⚙ row — S18 ratifies no cadence key and ⚙ `retention.compaction_horizon` is a HORIZON in months. Each carries its reason: the tick decides only how promptly a months-old payload is stripped / a new record becomes searchable, never WHAT is stripped or WHETHER it is indexed. Interim under the standing settings-tab directive. The pass holds no cursor at all — what to strip is a pure function of the horizon, the row's ts and its type — so it is idempotent and restart-safe by construction. **The ⚙ is read with `IntFor(key, userID)`, never `Int`**, live at evaluation time (a mid-life operator change lands on the next pass, proven), and two owners with different horizons demonstrably get two boundaries. The pass event is PLATFORM-scope, one row per pass, with per-owner legs `{user_id, horizon_months, boundary_ts, boundary_event_seq, events_stripped, bytes_reclaimed, by_family, transcripts_stripped}` inside. **A pass that stripped nothing logs nothing** — the audit trail records COMPACTION, and on a quiet household a daily no-op row would eventually be the bulk of the log the pass exists to bound.
- **Transcript copy-asides (S02.2).** Past the boundary, the `engine_sessions.transcript_copy_path` FILE is removed and the row's reference cleared; the ROW survives (it is the reboot-case mining index). An already-absent file is not an error; any other filesystem error is COUNTED and the reference is LEFT IN PLACE so the next pass retries rather than orphaning a file nobody can find again.
- **S14.10 ¶4 indexing (the B5-3 F16 discharge).** `run_events` carried eight columns and ONE index before this migration, so every `WHERE type = ?` derive in `internal/api/projection.go` and `internal/watchdog` was a full scan. 0015 adds six indexes, each naming the query shape it serves, and `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` asserts index use for all five previously-scanning shapes with a non-tautology probe (an unindexed column still reports a scan). Two VIRTUAL generated columns (`ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` admits only VIRTUAL, which is also the shape wanted — no rewrite, indexable): `change_class` (the one payload field an existing projection filters on) and `dotted_order`. **Recorded reading on `dotted_order`:** v0 payloads carry NO universal parent linkage — the S04.4 spawn record carries a DEPTH, not a parent event or run — so the path is `run_id . depth . zero-padded event_seq`, degrading to depth 0 where no linkage exists and to a `platform` root for platform-scope rows. That is a real absence rendered honestly, never a fabricated parent; a prefix scan on `<run_id>.` still reads the run's subtree in event order. Both generated columns go NULL on a compacted row, which is honest — the hot field genuinely no longer exists there.
- **FTS5 + rollups are DERIVED, never a system of record (S14.1).** One plain FTS5 table `history_fts` (the 0006 precedent) covers the three S14.10 ¶4 sources — run summaries (from the `run_summaries` row, not the ref-only event payload), verdict texts and drift summaries — maintained Go-side by a projector past a DURABLE cursor, with **rowid = the source event_seq** so every write is an idempotent replace and the whole index is rebuildable by replay. The cursor is durable rather than head-bootstrapped because a missed row here is a permanent hole in the search surface, not a stale signal the next evaluation re-derives. `run_event_rollup` is (day, owner, type) counts, deletable and reproducible. `Rebuild` proves the derived property; `ReconcileIndex` repairs the ONE state a restore leaves behind — the S13.10 dump never carries FTS content while the cursor table restores at its old position — and is called at boot, rebuilding only when the index is empty behind a non-zero cursor. `Search` returns REFS only and is owner-scoped per S01.9; **redact-before-match and excerpt bounding are B5-8B's** (§30 C2), over this index.
- **§29 flip mechanics.** `run.summary_written` and `retention.compacted` flipped declare→minted with real provenance + `producerTypes()` entries in the same packet, verdict `CONFORMS`, `SchemaVersion` 1, and representative goldens joining the required-field conformance set. Inventory **85/9 → 87 minted / 7 declare-only (94 registered)**; family 15 (Run summary) is now fully minted alongside 11 and 14. **Topic reading (OQ7, stated not changed):** `run.summary_written` is run-scoped so it carries the `run` tag ONLY — a summary landing on `board` would double-notify the run's own completion, which the accompanying `run.state_changed` already announces there; `retention.compacted` is platform-scope and carries NO tag, per §30's rule that platform-scope audit types flow only on the unfiltered relay. Both readings are tested in `internal/api`; the four-topic set and the map are untouched.
- **OQ8 — the B5-7 D5(b) declined-row freeze is TAKEN.** 0015 adds `benchmark_pairs_declined_frozen`, the sibling of 0014's `benchmark_pairs_recorded_frozen`: a decline is as final as a record — the record of record for it is the append-only event and the row is working state over that — so the working state stops moving at the same point. A `sampled` row still moves (asserted).
- **No new things.** NO new ⚙ key (tally **118/33**; `internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged; `retention.compaction_horizon` consumed by dotted key with a live-apply test), NO new dependency (`components.lock` **21**, `go.mod` unchanged, stdlib-only — FTS5 and generated columns are pinned-driver features, not adoptions; DuckDB is explicitly not v0), migration **0015** is the only new one and is immutable once committed (0001–0014 byte-untouched; the exact version pins moved 14→15 in `internal/conformance` and `internal/evals`, and the preview never-adds-a-migration guard moved to `0016`), and **$0 spent** — every local-tier leg is tier-F against `local.FakeServer` and a live seat is a bring-up act (SANCTIONED SKIP, §10). `Spec/`, `Research/`, `Docs/` and `P3/STATE.md` untouched.

### §36 drain round 1 (P3-B5-8A drain r1, D1–D11) — corrections and readings

Migration 0015 was EDITED in this drain under the coordinator's ruling that §6 immutability binds landed migrations applied to real databases; 0015 existed only in this unlanded packet and no production DB exists, so immutability re-attaches at landing. 0001–0014 stayed byte-untouched throughout. **F10 (RFC3339Nano sub-second ordering) was dropped by the coordinator with reason and is not addressed.**

- **The keep-forever boundary is enforced by the TRIGGER, not only by the pass (D1).** The narrowed `run_events_payload_compaction_only` WHEN consulted the marker, the age and the identity columns but never the allowlist, so ONE raw `UPDATE run_events SET payload = <marker>` elided verdicts, routing records and benchmark records straight past the pass — the proven finding. The WHEN now carries `OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM retention_keep_forever k WHERE k.type = OLD.type)`, and `retention_keep_forever` was hoisted to section A of the migration so it exists when the trigger is created. The consequence is the property S14.9 ¶3 actually claims: no writer at all — a correct pass, a future packet, an operator at a SQL prompt, a bulk UPDATE with no WHERE — can elide a keep-forever body. Tested single-row per class, in bulk with no WHERE, and through a legacy alias (`verify.round`), with the pass asserted to agree with the trigger.
- **Every committed strip is covered by a committed audit event, and owners are isolated (D2).** The pass committed elisions per batch but appended `retention.compacted` only after ALL owners succeeded, so a later owner's failure left committed strips with zero audit rows. The unit of work is now ONE transaction containing both the elisions and the `retention.compacted` row that counts them — there is no ordering of failures in which bodies are gone and nothing records it. Owners are isolated: a leg's failure is recorded on that leg (`Error`) and in `FailedOwners`, the pass continues, and `Compact` returns an error only for a pass-level failure. The event stays platform-scope with per-owner counts (OQ5 intact); it now carries ONE owner's unit rather than the whole pass. Tested as the probe scenario: owner `aaa` succeeds while owner `zzz`'s ⚙ read is fault-injected, and aaa's audit row exists with aaa's counts. **The coupling itself is guarded at drain r2 (R3)**: the happy path cannot tell an in-transaction append from a follow-up one, so `Config.AuditFault` (nil in production) fails the append INSIDE the unit tx and a test asserts the elisions rolled back with it — zero markers, zero audit rows, every body intact. A future edit that decouples them fails loudly instead of silently.
- **Only BULKY payloads are stripped (D3).** S14.9 ¶2 says "strips bulky event payloads" and the qualifier is load-bearing: the pass stripped everything non-keep-forever past the horizon, so 140 B of `auth.login`/`settings.changed`/`run.state_changed` payloads became 412 B of markers — content destroyed AND the database grown, with `BytesReclaimed` structurally unable to report the loss. `BulkPayloadFloorBytes` = **1024**, a structural constant (no ⚙) with a two-part reason: the marker is itself ~110 bytes so no threshold below its own length can ever reclaim, and `internal/adapters` caps an embedded excerpt at 4096 (ExcerptCap, refs-not-blobs) so a payload at or above 1 KiB is carrying CONTENT while anything below it is a structured audit record. Small audit payloads now survive past the horizon BY CONSTRUCTION, and the reclaim figure is honest by construction — a test asserts the floor exceeds the marker, that the evaluator's four small payloads survive while a bulky one strips, that `BytesReclaimed` equals the bytes the compacted rows actually shed, and that the log never grows on net across a pass.
- **The search corpus is redacted, not the reader (D4).** `history_fts` stored RAW text, so `Search` confirmed a planted `sk-ORACLE…` verbatim. This is not fixable at query time and is not B5-8B's to fix: a full-text index over raw text IS an oracle however the reader is written, because a MATCH on the plaintext confirms presence without returning it. Every string entering the index now passes through `internal/redact` FIRST — per individual string VALUE, never over serialized JSON (redact.go:94-97). **`internal/redact` joins the retention import wall as a DECLARED edge with its reason**; it is a stdlib-only leaf that B5-2 OQ2 built for exactly this reuse (§30 final bullet), so no transitive dependency and no cycle. `run_events.payload` is NEVER mutated — store-raw / serve-redacted holds (§30 R19) and the test asserts the raw rows still carry the secret. **Recorded reading:** `Search` stays the package primitive — it is the projector's own read over its own index, returning refs only; the Layer 0/1/2 surfaces around it are B5-8B's.
- **`dotted_order` is indexed (D5)** — `run_events_dotted_order_idx`. A generated column advertised for prefix scans but unindexed is a write cost with no read benefit; the EXPLAIN test now asserts the ordered-tree prefix scan seeks through it.
- **`change_class` is reachable from production (D6).** `internal/api/projection.go`'s drift query still filtered on the `json_extract` EXPRESSION, so every INSERT paid for an index nothing could choose. `QueryDriftCards` now reads the COLUMN (the same expression, materialized once and indexed), the load-bearing `COALESCE(change_class, '')` is preserved verbatim, and a test asserts both the query text and that the live projection returns cards over it. No dead index ships.
- **The F16 test pins VERBATIM production query texts (D7).** The first cut asserted paraphrased shapes — which cannot catch a production query edited into a scan — and its drift case dropped the real COALESCE and owner-scope branch. The projection query texts are now exported constants in `internal/api` (`QueryByType`, `QueryWatchdogFlags`, `QueryMaxSeqByKey`, `QueryDriftCards`, `QueryLatestTypeForRun`, plus the scope/order clauses) which production and the test share, and the EXPLAIN test lives in `internal/api` where those queries live, running each in BOTH its operator and its member form and naming the index that must serve it. `internal/retention` pins its own predicate the same way (`CompactableRowsQuery`). **Honest attribution:** the two per-run derives are served by **0001's `run_events_run_idx`**, not by anything 0015 added — that partial index already leads with `run_id` — and the test says so.
- **The run summary carries counts, never trace-derived free text (D8).** The summary is keep-forever, so it LEAVES the host in every snapshot while the trace it was computed from does not; tool NAMES and the unbounded transition `reason` were riding across that boundary. `ToolCalls` is now `{Total, Distinct, Unnamed}` — S14.9 ¶1 asks for tool-call COUNTS — and `Stage` carries `{Name, From}` as ENUMERATED S02.3 state values (an unrecognized value is dropped, not renamed), with the free-text `reason` deliberately not carried at all: it stays in the `run.state_changed` payload, which is trace and is stripped past the horizon exactly as S14.9's closed keep-forever list prescribes. Anything that does remain is redacted and bounded (`safeLabel` 96 runes, `safeTitle` 200, truncation marked). Tested with the evaluator's canaries: neither `TOOLNAME-CANARY` nor `REASON-CANARY` reaches the stored aggregate or the dump, and an injected non-FSM state value is dropped.
- **The boundary and the trigger floor share a clock domain (D9; CORRECTED at drain r2 — see R1).** The pass derived its boundary from the Go clock while the trigger floor used SQLite's `'now'`, so skew could fail a whole pass closed. `boundaryFor` now derives the boundary with SQLite `strftime` — the same function family, the same database. **The round-1 slack carried the WRONG SIGN** (`'-1 month','-1 minute'`), and the claim made here that it meant "the guard can never refuse a legitimate strip" was FALSE as shipped: the trigger permits a strip iff `OLD.ts <= floor` while the pass selects `ts <= boundary`, so the guarantee is exactly `boundary <= floor`, and a minus sign put the floor a minute BEFORE the boundary. At horizon = 1 a row inside that one-minute band was selected and then refused, and because each unit is one transaction the refusal rolled back every legitimate strip batched with it (measured: `FailedOwners=[edge]`, `EventsStripped=0`). The slack is now `'+1 minute'` — the floor at-or-later than any boundary the pass can compute — which is the direction the guarantee actually requires. With D2's per-owner isolation the residual blast radius of any such skew is one owner.
- **The pass has durable evidence it ran (D10, the ref3 ruling).** The pass is deliberately cursor-less and a no-op pass writes no event, so a quiet year was indistinguishable from a driver goroutine dead at boot — below the §32 dueness bar. `retention_pass_state` (the `retention_index_cursor` shape) is stamped on EVERY pass including a no-op and a failed one, carrying last-run, last/total counts and `last_error`; liveness is now a READ (`PassState`). No per-pass event was added — the audit trail still records COMPACTION only when compaction happened.
- **The terminal hook is install-once by construction (D11).** Its only protection was a doc comment. `Store.terminal` is an `atomic.Pointer[TerminalHook]` filled only by a nil→h compare-and-swap; `SetTerminalHook` returns an error on a second call or a nil hook, so a composition defect wiring two summary writers fails at boot instead of writing two summaries per run, and concurrent terminal transitions read it race-free (asserted under `-race`).

### §36 drain round 2 (P3-B5-8A drain r2, R1–R5) — the final round under the cap

- **R1/R2/R4 are one correction:** the D9 slack shipped with the wrong sign, the D9 bullet above is corrected in place to say so (a record correction of an error, not a dated-truth append), and the guard test that should have caught it is recalibrated. `TestHorizonOneIsNotRefusedByTheTriggerFloor` planted its row two minutes past the boundary — OUTSIDE the one-minute band — so it passed under the wrong sign, under no slack and under the right sign alike; it now plants ONE SECOND past the boundary, inside the band, and fails under the inverted sign with the coordinator's own measurement. A second guard, `TestTriggerFloorIsNeverEarlierThanAnyBoundary`, states the property as the ORDERING it is (`boundary <= floor` at every horizon the clamp admits, tightest at the minimum) and **extracts the floor expression from migration 0015 itself** rather than holding a copy — a test asserting the ordering of an expression that is not the one shipping is exactly how the regression survived.
- **R3 — atomicity is observed, not merely correct.** `Config.AuditFault` is a fault-injection seam over the compaction pass's audit append, nil in production and set by nothing outside a test. Its only purpose is to make the S14.9 ¶2 coupling visible: the evaluator moved the append into a follow-up transaction and the entire suite still passed, because the happy path cannot see the difference. The guard fails the append inside the unit tx and asserts zero markers, zero audit rows and every body intact, plus an unfaulted control so it fails for the right reason.
- **R5 — a known C2 bound, recorded.** `internal/redact` scrubs only its enumerated secret classes (PEM blocks, `sk-ant-…`, GitHub `gh?_`/`github_pat_`, generic `sk-…`, AWS `AKIA`/`ASIA`, `Bearer …`, the `sinet_session` cookie, uppercase `KEY=value` pairs), so a secret outside them — a short token, a lowercase-keyed pair — remains findable in the FTS corpus; this is inherent to the primitive, is identical to the live `/events` posture it already governs (§30), and is a bound on the backstop rather than a relaxation of the D2 prevention story (§7-C2·4).

## 37. Queryable-history conventions — Layers 0/1, search & routing quality (P3-B5-8B; S14.10, S12.3/S12.4/S12.5, S10.1/S10.10, S02.2, §30/§36)

`internal/history` is the S14.10 query surface: Layer 0's named deterministic views, the Layer-1 canned catalog with grammar-constrained intent, redact-before-match search, and the routing-quality view. It is a LEAF, AST-pinned with a forward allowlist and a non-tautology probe (`importwall_test.go`); the sanctioned allowlist is storage/eventlog/settings plus **two DECLARED edges** — `internal/local` (the duty-calling layers: intent classification and slot fill ride alias `intent-filling` through the existing `*local.Duty` seam, and the S12.5 margin/calibration types are that package's) and `internal/redact` (codor C2, below). `internal/settings` is permitted and **unused in fact**: this package declares and reads NO ⚙ key. The one edge it cannot own — the advisory run a duty call meters against — is a func seam composed at the shell root (`internal/shell/history_seams.go`). **This packet is the B half of the split B5-8B; Layer 2 (open SQL + the S12.3 guardrail stack + the injection battery, R29–R37) is NOT in it and lands as its own sitting at the ratified B|C seam.**

- **Layer 0 is SELECT, and the money prohibition is a checkable NEGATIVE.** Migration 0016 creates eleven views: `cost_per_run` / `cost_per_task` / `cost_per_project` / `cost_per_person` / `cost_per_period` / `cost_burn_rate` / `cost_budget_remainder` / `cost_done_directly` / `limit_event_history` (the nine S2.5 cost questions), plus `routing_quality` (S14.10 ¶5) and `task_project` (a linkage support view). The substrate is the `receipts` table, whose `usage_json` IS the marshaled `internal/metering` Receipt — **already priced, per row, at the row's own timestamp (S10.3)** — so every money figure is READ, never computed. `TestNoMoneyByGeneration` scans both the migration's SQL (comments stripped, so a citation in prose is not judged as a construct) and the package source for token fields, rate/price identifiers and multiplication inside a money expression, plus the `internal/metering` import, with a probe proving the scan can fail. Pricing stays in exactly one place.
- **Three HONEST ABSENCES, none of them a zero (S10.1: "no row silently prices to $0").** The v0 price table ships EMPTY, so a receipt whose calls all went unpriced renders `pricing_status = 'UNPRICED'` with a **NULL** amount (`PARTIAL` when some priced, `NO-RECEIPT` for a terminal run that materialized none, `NO-CALLS` for a run that made none). `cost_budget_remainder` reports `remainder_status = 'UNAVAILABLE'` with its reason — **no operator budget is persisted anywhere at v0** (`internal/metering` Budget is a caller-supplied value object and the 13.4 surface is a later phase), so the remainder has no minuend; the view still emits one row per person so the answer is about the right subject. `cost_done_directly` **CITES**: every column is lifted from the receipt's own `direct_use` block, the view selects `$.direct_use.formula_ref` rather than embedding it, and a test forbids the registration reference appearing in the migration's SQL at all (BENCH-REG §17 numbers are read-only).
- **RECORDED READING — there is no run→project column.** Neither `runs` nor `tasks` carries one and `deliverables.project_id` is written by nothing (both production call sites omit it). The only POPULATED relational edge is `artifact_claims.project`, written at intake from the resolved registry slice. `task_project` isolates that weakness in ONE place: `''` rows excluded, rows collapsed to one per task (a fan-out would double-count money), and `project_choices` carried so an ambiguous task renders the ambiguity. Work with no resolved project lands in an explicit `'(no project)'` bucket rather than being dropped, so the project altitude accounts for the same money the run altitude does (asserted).
- **RECORDED READING on "a periodic view" (S14.10 ¶5).** `routing_quality` is a VIEW, evaluated on read, so it is strictly fresher than any periodically-refreshed rollup and needs no scheduler job to stay true; the ¶4 rollup tables exist for the count surface where the aggregation is over the whole log, while routing decisions are bounded per run. It carries the `routing.decided` payload verbatim (cause, score, worker + version — the S08.4 version→outcome join key — model, lane, effort, degraded, plain_reason), joins the worker's `domain` through `worker_templates`, and joins the three OUTCOME families S14.10 ¶5 names: verdicts (`verdicts`/`verdicts_ship`/`verdicts_adverse`), rework (`verify_rounds` and the derived `reworked` — rounds beyond the first ARE the rework, left as a count so the caller chooses its denominator) and the receipt (through `cost_per_run`, so the pricing honesty is stated once and carried).
- **Legacy aliases are carried in SQL, and the literals are PINNED to the registry.** `limit_event_history` names `limit.event` **and** `engine.rate_limit`; `routing_quality` counts `verdict.recorded` **and** `verify.round` — a history that silently began at a rename is not a history (§29 R14). A view cannot read a Go registry, so the literals live in SQL and `TestLimitEventTypesArePinnedToTheRegistry` asserts each one classifies to the family the view claims (the §36 marker-pinning precedent): a retype fails loudly instead of silently emptying the view.
- **OQ3 — the Layer-1 catalog is DATA, in-band, with FOUR structural rules asserted over the whole table.** 49 named queries across the six categories (status, failures, verdicts, routing quality, drift history, cost — the last selecting Layer-0 views, so the money question is answered by a view and never recomputed), inside the ratified 30–50 band. Every entry: (1) **`?` placeholders only** — a `%`-verb anywhere fails the test, because a formatted query is the interpolation S14.10 ¶2 rules out; (2) **carries the owner-scope predicate** `AND (? = '' OR <col> = ?)` in the exact exported text production uses (`OwnerScopeOpen`/`OwnerScopeClose` — the §36 D7 lesson: assert the shipping text, not a paraphrase); (3) **ends `LIMIT ?`**; (4) **contains no statement separator**. Placeholder count is asserted equal to `len(slots)+3`, so bind order and SQL cannot drift apart. **The scope predicate is PARAMETERIZED rather than two query variants precisely so there is no unscoped form to reach for** — the operator binds the empty string and a member binds their identity, and `user_id` is CHECK'd non-empty in every table, so the empty string can only ever mean "operator". A hostile slot value is DATA: it matches nothing and destroys nothing (asserted with three injection shapes).
- **All eight S14.10 filter axes are reachable and owner-scoped, END TO END.** project, person, status, date range, worker, domain, lane, run/task id — each typed (`SlotType`), each reachable through at least one catalog query (asserted), and each exercised against a two-owner database where bob's rows are deliberately disjoint on every axis: the operator sees both, the owning member sees theirs, and the other member sees **zero**. A dropped predicate shows up as a leak rather than as a coincidence.
- **Layer 1's failure mode is FIXED by S12.4: a card, never a guess.** Intent classification rides alias `intent-filling` (fast tier) as TWO grammar-constrained calls, not one — choosing among 49 named queries and filling a chosen query's typed slots are different grammars, and a single schema would have to admit every slot of every query, which is the un-typed shape ¶2 rules out. Both schemas are built through `local.BatterySchema`, so the required free-text `reason` is **FIRST in property order** (the F5 finding — a Go map marshals alphabetically and would reorder the grammar) and an explicit abstain member is present. **Every** unresolved path produces the disambiguation card with real catalog choices and the reason it appeared: no local stack, no advisory run, a duty error, an abstain, an unknown query name, a below-threshold margin, an unfilled required slot. The S12.5 margin gate reads `local.LabelMargin` over the `query` label and the calibration for (`intent-filling`, seat model hash, engine build); **UNCALIBRATED is the honest posture** (the `internal/local` ReChecker precedent) — no gate, the answer stands, and the abstain member remains the floor that always applies. Tested tier-F with a `local.FakeServer` + a real `local.Duty`, including the control that makes the gate non-tautological: the SAME answer above and below the threshold, only the margin changed.
- **R26 is structural.** `newAnswer` is the ONLY constructor and it takes the layer and the query name together, deriving the confidence label from the layer — so an answer can neither be anonymous nor mislabel its own floor. Even the non-answer names something (`catalog.disambiguation`). Asserted across every verb.
- **Layer 2 is never reached by fall-through.** S14.10 ¶3's "canned queries remain the floor; Layer 2 is escalation, never default" is enforced here as the absence of a path: an unresolved Layer-1 question produces the card and stops. Asserted (`TestLayer2IsNotReachedBySilentFallthrough`). `LayerOpenSQL`/`ConfidenceLower` exist as the shape the C packet fills; nothing constructs one.
- **Codor C2 search — and the FINDING that redaction alone is not enough.** B5-8A bought the corpus property at index time (§36 D4). This layer redacts the QUERY before matching, and then **strips the redaction markers before tokenizing** — because `redact.Redact("sk-ant-…")` yields `[REDACTED:anthropic_key]` and the corpus is full of that same marker, so a merely-redacted query would match every record carrying any key of that class: a weak oracle, but an oracle, and one that reads to the asker as confirmation of the exact secret they typed. **This was proven by the test failing before the fix.** A secret therefore contributes no search term at all; a question that was nothing but a secret matches nothing and says so in the answer's notes. The MATCH expression is a **whitelist, not an escape** (letters and digits only, phrase-quoted, OR-joined, capped at 16 terms), which disposes of the whole FTS5 query-syntax surface — column filters, NEAR, prefix stars, booleans — so syntax in a question is matched, never executed. Excerpts come from `snippet()` over the already-redacted corpus, are bounded at `SearchExcerptRunes` = 240 with the truncation marked, and pass through the primitive again on the way out. Results order by `rank` then rowid. **Store-raw / serve-redacted holds**: nothing here writes, and the test asserts the raw `run_events.payload` still carries the planted secret verbatim.
- **RECORDED READING on the B5-8A seam.** `retention.Search` stays retention's OWN refs-only read over its own index; the S14.10 search LAYER reads the same corpus directly rather than through it, because `internal/history`'s wall admits storage/eventlog/settings/local/redact and `internal/retention` is not on that list. **The C2 property belongs to the CORPUS both read, not to either reader** — which is exactly what makes reading it twice safe. A KNOWN BOUND carries forward unchanged (§36 R5): `internal/redact` scrubs only its enumerated classes, so a secret outside them remains findable.
- **The deliverable-path negative-import assertion now names both sides of the line.** `internal/api`'s `TestRedactionObservabilityEdgeOnly` keeps its negative over review/preview/accept/intake and gains a POSITIVE limb: `internal/retention` (the write side) and `internal/history` (the read side) must each still import the primitive. Without that, the negative would pass for the wrong reason if the observability path stopped using redaction entirely.
- **OQ6 — no new ⚙, no new dependency, no new event type.** The number-shaped choices are STRUCTURAL CONSTANTS with their reasons: `DefaultRowLimit` 200 and `MaxRowLimit` 1000 (a read that materializes an unbounded result set is a liveness risk to the single-writer connection the control plane shares), `SearchExcerptRunes` 240 and the 24-token snippet window (an excerpt exists to let a person recognize a hit; the record is read from its ref), `searchMaxTerms` 16, and the two 400-token duty caps. Tally stays **118/33** (`internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged), `components.lock` **21**, `go.mod` unchanged, and the §29 inventory is untouched at **87 minted / 7 declare-only / 94 registered** — this packet ships a READ surface and appends nothing (AST-asserted: no `eventlog.Append`, and a scan asserting the package never writes at all). Migration **0016** is the only new one; **0001–0015 byte-untouched**, with the three exact version pins moved 15→16 (`internal/conformance`, `internal/evals`) and the preview never-adds-a-migration guard moved to `0017`. **$0 spent** — every duty leg is tier-F against `local.FakeServer`; a live seat is a bring-up act (SANCTIONED SKIP, §10), and the seat-not-servable case skips rather than dials.
- **Held, not routed.** `*history.Store` is composed at the shell root and HELD on `api.Config.History` for the S15 assistant, which "consumes these layers and nothing else" — wired but NOT routed, the same posture Accept/FollowUp/Preview sit in (§31/§35). **There is no loop**: a query surface's WHEN is "when a question is asked". The composed surface is proven usable with a nil duty and a nil calibration store, which is the case that matters — Layer 0 has no model in it by definition, and Layer 1's named verbs do not need one either.

### §37-C — Layer 2: open SQL, the S12.3 guardrail stack & the injection battery (P3-B5-8B sitting 2; S14.10 ¶3, S12.3/S12.4, S02.1, §29/§30/§32/§36/§37)

The C half at the ratified B|C seam. It adds `internal/history/{layer2,guard,lex}.go` and `internal/storage/readonly.go`, and changes nothing about Layers 0/1 beyond two honestly-narrowed source scans (below). **This packet's binding premise: the model is untrusted input and the guardrail is the boundary.** Nothing about the seat's output is assumed — not that it is SQL, not that it is one statement, not that it reads what it claims to.

- **The guardrail stack, and where each of the six limbs lives.** read-only connection → `internal/storage` `ReadOnly`; allowlisted views only → `guard.go` against the Layer-0 registry; single-statement parse → `guard.go`, on TOKENS, and it **extracts**; LIMIT injection → `guard.go`, as a wrap; timeout injection → `layer2.go`, as a context deadline; audit-logged → `layer2.go`, on **every** exit; lower-confidence → `history.go`'s `newAnswer`, which derives the flag from the layer so the unflagged form stays inexpressible.
- **MEASURED THIS SESSION, and it is why every limb exists rather than deferring to the handle** (probe against the pinned `modernc.org/sqlite`, 2026-07-27): (1) **multi-statement text EXECUTES** — `SELECT 1 AS one; SELECT 2 AS two` runs both and returns the SECOND statement's rows with no error, so a benign prefix is a real smuggling vector and only the parse stops it; (2) **`ATTACH` SUCCEEDS on the read-only handle and creates a file on disk** — SQLite does not refuse it, the parser does or nothing does; (3) **`PRAGMA query_only = 0` succeeds silently**, clearing the spec's *named* limb — which is precisely why `mode=ro` ships beside it and is the limb that actually holds the refusal; (4) **a runaway recursive query is NOT bounded by a limit clause** (the aggregate never yields a row to count), so LIMIT and timeout are independent limbs and neither substitutes for the other. `REINDEX` likewise returns cleanly. All four are asserted in `internal/storage/readonly_test.go` and `injection_test.go`.
- **R29 — the read-only handle is a SECOND handle, and it PROVES itself at every open.** The writing handle keeps `SetMaxOpenConns(1)`, so a `query_only` pragma on it would disable the control plane's own writes; Layer 2 therefore gets its own `*sql.DB` opened `mode=ro` + `query_only(1)`, also at one connection **because `query_only` is per-connection state and a pool whose members could drift is a pool where the property holds on some connections and not others**. `VerifyReadOnly` reads the pragma back AND attempts a real write, requiring it to fail *for the readonly reason* — the S02.1 `PRAGMA integrity_check`-at-every-open posture. The type exposes `QueryContext` and nothing else: there is no `WriteTx`, no `ExecContext`, no Tx verb, so the unwritable form is the only form a caller can express. Opened at the shell root (after the writing handle has made the file and its WAL sidecars) and **non-fatal if it fails** — Layer 2 is the escalation, Layers 0/1 are the floor, so the platform comes up with an honest absence.
- **R31 — extraction IS the design, and it is the adverse measurement's own conclusion.** The deployed seat scored **0/30, Wilson [0.00, 0.11]**, worse than the general-purpose proxies, because Arctic-Text2SQL-**R1** emits chain-of-thought before the statement (`P3/measurements/2026-07-22-sql-open-arctic.md`). **`NoThink` is not the fix** — the reasoning is the model's method and suppressing it degrades the seat that was chosen for it; `openSQLMaxTokens` is 2048 for the same reason (a cap sized for a terse answer truncates the statement before it arrives — a 600-char contract is exactly what scored 0/30). Order: fenced block first (the model's own "this is the answer"), else the last statement START.
- **"START" is load-bearing, and finding that out was a real correction.** Taking the last `select` outright SILENTLY TRUNCATES: `… UNION SELECT …` loses its first half and `WITH x AS (…) SELECT …` loses its whole with-clause, each answering a **different question than the model asked**. So a candidate preceded by a continuation word or by `(`/`,`/`)` is not a start. Asserted by `TestExtractionNeverTruncatesACompoundStatement`.
- **Extraction opens a gap, and the gap is closed rather than lived with.** Locating a statement inside surrounding text means a PREFIX gets dropped — so `DROP TABLE runs; SELECT 1` and `INSERT INTO t SELECT …` would be quietly DEFUSED into harmless reads. Defusing is safe but silent, **and a guardrail that handles an attack without recording one has not detected it.** `checkStatementPrefix` therefore refuses a statement whose OWN LINE carries a banned word or a statement separator — the line being where a SQL-contiguous prefix lives, while reasoning prose sits on earlier lines and is not judged. A false refusal is possible and is the SAFE direction: loud, audited, with the floor still answering. The separator limb also catches `SELECT a; SELECT b` where both halves read and `Guard`'s own single-statement check (which sees only what extraction returned) would not.
