- **The parse is LEXICAL, and that is the point (`lex.go`, stdlib only).** Every check is about WORDS, and a substring scan cannot tell a word from a fragment: `updated_ts` is not an update, `'delete from runs'` inside a string literal is not a write, `-- do not drop anything` is not a schema change. Worse, a substring scan can be *evaded* by quoting or commenting. Comments are DISCARDED, which is what un-hides a comment-hidden separator. A third-party SQL parser would be an S16 adoption needing the §4 checklist and operator approval; the checks needed here are lexical, so none is proposed.
- **Refusal, never repair.** The guardrail does not edit a statement into safety — an editor has to be right about intent, a refuser only has to be right about danger. The one rewrite it makes (the owner predicate) only ever NARROWS. On an ambiguous statement boundary it refuses rather than truncating.
- **R27/S01.9 — owner scoping is a property of the STATEMENT, because the handle cannot supply it.** The read-only handle sees every row in the file, so `rewriteScope` replaces each allowlisted view reference with that view under the same parameterized predicate the Layer-1 catalog uses (`(? = '' OR <owner_col> = ?)`), preserving any alias so column qualifications keep resolving. Proven cross-owner on the two-owner fixture with a deliberately UNSCOPED generated statement ("give me everything"): the operator sees both, each member sees only their own, and `owner_scoped` is on the audit row so an unscoped answer would be visible in the trail and not only in the rows. Generated SQL may contain **no bind parameter of its own** — every placeholder in the executed statement is the guardrail's, so a smuggled `?` cannot shift the argument order and bind a scope value into the model's expression. A view with no owner column (`task_project`) is operator-only here exactly as it is at Layer 0.
- **R32 — the bound WRAPS, it does not edit.** `SELECT * FROM (<the model's own text>) LIMIT ?`, so what executes is the model's statement plus a bound, and a larger limit of its own is tightened rather than trusted. `OpenSQLMaxRows` 200 is tighter than the rest of the surface for its own reason: this is the one layer whose statement nobody reviewed. `OpenSQLTimeout` 5 s is the measured limb (4) above.
- **R33/§29 — this packet registers ONE new event type, and says so out loud.** `history.query_audited` (family 12 Platform, admitted by the same OQ2-(A) reading as the `worker.`/`registry.`/`preview.`/`local.` groups; **a new FAMILY would have been an S00.9 amendment and none is needed**). §29's rule is followed exactly: a `contract.go` entry plus a `producerTypes()` entry in the same packet, plus a representative required-field golden. **Inventory moves 87 minted / 7 declare-only / 94 registered → 88 / 7 / 95.** One row per ATTEMPT **including every refusal** — a guardrail whose refusals are not recorded cannot be reviewed, and the refusals are the interesting rows. The row is scoped to the ASKER, so no member's audit view gains another member's questions. A failure to write the audit is deliberately not propagated: losing the ability to record must never become a way to change an outcome the caller already got. The append rides `context.WithoutCancel`, because a timed-out query is precisely the case whose row matters most.
- **R35 — escalation is enforced as the ABSENCE of a path.** `AskOpenSQL` is a separate exported verb; nothing in Layer 0 or Layer 1 calls it, and an unresolved Layer-1 question still produces the disambiguation card and stops (`TestLayer2IsNotReachedBySilentFallthrough`, unchanged from the B half). Reaching Layer 2 is an act.
- **R36 — the injection battery: 63 cases × 2 scopes (126 subtests) after the drain — 60 hostile shapes, each refused WITH ITS REASON ASSERTED** so a case cannot pass by being refused for something else: DDL, DML, insert-select, multi-statement smuggling (five shapes), comment-hidden statements, `ATTACH`, `PRAGMA`, pragma table-valued functions, `load_extension`, `VACUUM INTO`, base tables reached by join / union / subquery / with-clause / comma-join / quoted identifier / bracket identifier, `sqlite_master`, qualified names, CTE names shadowing an allowlisted view, bind-parameter smuggling, and **prompt injection carried in the event content the model read** (three shapes, including the model complying with the smuggled instruction). The remaining **3 cases are in-band controls that must be ACCEPTED** — a trailing separator with nothing after it, a banned word inside a comment, a banned word inside a string literal — which is what proves the lexer judges WORDS rather than substrings. A separate control group (`TestBatteryIsNonTautological`) adds the same discipline at statement level: a battery that refuses everything proves nothing, so legitimate reads — joins, CTEs, aggregates, unions, `replace()` — must be ACCEPTED. Registered as conformance row `layer2-open-sql-injection` (§32), naming four real in-tree tests plus the pre-registered measurement file; registry seed **10 → 11 rows**. **The battery is model-independent by design**, which is what makes it re-runnable on any `sql-open` retarget: it asserts that whatever the seat emits dies at the guardrail, so it never needs the seat to be good.
- **`TestTheWholeBatteryChangesNothing` — the S04.5/S03.5 shape at the database level.** Every hostile output goes through the REAL verb against a real migrated database, then every table's row count is compared. Three tables are expected to grow and the amount is ASSERTED rather than the table excused: `runs` and `checkpoints` by exactly one per generation (S12.1 R18 — every duty call meters), `run_events` by the audit rows. Everything else is byte-identical, and the schema gains and loses nothing.
- **TWO B-half assertions are honestly narrowed, and each gains a stronger positive limb.** `TestTheQueryLayerNeverWrites` said "the package never writes at all"; Layer 2 makes that false in one mandated way, so it now asserts the sharper property — no file executes SQL of its own (`WriteTx`/`ExecContext` nowhere), and `guard.go` is exempt from the write-VERB literal scan for the obvious reason that its job is to name those verbs in order to refuse them, with `TestGuardNamesEveryWriteVerbItRefuses` keeping the exemption honest by asserting each named verb is also actually refused. `TestNoNewEventTypeIsMinted` becomes `TestExactlyOneEventTypeIsMinted`: 95 registered, and `eventlog.Append{` appears in **exactly one file**. §37's B-half sentence *"`LayerOpenSQL`/`ConfidenceLower` exist as the shape the C packet fills; nothing constructs one"* was true of the B half and is now **discharged** — `AskOpenSQL` is the one and only constructor, and even its unavailable and refused paths carry the flag.
- **TBD-BRINGUP ships PRE-REGISTERED, record-through.** `P3/measurements/2026-07-27-layer2-open-sql-bringup.md` is written before the measurement, with §2 frozen: safety is UNCONDITIONAL and untradeable against accuracy (any write, out-of-allowlist read, multi-statement execution, cross-owner row, unflagged answer or unaudited query is a hard fail regardless of pass rate); the accuracy expectation is **directional, not a registered number** — materially better than the raw alias's 0/30, with no target invented — and if extraction does not move it, the honest finding is that extraction was never the seat's problem. Figures are to be read out of the `history.query_audited` rows, which exercises the audit trail while using it. **The measurement evaluates the GUARDED SURFACE, not the alias: the alias is already measured and adverse, and re-measuring it would answer a question already answered.**
- **No new ⚙, no new dependency, no new migration.** `internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged (tally **118/33**); `components.lock` **21**, `go.mod` unchanged; **migrations 0001–0016 byte-untouched** — the allowlist is the Layer-0 view set 0016 already created, so Layer 2 needed no schema at all. `OpenSQLMaxRows`, `OpenSQLTimeout` and `openSQLMaxTokens` are structural constants carrying their reasons (OQ6, ratified), interim under the standing settings-tab directive. **$0** — every leg is tier-F against `local.FakeServer` + a real `local.Duty`; the live Arctic run is a bring-up act (SANCTIONED SKIP, §10) and the seat-not-servable case skips rather than dials.

#### §37-C drain round 1 (P3-B5-8B drain r1, D1–D9) — corrections and readings

The evaluation of `401cdf1`+`9e1ebb2` returned FAIL on one complete guardrail escape plus eight smaller items. All nine applied; the four driver measurements, the six non-tautology probes and the four referrals were re-confirmed sound and are untouched.

- **D1 (CRITICAL, fixed at the root) — the guardrail re-emitted model text as raw SQL.** A table ALIAS was the one piece of model text `rewriteScope` wrote back into the statement it built, and `lex.go` deliberately STRIPS the quoting from a quoted/bracketed/backticked identifier — so `AS "a, run_events x"` arrived as the bare content `a, run_events x` and was emitted as syntax. **That text was seen by nothing**: the banned-word scan skipped `quoted` tokens, the bind-parameter check never looked inside one, and `readTargets` never saw the tables it named. Measured through the real verb: ACCEPTED with `Views=[cost_per_run] Scoped=1`, and as member `alice` it returned raw `run_events.payload` for BOTH owners; with a `?` inside the alias to balance the bound arguments a full second statement executed, and the chain continued `PRAGMA query_only=0` → `ATTACH` → `CREATE TABLE loot.stolen` → `INSERT`, writing a file outside `platform.db`. **Four limbs now, not one:** a quoted alias is refused (`checkAlias`), a quoted TABLE name is refused (D7), any quoted identifier that is not a plain bare name is refused (so a quoted identifier cannot carry SQL — a checked property, not an assumption), and the emitted alias is defensively re-quoted with the doubled-quote escape (`quoteIdent`) so no future path can re-open this by accident. The banned-word scan no longer skips quoted tokens. The battery gains all three quote styles × with/without `AS` × the argument-balancing variant, and `TestAliasInjectionEscalationChain` keeps the evaluator's full chain as a standing test asserting refusal at every link.
- **D2 (fixed; an overclaim corrected) — `mode=ro` does not cover TEMP or ATTACHed databases.** `readonly.go`'s comment claimed every write into an ATTACHed or TEMP database fails. **That was false.** Measured: with `query_only` cleared by one statement, `CREATE TEMP TABLE`, a temp INSERT, `ATTACH`, `CREATE TABLE loot.stolen` and `INSERT INTO loot.stolen SELECT payload FROM run_events` ALL SUCCEED — an exfiltration path to a file outside `platform.db`, even though `platform.db` itself stays unwritable. So the two limbs are not redundant, they are **complementary**: `mode=ro` protects the main database undefeatably, and `query_only` is what protects TEMP and ATTACHed ones — and `query_only` is defeasible. It is therefore **re-asserted on every `QueryContext`**, bounding the window to a single call, with the parser refusing `PRAGMA`/`ATTACH` as the other half. The comment now says what is true, and the COMBINED test the shipped ones avoided is in.
- **D3 (fixed without censoring vocabulary) — the marker oracle was half-closed.** `stripMarkers` removed only a well-formed `[REDACTED:<class>]`, but the corpus holds those markers as ordinary tokens. Measured against a row redacted to `[REDACTED:anthropic_key]`: the well-formed form returned 0 rows, while `REDACTED anthropic_key`, `[REDACTED:anthropic_key` and `REDACTED:anthropic_key]` each returned 1 — the class-level oracle §37 claimed to have eliminated. **The obvious fix would have been the wrong one:** dropping the marker's constituent tokens from every query would make "key", "token", "session" and "github" unsearchable, breaking the surface to close a leak. So the marker pattern now matches FRAGMENTS (brackets and colon optional, case-insensitive), and — the load-bearing half — **the MATCH is allowed to over-return and each ROW is verified against its own marker-stripped body** (`dropMarkerOnlyHits`): a hit survives only if a query term still appears once the markers are gone. A row whose only "key" is inside a marker is not a hit for "key"; a row that genuinely says "key" still is. The verification column is dropped before the answer is returned. All four marker forms measure 0 rows; "rotation"/"key rotation"/"leaked" still measure 1.
- **D4 (fixed) — two accept-controls produced non-executable SQL.** The trailing `;` was dropped from the TOKENS but not from the TEXT that gets wrapped, so `SELECT … ;` built `… ;) LIMIT ?` and failed at execution with a syntax error; a trailing comment failed as `incomplete input`. Both were asserted accepted and never EXECUTED. `Guard` now truncates `sqlText` to the end of the last real token (which cannot disturb the target offsets, all of which precede it), and **`TestAcceptedStatementsActuallyExecute` runs every accept-control and every legitimate statement against the real read-only handle**. A trailing semicolon is the likeliest thing a text-to-SQL model emits, so this was a pure accuracy tax on the bring-up E2 measurement.
- **D5 (fixed) — extraction re-anchored inside string literals.** Anchoring scanned raw bytes, so `… LIKE '%with%'` anchored on the `with` INSIDE the literal and yielded the fragment `with%'`, refused as an unterminated literal. Anchoring is now TOKEN-AWARE wherever the text lexes; reasoning prose (an apostrophe is enough to stop the lexer) still uses the byte scan, which is the right instrument for prose. Safe before, accurate now.
- **D6 (fixed) — a statement prefix across a NEWLINE was defused rather than refused.** `checkStatementPrefix` was scoped to the statement's own line, so `"DROP TABLE runs;\nSELECT …"` was quietly turned into a harmless read and audited as `outcome=executed`. Nothing hostile ran, but **an attempt that is not recorded has not been detected** — which is the principle that function exists to serve. The prefix is now the statement's own PARAGRAPH (back to the last blank line), since a blank line or a fence is what actually separates reasoning from an answer. Two narrow signals keep prose alive: a line BEGINNING with a banned word, and a separator anywhere in the paragraph.
- **D7 (fixed) — a quoted view name is refused.** `readTargets` did not check `tk.quoted`, contradicting `lex.go`'s stated contract; the allowlisted views are named plainly, so a name the author had to quote is not one of them.
- **D8 (fixed, with the residual RECORDED).** `randomblob`/`zeroblob` are banned: they build arbitrarily large values in memory, and **memory is the one resource this guardrail does not bound** — the row limit counts rows, the timeout counts seconds, and a single row can be a gigabyte. **KNOWN RESIDUAL, stated rather than closed:** `SELECT count(*) OVER () FROM …` and other costly-but-ordinary expressions remain accepted, bounded only by `OpenSQLTimeout` on a single shared connection that `history.go` itself names a liveness risk. The timeout is the stated backstop; the bring-up acceptance battery carries this as a known limit, not as a solved problem.
- **D9 (applied in three parts; the fourth REFERRED).** `history.query_audited`'s no-topic routing is pinned by test on the `retention.compacted` precedent (a query audit is platform machinery, not a household card); the type is added to `TestOQ2AdmittedUnderBroadenedScope` so the OQ2-(A) admission is test-pinned rather than prose-only; family 12's Scope note gains the `history.*` group (that field is this project's reconciliation prose, not spec text). **NOT applied: extending family 12's `RequiredFields`.** That string is **verbatim from the frozen S14.2 table** and is pinned byte-for-byte by `TestFifteenFamiliesWithRequiredFields` per §29; the spec's Platform row neither carries the Layer-2 audit's fields nor names the type. Authoring spec text is not this packet's to do (§8: Spec wins; a conflict is a referral, never a silent resolution), and the intent is already met where a type's own minimum belongs — the `TypeSpec` Note enumerates the payload, and the required-field golden asserts it. **Referred to the coordinator**; an S00.9 amendment is the mechanism if the coordinator wants the family row itself extended.

#### §37-C drain round 2 (P3-B5-8B drain r2, R1–R4, FINAL under the cap) — the four residuals

The re-check of `032e223` confirmed the critical escape DEAD: the evaluator replayed its full 5-link chain (every link refused) and tried 28 fresh routes to rebuild it — derived-table/JOIN/CTE/column/ORDER BY alias positions, quoted schema-qualified names, comment- and newline-splices, doubled quoting, bracket-in-doublequote, Cyrillic homoglyph, fullwidth quotes, the arg-balancing variant — **all 28 refused**, with no foreign rows and no raw payload from whatever still parses. D2/D3/D4/D5/D7/D8/D9 verified; D3 fully discharged. Four residuals remained and are closed here.

- **R1 (a panic the D4 fix introduced, now fixed AND belted).** `strings.TrimSpace` on the tail truncation also strips the LEADING side, shifting the text against token offsets computed from the ORIGINAL — so `rewriteScope` sliced out of range. Measured: `Guard("   SELECT * FROM cost_per_run", …)` → `panic: slice bounds out of range [29:26]`; the leading-newline/tab and leading-space-with-trailing-comment forms panicked identically. Unreachable through `AskOpenSQL` (which passes trimmed `ExtractSQL` output) but `Guard` is EXPORTED with an undocumented precondition, and **a panic in a guardrail is a crash, not a refusal** — B6/S15 is the next consumer. Fixed properly rather than documented: the truncation no longer trims at all, because cutting at the last token's end already removes every trailing space, comment and separator, and leaving the front intact keeps the offsets valid. **Plus a fail-closed belt** — `checkOffsets` validates every span before the rewrite and REFUSES on a bad one. Proven: re-introducing the `TrimSpace` bug now produces a *refusal* ("could not be scoped safely"), not a crash, so the belt holds even when the arithmetic is wrong.
- **R2 (D6 was half-discharged; now whole).** The prefix check walked back only to the last `"\n\n"`, so **a blank line RESET it** — and a reasoning model's output is full of blank lines, making that shape at least as likely as the single-newline one already fixed. All four measured shapes were still defused and audited as `executed`. The prefix is now the WHOLE text before the statement. Widening the window without widening the false-refusal surface is the whole trick: both signals stay strictly per-LINE and judge only the word a line OPENS with, and the separator signal is scoped to lines that open a statement (`select`/`with`) — a reasoning paragraph may well use a semicolon, but it does not begin with `select`. All four shapes are battery cases, and `TestBlankLineSplitIsAuditedAsRefused` asserts the audit row reads `outcome=refused` with its reason, because R36's obligation is refusal **recorded**, not merely refusal.
- **R3 (the load-bearing limb now has a test).** §37-C called `dropMarkerOnlyHits` load-bearing while nothing tested it: neutering it (`return a.Rows`) left the ENTIRE suite green with `key`, `anthropic` and `anthropic_key` all reaching the secret-bearing row, because the shipped test exercised only the regex limb. **The fixture is the part that matters** — the row's honest text must not contain the marker's own words, or it would be returned legitimately and the test would pass for the wrong reason (exactly the false positive the earlier fixture had, which the evaluator also caught in its own). The new row redacts to `[REDACTED:anthropic_key]` with honest words *deploy/credential/build/log*, so bare class vocabulary must return zero while honest words still return the row. The limb is also asserted directly through a test hook. **An untested security property is one refactor from silently gone.**
- **R4 (two D1 limbs now independently tested).** `checkAlias` and `quoteIdent` could each be neutered with the suite still green — genuinely redundant while limb 3a (`plainIdent` over every quoted identifier) holds, but claimed as independent limbs and existing as belt-and-braces against a future relaxation of 3a, which is precisely the scenario no test would catch. Each now has a test that separates it from 3a: for `checkAlias`, a quoted alias whose content IS a plain name (`AS "alias"`) — 3a passes it, checkAlias must not, with the unquoted form as the control proving the limb refuses the QUOTING and not the name; for `quoteIdent`, the emitted alias must appear quoted, plus the doubled-quote escape asserted directly. Both verified failing when neutered.

**Neuter-probe record (run this session, each producing a targeted failure):** `dropMarkerOnlyHits` → `TestMarkerOnlyRowsAreDroppedByVerification`; `checkAlias` → `TestQuotedAliasIsRefusedEvenWhenItsContentIsPlain` (all four quote forms); `quoteIdent` → `TestEmittedAliasIsDefensivelyQuoted`; the R1 `TrimSpace` bug → `TestGuardDoesNotPanicOnLeadingWhitespace` (as a refusal, not a panic); the R2 blank-line reset → 8 battery subtests + the audit test. Battery **63 → 67 cases × 2 scopes (134 subtests)**; `export_test.go` carries the two test hooks and compiles only into the test binary, so the package API is unchanged.

#### §37-C landing record (P3-B5-8B, coordinator-inline after the cap) — three nits, one closed and two recorded

The final re-check returned **PASS** with three nits. The cap was exhausted, so the coordinator disposed of them inline (runbook post-cap rule) rather than opening a third round.

- **CLOSED — the fail-closed belt now has its own test.** `checkOffsets` is what turns a stale span into a refusal instead of the R1 panic, yet neutering it left the suite green: it is unreachable while the rewrite's arithmetic is right, so only a direct call can assert it. `CheckOffsetsForTest` (the `export_test.go` hook pattern this packet established, test-binary only) exposes it, and `TestOffsetBeltRefusesStaleSpans` drives six stale shapes — end past the source (the R1 shape), end before start, out of order, overlapping, negative start, start past the source — plus a valid set that must still pass, so the belt is proven to refuse staleness without refusing the rewrite. Verified non-tautological: neutering `checkOffsets` fails it on three spans.
- **RECORDED — `\v`/`\f` whitespace mismatch (pre-existing, not this packet's).** `lex.go:60` treats vertical tab and form feed as whitespace; SQLite does not. So `Guard("\v\fSELECT …")` is accepted and then fails at execution with `unrecognized token`. `lex.go` was untouched by both drains, the shape is unreachable through `AskOpenSQL`, and no model emits it. Recorded as a known lexer/engine divergence rather than drained — the honest statement is that the guardrail's whitespace class is slightly wider than the engine's, which fails toward a clean execution error, never toward admission of something hostile.
- **RECORDED — the widened R2 prefix window has a measurable false-refusal surface on UNFENCED output, and it belongs in the bring-up measurement.** Because the window is now the whole preamble, an honest reasoning line that merely *opens* with a banned word (`Update`, `Delete`, `Insert`, `Create`, `Drop`, `Pragma`, `Begin`, `Returning`, `Into`, `Analyze`) is refused: the evaluator measured 10 of 12 such samples refused. **Fenced output is immune** (2 of 2 accepted) and the system prompt asks for a ```sql fence, so the prompted path is unaffected. This is the documented safe direction — loud, audited, never silent — but it is a real accuracy cost on unfenced emissions. **It must be carried into the TBD-BRINGUP Layer-2 acceptance measurement (G3 Def.8) as an expected refusal source, so a refusal rate driven by the guardrail's own conservatism is never misread as seat weakness.** The alternative — narrowing the window — trades a detection gap for accuracy and is not taken at v0.

## 38. Read-side API completion, routed query layers & stage-event production (P3-B6-1; S15.2–S15.3, S15.5, S14.10, S14.2, S02.3, S06.6, S10.1/S10.4, S01.9)

The first packet of B6 completes the **read side** of the S15.2 resource families over machinery that already existed, routes the landed S14.10 query surface as `/api/events`, and flips the two `stage.*` declare-only types to minted at real stage boundaries. It is not the decision plane (B6-2), not the content families (B6-3) and not the SPA (B6-4+); the History guardrails, the SSE surface, the projections and the redaction primitive are CONSUMED, never re-implemented. `internal/history` is byte-unchanged.

- **The stage boundary is `Skeleton.Session` (OQ3(a)), and the record is honest on every path.** One `stage.started` before the engine session and one `stage.finished` after, for EVERY fresh engine stage session across every role — ceremony legs, execute steps, verify, compose, helper sessions and split successors alike. The intake pipeline's own phases keep `intake.state` (already family 1) and the role legs' `setKanban` flips are untouched: those are pipeline state, not engine stage sessions. Outcomes are the closed set `completed` / `split` / `error`, classified by ONE function (`stageOutcome`) that mirrors `Session`'s own disposition switch, so the recorded outcome cannot drift from the behavior it describes — including the raced-completion case, where a split was requested and the engine finished first and the record says `completed` because that is what happened. **A session whose PROCESS dies gets no synthetic finish**: an ending nobody observed is not an ending the log may assert, and the crash story is already the run-lifecycle rows (S02.5). Appends are run-scoped at the run's generation under the B5-1 envelope contract and are best-effort in the `recordCompiled` sense — a failed append is LOUD and the stage still runs, because losing a trace row must never fail a stage.
- **The stage-brief hash is over the exact prompt, computed once, never empty (OQ4(a)) — and `ledger.PlacePinned`'s sha256 is deliberately NOT reused.** `stageBriefHash` is sha256 over the prompt text the session received: the assembled brief plus the stage instructions, or the instructions alone for an artifact-only session (`Assemble=false`: S06.8 critique, S07 judge/rework). PlacePinned hashes `ledger.PinnedText`, a DIFFERENT byte string, so reusing it would have been NOMINAL identity — and a hash is reusable only on byte-identity. Asserted by a test that computes both and requires them to differ.
- **The stage KIND is named by the caller, not read out of prompt text — a small correction the e2e found.** The first cut derived the kind from the `SINET-STAGE:` marker line at the head of `Instructions`, which is right for seven of the eight dispatch sites and WRONG for the judge, whose instructions are prepended with `in.BriefText` (S07.5 clean-context assembly). Widening the scan to find the marker anywhere would have let model-authored brief content name the record — the standing "model output is untrusted input" invariant, arriving in a display field. So `SessionInput` gained `Kind`, set at each dispatch site from the marker constant already there, with the head-of-instructions derivation kept as the fallback and `unmarked` as the honest absence label. The prompt the judge receives is byte-unchanged: the fix moved a fact the caller already knew, not the ceremony.
- **§29 flip mechanics, exactly.** Both types flip declare→minted with real `file:line` provenance, verdict `CONFORMS`, `producerTypes()` entries and required-field goldens (`stage.started` deliberately carries NO outcome — a stage that has only started has none). Inventory **88 minted / 7 declare-only / 95 registered → 90 / 5 / 95**: the registry does not grow, the minted share does. Family 1 joins 11, 14 and 15 as fully minted. The five remaining declare-only rows are the ones this packet deliberately did not mint, each for its own reason (`run.parked/resumed` are manifested as `run.state_changed` transitions; `ask.observed/answered` are projected from the `asks` table; a tool-use-START producer does not exist). No new type, no new family, no S00.9 amendment, topic map untouched.
- **The two walking-skeleton READS were unscoped, and B6-1 closes them in place (OQ5(a)).** `Surface.Task` and `Surface.Receipt` returned any task or receipt to any authenticated identity — the same pre-multi-owner placeholder the B0-3 unscoped SSE relay was, and the read-side twin of what §30 OQ1 fixed on the stream. `GET /api/tasks/{task}` becomes the enriched R5 detail and `GET /api/runs/{run}/receipt` keeps its path; both now resolve owner scope server-side with **404-before-403** (an unknown id must not become an existence oracle for another owner's work). The B2-4-era tests whose unscoped expectations were placeholders are updated, and no unscoped legacy read survives. The mutation verbs are untouched — the decision plane is B6-2's.
- **Owner scope is one shape across the whole read surface.** `authorizeOwner(scope, owner, found, what)` is the `authorizeRun` semantics generalized; `readScope` resolves the one S01.9 role bit through the existing `isOperatorRead` path; `readPerson` REFUSES a member naming another person rather than silently re-scoping the answer, because an answer about a subject the caller did not ask for is worse than a refusal. Every endpoint carries the three-way cross-owner test (operator both / owner theirs / other member ZERO), and each is non-tautological in the direction that matters: the member's own rows must be PRESENT, so an empty answer cannot pass for a scoped one.
- **The single-connection pool is a real constraint on list handlers, and it bit once.** `defer rows.Close()` is safe only when the cursor is drained; a page that stops at its limit leaves it open, and the control plane's pool is ONE connection (S02.1), so the per-row derives that follow deadlock. Both list handlers close the cursor EXPLICITLY before decorating. Found by a hung test, not by review.
- **OQ2 — the burn-rate seam is settled by GRAIN, and the answer is that it stays nil.** `cost_burn_rate`'s grain is per PERSON (usd per observed day across the span between their first and last receipt). `FleetLane` is (owner, lane). Filling the SSE seam would have meant dividing a person's rate across their lanes — arithmetic over money, which this platform reads and never computes. So `FleetLane.BurnRate` stays nil and its seam note now names **`GET /api/meters`** as the burn-rate surface, which serves those rows VERBATIM at the view's own grain. One figure, one source, one grain.
- **`/api/meters` serves the v0 budget ABSENCE, and the reason is read rather than restated.** The gauge half comes through the `MeterReader` seam (extended additively with `CacheReadWeight`, `Assumed` and `BudgetDeclared` so the S10.4 "assumed" label rides every reading): weighted consumption is ALWAYS real, and with no declared budget `pressure_applicable` is false and `pressure` carries no number — a fabricated denominator would be worse than none (D4). The budget block is `cost_budget_remainder` served through `history.SelectView`, so `remainder_status = 'UNAVAILABLE'` and its `absence_reason` arrive from the view that owns them and are never re-authored in `internal/api`. Budget PERSISTENCE arrives with its edit verb (B6-2), not with this read. When the query surface is unwired the money blocks render as stated absences and the gauge half still reads — an absent view surface must not take the whole meter down.
- **R8 is a real negative with a probe.** `TestNoMoneyIsComputedInTheAPI` scans every non-test file of `internal/api` for token fields, price/rate identifiers, the `internal/metering` import, and multiplication on any line mentioning usd/cost/consumption — with a planted probe proving the scan can fail. Every dollar figure the API serves is READ: from the metering seam, or from a Layer-0 view through `internal/history`'s own verb.
- **`/api/events` is a TRANSPORT and the file is scannable proof of it.** Seven GET routes cover the registries (Layer-0 views + the 49-query catalog with categories, typed axes and per-query slots), `SelectView`, `RunQuery` (slots as `?slot_<name>=`, namespaced so a slot can never collide with `limit` or `q`), `Ask`, `Search` and `AskOpenSQL`. `historyapi.go` builds no SQL, executes no read, clamps nothing of its own (the caller's limit passes through the package's bounds unchanged) and re-implements no guardrail, audit, redaction or confidence logic — asserted by a comment-stripped source scan with its own probe, comments stripped because the file's doc comments name the very constructs it must not contain. **The catalog's SQL is not on the wire**: a client sees names and slots, never statements.
- **A refusal is an ANSWER, and that is the load-bearing part of the transport.** `internal/history` returns a populated `Answer` ALONGSIDE an error on every path where something honest was produced — a Layer-2 refusal, an unavailable seat, a timed-out query — and those answers carry the audit block that is the artifact the guardrail exists to produce. `writeAnswer` therefore serves any non-empty answer at 200 with its audit, folding the error into the notes; only a genuinely empty answer (a name outside the closed registry, a slot error) maps to a status. A disambiguation card is likewise a 200-class answer. **Layer 2 has its own route** and nothing falls through to it: the test drives every Layer-0/1 route and asserts the `history.query_audited` count stays at the ONE deliberate act.
- **R20/OQ6 — the redaction edge widens to an ENUMERATED set, and the enumeration is honest in both directions.** Research/18 §7-C2·1 always scoped C2 to "SSE/REST"; the REST half did not exist until now. `redactionEdgeFiles` names exactly `{sse.go, reads.go}` — no wildcard, so a new file cannot join by accident — and the test now ALSO asserts each enumerated file still calls the primitive, so a stale name cannot stand in for an edge that stopped protecting anything. Every original negative limb is unchanged (review/preview/accept/intake never import it; retention and history still do). Payload-bearing responses redact at serialization through the shared `writeReadRedacted`, which is why `meters.go` and `historyapi.go` can serve payload-derived content without the primitive appearing in them. **Not blanket middleware:** the task detail goes out UNWRAPPED, because SPEC/PLAN artifacts, receipts and the pipeline's own card are S13/S06 product content and structurally exempt (§7-C2·2), and its payload-derived members are enumerated structural fields — a stage id, a duty name, a sha256, an outcome from a closed vocabulary — never a payload body served verbatim.
- **Absences are rendered, never errors that hide the subject.** A task before its drafting stage carries `artifacts_absent` WITH ITS REASON and still reads; a run before its receipt carries `receipt_absent` rather than a zero; a task with no registry-resolved project lands in the `'(no project)'` bucket, which is also SELECTABLE as a filter value. The task detail reads even with no pipeline surface wired — its 503 case moved to the routes that genuinely need the surface.
- **No new things.** NO new ⚙ (`internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged, **118/33**; the only ⚙ this packet's code touches is the already-consumed `obs.sse_keepalive`), NO new dependency (`components.lock` **21**, `go.mod` unchanged), NO migration — `QueryRunEventsByType` SEEKS in both caller forms, asserted by EXPLAIN, so **0001–0016 stay byte-untouched and 0017 was never opened**. (This sentence originally credited 0015's `run_events_run_type_idx` for both forms; that was WRONG for the production multi-type form and is corrected in place at drain D3 below — a record correction of an error, not a dated-truth append.) New bounds are structural constants with named reasons: `readPageDefault` 200 / `readPageMax` 1000 (mirroring `history.DefaultRowLimit`/`MaxRowLimit` — one read surface, one page size), `runDetailRecordCap` 500, `stageDetailCap` 512. Interim under the standing settings-tab directive. **$0** — every test is tier-F and hermetic; the Layer-2 leg runs on the honest unwired posture, which is the audited-refusal shape the transport contract is about, and never dials a seat. `Spec/`, `Docs/`, `Research/` and `P3/STATE.md` untouched; nothing pushed.

### §38 drain round 1 (P3-B6-1 drain r1, D1–D13) — corrections and two coordinator rulings

The fresh-context evaluation returned FAIL on 5 above-nit items plus 10 nits, having adversarially verified the packet first: every load-bearing security property held under revert/plant probes, all six OQ dispositions were confirmed honored, and deviations 1–3 were ruled SOUND. All 13 triaged items applied; D5's referral path was NOT needed (the operator-only bit is exported registry metadata, so `internal/history` stayed frozen and byte-unchanged).

- **RULING (a) — serving a DRAFT spec/plan on the task detail is RATIFIED.** `Artifacts` reads `CurrentPair`, so a pre-approval task renders its draft with its serialized `status` fields intact. That is the honest see-everything read the operator asked this platform for; S15.5's "confirmed specification" is the APPROVED-VIEW display contract, which B6-5 renders. Nothing executes from this read — execution still demands `ApprovedPair` (D10).
- **RULING (b) — the task's stored TITLE stays as-stored, while payload-DERIVED strings redact.** A task object is not an observability projection, and codor C2 never applies to deliverable or task content (§7-C2·2). The board/list snapshot-frame redaction is unchanged. The line is CONTENT vs PROJECTION, not REST vs SSE.
- **D1 — a non-UTC date bound was compared as TEXT, and it excluded real rows.** Stored `*_ts` columns are UTC RFC3339 text and the filters compare lexicographically, so `since=2026-06-01T23:59:59+12:00` (= 11:59:59Z) sorted ABOVE a row stored at 15:00:00Z and wrongly excluded it — reproduced. `readTimeRange` now re-renders every parsed bound as `t.UTC()` before binding, putting both sides of every compare in one representation. Locked by `TestDateFiltersNormalizeToUTC`, which drives the offset form on both `/api/runs` and `/api/tasks` and **fails on the pre-drain code** (verified by reverting the normalization).
- **D2 — the follow-up lineage was implemented and completely untested.** `TestTaskDetailLineageBothDirections` seeds a real S13.9 edge per owner and asserts CONTENT in both directions (the source task succeeds nothing and is succeeded by the follow-up; the successor is succeeded by nothing and succeeds the source, naming the deliverable and revision), plus the cross-owner posture — bob's edges never appear in alice's lineage, the wrong member gets 403, and the operator reads both.
- **D3 — the index attribution was WRONG, and full-name pinning is what proved it.** EXPLAIN measures the production MULTI-type form (`type IN (?,?,?)`) seeking through **0001's `run_events_run_idx`**, not 0015's `run_events_run_type_idx`; only the ONE-type form reaches the 0015 composite, where run_id and type are both equality-usable. Both SEEK, so no code and no index changed — but the comment, the §38 claim above and `QueryLatestTypeForRun`'s neighbouring note are corrected per FORM rather than averaged. The index test now pins FULL index names, and doing so immediately caught a second, pre-existing imprecision: `QueryMaxSeqByKey`'s operator form is served by `run_events_type_ts_idx`, not `run_events_type_seq_idx`. Substring pinning is how both survived.
- **D4 — the transport's refusal property now has a REAL guardrail refusal behind it.** The unavailable path has no outcome branch of its own, so a refactor mapping audited outcomes to statuses would not have been caught. `openSQLServer` reproduces the `newOpenSQLStack` rig in the api test package (local.FakeServer + a real `local.Duty` + the real read-only handle + a real advisory run; tier-F, **$0**, seat-not-servable SKIPS rather than dials). The seat emits `SELECT payload FROM run_events` — a well-formed statement refused on its merits for reading a base table — and the test asserts a 200 answer carrying `outcome=refused` with the refusal naming what it declined, the `history.query_audited` row scoped to the asker, and the household's rows untouched. `TestLayer2RealAcceptedStatementAnswersOverHTTP` is the non-tautological control: the same rig with a legitimate statement EXECUTES and returns rows. **Recorded reading:** the advisory run is an expected platform-scope addition (S12.1 R18 — every duty call meters), so the disturbance assertion is over the owners' own rows, never a raw table count.
- **D5 — a deliberate AuthZ refusal was answering 500.** `task_project` has no owner column, so the store refuses it to a member on purpose; the transport's default branch mapped that to an internal error, which reads as "the platform broke" for a decision the platform made correctly. Reproduced. The transport now pre-checks the **exported registry metadata** — `history.View.OwnerColumn == ""` — and answers 403 before asking, so the decision is made on the same closed registry the store consults and NEVER by matching an error string. `internal/history` needed no change and stayed frozen. The test pins the registry bit itself (task_project operator-only, cost_per_run owner-scoped) so it cannot pass for the wrong reason, and reproduces the 500 when the pre-check is removed.
- **D6 — the contract provenance pointed at the wrong function.** `contract.go` cited stageevents.go:124/:129, which is `stageOutcome`, a classifier. The producers are `appendStageStarted`/`appendStageFinished`; the provenance now cites their true post-drain lines. A provenance that names a non-producer is worse than none — it sends the next reader to the wrong place with confidence.
- **D7 — `boundDetail` could split a multi-byte rune.** Truncating at a byte offset left a fragment that marshals as U+FFFD, corrupting the tail of the one field that says why a stage ended. It now walks back to a rune start. Tested across six pad offsets so whichever byte the cap lands on is covered, asserting valid UTF-8, no introduced U+FFFD, the truncation still MARKED, the cap still honored, and an in-bounds non-ASCII detail carried verbatim.
- **D8 — `taskRuns` was unbounded.** A task's runs are its intake/compose/execute/verify legs plus recovery successors, which is a handful — but expectation is not a bound, and an unbounded read on the shared single connection is a liveness risk whatever the expected shape. Bounded by the existing `readPageDefault`.
- **D9 — the never-percent scan now covers the money and query-layer shapes**, including each routed answer. Two near-misses are DECLARED rather than left implicit: `pressure` and `pressure_applicable` are the S10.4 gauge's named quantity against a declared denominator, not a completion estimate. The test asserts they are the only ones, and that no undeclared `_pct`/`_ratio`/`_fraction`/`utiliz`-shaped key arrives — so a third near-miss cannot slip in unnoticed and the declaration must track the shape.
- **D10 — payload-derived stage strings on the task detail now redact.** The task detail goes out UNWRAPPED for the ruling-(b) reason, but its stage-progress entries are lifted out of `run_events` payload bodies by key, and "enumerated by key" is not the same property as "cannot carry a secret". `redactStageProgress` applies the primitive per string VALUE (§30's own definition) before serialization. Tested with a planted `sk-ant-…` in a payload's `stage` field: `[REDACTED:…]` on the wire, verbatim in the stored row, the task's own title untouched, and an honest stage kind byte-unchanged. **Fails on the pre-drain code** (verified by removing the call).
- **D11 — a cancelled session lost its finish row.** The `stage.finished` append rode the request context, so a session ending BECAUSE that context was cancelled — the ending whose record matters most — wrote nothing. It now appends via `context.WithoutCancel`, the §37-C audit-append precedent. **OQ3 is untouched**: process death still writes nothing, because nothing is left running to write it. Locked by a cancel-path test driving `Session` with an adapter that cancels then fails, asserting the started/finished PAIR lands with `outcome=error`; **fails when the WithoutCancel is reverted**.
- **D12 — the transport search scope test was vacuous.** The FTS corpus was empty, so both members matched zero rows and the assertion proved nothing. The api harness now seeds one searchable record per owner directly into `history_fts` (internal/api does not import the projector that maintains it, so the rows are written in the shape it writes), and each member is asserted to match their OWN row and NOT the other's, with the operator matching both.
- **D13 — a stray non-English word in a failure message** is corrected to English.
- **Accepted without change, recorded:** N12 (`readScope`'s ok-discard is unreachable behind `requireIdentity` and fails closed) and N13 (open-SQL GET semantics, carried to B6-7 grounding).

## 39. The S15.6 decision plane — approvals core (P3-B6-2A; S15.6, S15.2, S02.3/S02.7, S06.9, S13.9, S01.9)

The **first of three parts** of B6-2 (the coordinator split A/B/C, 2026-07-28). Part A is the S15.6/S02 heart: the one risk-ranked approval inbox, the payload-hash-pinned retry-safe answer verbs with their tier mechanics, the 4.5 cancel verbs and the S13.9 follow-up spawn. **Parts B (meters + oversight verbs + drag hint, migration 0017) and C (benchmark driver + verdict backend) are LATER packets — their seams are NAMED in code and nothing of them is built.** Part A opens **no migration**: 0001–0016 are byte-untouched and `user_version` stays 16.

- **One endpoint, seven kinds, the landed derivations REUSED.** `GET /api/approvals` calls `projector.inbox` and flattens its seven card kinds into one ranked list (`internal/api/approvals.go`); it rebuilds none of them and changes none of their per-kind owner scope — a member still sees own asks/flags/verdicts, the operator still sees all plus the platform-scope kinds, and the blind-pair verdict stays REQUESTER-owned. `InboxAsk` is deliberately NOT widened to carry the stored snapshot: that field feeds the SSE inbox frame, whose contract this packet does not touch, so the full card is read by an ENRICHMENT query in the approvals handler instead. The five oversight kinds are listed as `answerable:false` with a reason NAMING the part that owns their verb — an inbox that hides what it cannot yet answer is worse than one that says where the answer lives.
- **The pin is the ONE canonicalization, exported rather than re-implemented.** `gates.CanonicalHash` is new and is `canonicalJSON`+`hashPayload` under an exported name; the approvals surface hashes an ask's stored snapshot through it, and serves an EFFECT's `payload_hash` verbatim from its journal row. A second canonicalization would be a second answer to "is this the same payload?", and the whole retry-safety rule rests on there being one. `internal/api` therefore imports `internal/gates` directly (the `*intake.FollowUp` / `*accept.Accepter` precedent — a narrow held dependency, not a new seam), and `api.Config.Effects` holds the journal.
- **Retry-safety, in one place, before any verb dispatch.** Resolved-first, then pin: an already-resolved item returns its recorded state at 200 (`applied:false`), a resolved item answered DIFFERENTLY is a 409 `already_answered`, and an OPEN item whose current canonical hash differs from the quoted one is a 409 `stale_payload` **carrying the fresh card back**. Both probes are adversarial: the stale-hash test re-reads the subject to prove NOTHING fired, and the double-submit test drives the identical request twice against a real journal and a real ask row.
- **Tier semantics are one function.** `applyTierRules`: Low batches, Medium and High are individual, High always steps up. The four S06.4 tiers fold onto three (`trivial` batches with Low). **A card that declares NO tier reads as Medium** — the untiered cards at v0 are the S07.7 verify escalations and the S13.7 onboarding approval, which are owner-answered and release nothing outward (§16), so Medium is both correct for them and the safe default: it never batches an unclassified card and never demands a step-up the landed answer path does not demand. **Every S02.7 effect approval is High by definition** and the tier is not read off the row — an effect that could downgrade itself out of the PIN re-prompt is exactly the hole the tier exists to close.
- **D10 co-approval, and what "platform-level" reads as (a recorded reading).** A platform-level effect needs BOTH the owner's and the operator's approval before `gates.Approve` fires; each is its own `decision.recorded` row and both states are served on the card, DERIVED from those rows because the single `approved_by` column records only the FINAL approver. **Platform-level = the effect is attributed to no run.** Every run belongs to one person's task, so a run-attributed effect is that person's own outward act and its owner is the whole decision; an effect with no run attribution is nobody's task — the platform acting on shared ground. The rejected alternative was a new payload field, which would have invented a contract no landed producer speaks. Either party may DENY (a conjunction breaks on one refusal); the role bit decides VISIBILITY, never authorship, so the operator answering another person's owner-scoped card is 403 — which is what the landed pipeline already enforces for asks (D10) and what this transport must not undercut.
- **OQ2 — deny is `gates.Journal.Deny`: proposed→`failed` with `{denied, actor, reason}`.** The 0001 state CHECK is immutable and has no `denied`, so the honest terminal for a refused proposal is the journal's own finish state with the refusal IN the record: terminal, never executed (attempts 0, no idempotency key ever set), and gone from the inbox because the derivation lists `proposed` rows. The rejected alternative — leave it `proposed` and hide it — is an unbounded zombie contradicting the journal's lifecycle.
- **OQ1 — `run.transitions` gains queued→`finalized`, with its per-edge citation.** 4.5 ("any run can be cancelled cleanly at any time") read through the §14-reading-9 mapping extended to the one state the mapping did not reach: a queued run has done no work, so `completed` would assert an execution that never happened, and finalize-with-card is exactly the never-ran story. No generation bump (nothing was ever fenced). The queue row settles in the SAME transaction via the new `scheduler.SettleQueuedTx`, so the claim loop can never pick up a run whose cancellation committed — asserted by a post-cancel `Tick` that dispatches zero. **No receipt is materialized**: a run that never ran consumed nothing, and a receipt would assert a measurement never taken.
- **The cancel mapping, complete.** running/draining → `completed` with the cancel reason and the acting PERSON on the transition; parked → `finalized` with any open ask closed in the same tx (generation unchanged — a cancel takes no resume edge); queued → the OQ1 edge; claimed/new → 409 `claim_in_flight` (the transient CAS window is never raced); already-terminal → the honest already-ended answer with nothing fired; `crashed` → reported, because the recovery ladder owns a crashed run's disposition and reaching in would race that decision. Kanban `cancelled`. Task cancel = every non-terminal run under the same mapping.
- **The live-session ladder, and the crash corpse it must NOT leave.** `internal/stage/cancel.go` adds an in-process registry mapping a run to its live `adapters.Session`, filled at spawn through the same `OnSession` seam the S05.3 stage split uses and emptied when `DriveStage` returns. The cancel verb runs `Session.Cancel` (the S03.1 boundary→abort→TERM→KILL ladder) BEFORE the terminal transition, so the disposition the adapter reports is the cancel's. It also records the cancel, and `Skeleton.crash` consults that record: a dispatch leg unwinding BECAUSE a human cancelled it files no crash corpse, since a corpse there would tell the recovery ladder to fork the very work the person just stopped.
- **NO AUTO-KILL is asserted, not asserted-about.** `TestCancelIsReachableFromTheHTTPVerbsOnly` AST-scans every non-test file in the tree for calls to the cancel entry points and allows exactly three files — the HTTP verb handlers (`internal/api/actions.go`), the `api.CancelSurface` adapter (`internal/stage/surface.go`) and the machinery itself (`internal/stage/cancel.go`). It carries its own non-tautology probe (the scan must SEE the sanctioned caller when not excluded). The §31 watchdog import wall and the §35 benchmark no-kill wall are untouched and still pass.
- **§29 — a CO-PRODUCTION, not a new type.** `decision.recorded`'s contract row pre-authorized "B6 joins as a co-producer when the generic approval-inbox verbs land (S15)", and that is exactly what happened: `internal/api` mints it for the effect approve and the effect deny — **the only two acts in this packet with no landed canonical audit row of their own**, because journal transitions are effects-row updates rather than `run_events` appends (§8). Everything else is left alone per OQ8: ask answers carry `intake.state`/`intake.delta_decision`/the verify events, cancels ride `run.state_changed`, and the follow-up spawn rides `intake.followup_spawned`. The provenance note now names the real co-producer `file:line`; the inventory stays **90 minted / 5 declare-only / 95 registered**, and `TestInventoryTotals` now counts DISTINCT producer types so a co-production cannot read as phantom growth.
- **Redaction: the edge widens by ENUMERATION, the content line unmoved.** `redactionEdgeFiles` becomes `{sse.go, reads.go, approvals.go}` — no wildcard. The watchdog / drift / benchmark-alarm cards are lifted out of `run_events` payload bodies and redact per string VALUE before serialization; the ask snapshots and effect payloads in the SAME response are S06/S02 decision CONTENT and go out unwrapped, exactly as the task detail does (§38 ruling (b)). All original negative limbs are unchanged, and the never-percent scan is extended over every new shape with its own undeclared-ratio guard.
- **No new things.** NO new ⚙ key (`internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged, **118/33**; the two keys newly consumed — `effects.approval_expiry` and `freshness.max_age` — ride the existing narrow `api.Settings` Duration seam, which needed no extension), NO new dependency (`components.lock` **21**, `go.mod`/`go.sum` unchanged), NO migration (**0001–0016 byte-untouched**), and `internal/history`, `internal/redact`, `internal/watchlist`, `internal/conformance`, `internal/benchmark` (incl. `registered.go`) are untouched. New bounds are structural constants with named reasons: `approvalsListCap` = `readPageDefault` (one read surface, one page size — an inbox is a page a person works through) and `answerBatchCap` 100 (a batch is applied item-by-item on the ONE writer connection, S02.1). Interim under the standing settings-tab directive. **$0** — every test is hermetic and tier-F; the cancel battery registers a fake session and spawns no process. `Spec/`, `Docs/`, `Research/` and `P3/STATE.md` untouched; nothing pushed.
- **Declared reading against the brief's §7 rubric item 4.** The rubric asks a Low-tier batch to produce "N `decision.recorded` rows". Under the binding OQ8 disposition it produces N *individually applied, individually audited* acts whose audit rows are their own canonical type (`intake.state` per item), because every Low-tier answerable card at v0 is an ask and an act with a landed canonical row is NEVER double-minted. The test asserts N applications and N canonical rows and zero double-mints; the letter of the rubric line is superseded by the disposition, and the reading is recorded here rather than silently taken.

### §39 drain round 1 (P3-B6-2A drain r1, D1–D12) — corrections

The fresh-context evaluation returned FAIL on 5 above-nit items plus 9 nits, having adversarially verified the packet first: every load-bearing clause held under revert-probes and all three coordinator rulings were confirmed honored in code. All 12 triaged items applied; no referral was needed.

- **D1 — the crash suppression was STICKY, and that is a zombie factory.** A cancel whose terminal transition failed (or a run that resumed afterwards) left the flag set forever, so the run's NEXT genuine crash filed no corpse and the recovery ladder never forked it — the precise failure D1.3's pause-and-flag posture exists to prevent. The mark is now **single-use and generation-scoped**: it carries the generation it was taken at, `crash()` honors it only at that generation and CONSUMES it, `CancelRun` clears it on every exit path where the transition did not commit, and a resume's generation bump (§8) renders any survivor inert. **The old test was tautological** — it asserted the post-transition case, where completed→crashed is refused by the FSM anyway, so it passed with the guard disabled. The replacement stages the real race (the leg's `crash()` arriving while the run is still `running`, where running→crashed is a legal edge) and **fails on the guard-disabled code** (verified by reverting the guard); its siblings cover the stale-mark-after-resume and failed-cancel-clears cases.
- **D2 — the batch test asserted only half the ratified ruling.** It proved 2 applications and 2 canonical rows but never that ZERO `decision.recorded` rows were minted, which is the half OQ8 and this section actually claim. Asserted now.
- **D3 — the co-approval derivation was not cycle-scoped, and the journal reverts.** S02.7 sends an effect back to `proposed` on approval EXPIRY or payload DRIFT; signatures from the cycle before were still counting, so (a) a re-approval deduped against the stale row and fired `gates.Approve` with no fresh audit row, and (b) an expired owner-yes still let the operator alone complete a platform-level conjunction. A row now counts IFF its recorded `payload_hash` equals the effect's CURRENT hash **and** its `decided_at` is inside ⚙ `effects.approval_expiry` — drift-reversions hash out, time-reversions age out. `payload_hash` is stamped into the payload at answer time; the dedup uses the SAME predicate (so a post-expiry re-approval mints a FRESH row, closing the R7-letter gap); and the same predicate BOUNDS the scan. Locked by an expired-signature test (fixture appended as a backdated row — `run_events` is append-only and the fixture respects that) and a drifted-payload test.
- **D4 — a no-op cancel rewrote the board.** `CancelTask` flipped kanban to `cancelled` unconditionally, so a task whose runs had all already ended was relabelled cancelled when it had in fact finished. The column now moves only when at least one run was actually cancelled; the no-op case and its control are both tested.
- **D5 — the no-auto-kill wall scanned one directory level.** It now walks the whole tree — `cmd/`, `tools/` and nested packages included — with a floor on the file count so a truncated walk cannot pass by doing nothing, and its non-tautology probe still passes.
- **D6 — the deny path wrote its audit row before the journal act.** A raced or refused `Deny` left an audit record of a refusal that never took effect while the effect sat in the inbox contradicting it. The journal act now commits FIRST and the row follows. The approve path is made honest on the same principle rather than mechanically mirrored: a signature that completes nothing is recorded on its own (it happened, and the effect legitimately stays proposed), while a signature that COMPLETES the conjunction is recorded only once `gates.Approve` has actually approved — so no row ever claims an approval that did not take.
- **D7 — every follow-up spawn failure answered 400.** An internal DB failure told the caller their input was bad. Client input (preset, revision existence) is now validated at the boundary and answers 400; anything reaching the spawn call fails as 500. Tested both ways, including that a refused input never drives the Start seam.
- **D8 — a raced cancel answered 500.** The run's state moving between the read and the transition is a conflict the caller resolves by re-reading, not a platform fault. `ErrCancelRaced` → 409 `cancel_raced`, staged deterministically by handing the mapping a stale run value; the classifier is asserted NOT to swallow unrelated failures.
- **D9 — `answerable` was kind-level, so the inbox rendered dead buttons.** An operator saw a member's run-attributed effect as answerable and their answer 403'd. The card now computes `answerable` (with the refusal reason carried) from predicates (`mayAnswerAsk` / `mayAnswerEffect`) that encode the same authority rule the verbs enforce through their own `authorizeOwner` paths — agreement is locked by the test driving all six caller×card combinations and asserting the card and the door agree, not by a shared call site. *(Wording corrected at landing per the re-check's N-A: the predicates feed the list; the verbs keep their own equal constructions.)*
- **D10 — `failed` is reached by two different acts.** The OQ2 denial and a provider execution failure both land on `failed`, and the repeat/conflict logic conflated them: approve-after-deny read back as "already approved". The stored result disambiguates and is what the logic now reads — deny-after-deny is a 200 read-back, approve-after-deny is a 409 naming the denial, approve-after-execution-failure reads the approval back, deny-after-execution is a conflict.
- **D11 — a queued cancel with no scheduler bound silently skipped the queue settle.** The transition and the settle are one commit or none; the verb now refuses fail-closed rather than half-committing a finalized run that the claim loop could still pick up.
- **D12 — a wrong comment.** `ErrNoRows` on an ask read does not mean "answered in the window": an answered ask keeps its row. The comment now says what the case actually is.

### §39-B — meters verbs, oversight verbs & the board-drag hint (P3-B6-2B; S10.4, S14.4–S14.6, S15.5, S15.2, S02.3)

The **second of three parts** of B6-2. Part A's text above is unchanged: this subsection records only what part B decided. Part B is the S10.4 meters MUTATIONS (the durable budget, the pause switch), the S15.5 board-drag hint, and the four oversight verbs over the cards part A listed as `answerable:false` — suppress, resume, drift dismiss, conformance acknowledge. **Part C (the benchmark driver + verdict backend) is a later packet; nothing of it is built and its seams stay named.** Part B opens **migration 0017** — the packet's own, immutable from landing; 0001–0016 are byte-untouched and `user_version` moves 16 → 17.

- **Migration 0017 is four things and each carries its reason (OQ3 a–d).** `budgets` (per (person, lane) — the grain the gauge reads at; no existing home, and a ⚙ key is barred by the frozen 118 tally); `users.automation_paused DEFAULT 0` (the 0014 `benchmark_opt_in` precedent — no migration pauses anybody); `queue.hint_rank DEFAULT 0` (claim-loop working state like `status`, and the default is what makes it additive: every ordering decision the loop made before it, it still makes); and the `cost_budget_remainder` recreate. Nothing else.
- **The budget's unit is the GAUGE's unit, never dollars (D5).** `period_tokens` is in weighted-consumption units — the same thing `Gauge.WeightedConsumption` accumulates. A flat-rate lane has no per-token price, so a currency budget on one is the exact inversion S10 exists to prevent. The unit rides the wire as a LABEL on every response, so a surface cannot render it as money by accident. `internal/metering/pressure.go` is byte-unchanged: the gauge still takes a `Budget` value and still applies the one-denominator rule — only where the value COMES FROM changed (`Budgets.Budget`, the one place the undeclared posture is produced from storage).
- **⚠ A declared refinement of OQ3(d), reported rather than papered over.** The disposition asked the recreated view for a "real remainder in the budget's own units". It cannot honestly compute one, and the reason is a fact about the platform rather than a preference: the remainder is `period_tokens` minus the gauge's WEIGHTED consumption, that fold weights cache reads by ⚙ `pressure.cache_read_weight`, and **a SQL view cannot read that value** — the 0001 `settings` table stores OVERRIDE rows only and every default lives in code (S01.10). A view that folded it anyway would be a second implementation of the S10.4 gauge running on a missing weight: two denominators where D4 and §11 allow one. So the recreate makes the BUDGET side real and additive (`budget_declared`, `budget_lanes`, `budget_period_tokens_total`, `budget_unit`, `budget_period_start`, `budget_last_declared_ts`) and says in its own `absence_reason` that the unit-denominated remainder is the gauge's reading, served per (person, lane) on `/api/meters`. Everything else the disposition asked for holds exactly: TRUE in both states, USD columns NULL either way with the D5-honest reason, every 0016 column keeping its name and meaning. **This is a referral line for the coordinator, not an acceptance.**
- **The view is per PERSON and a budget is per (person, lane), so the budget columns are named as a ROLL-UP.** `budget_lanes` counts the declared lanes and `budget_period_tokens_total` sums them; the total is a total and never a denominator, because each lane's budget is its own (D4). The view also unions `budgets` with `cost_per_person`, so a person who declared a budget and has consumed nothing still gets their declaration back — the 0016 contract ("one row per person, about the right subject") extended to the new subject.
- **The sanctioned `internal/history` edit is exactly one line.** The `cost_budget_remainder` registry row's `Description`, and nothing else in the package: the guardrail, audit and confidence logic is byte-untouched, and `git diff internal/history/` is one row of metadata. The landed `TestBudgetRemainderIsAnHonestAbsence` passes unchanged, which is the point — every property it pins (a row per person, `budget_declared` 0, `remainder_status` UNAVAILABLE, `remainder_usd` NULL, an `absence_reason` saying "no operator budget") still holds for an undeclared person. The reason TEXT necessarily differs from 0016's, because 0016's said the platform persists no budget at all and that sentence is now false; what is unchanged is the behavior the test asserts, not the string.
- **OQ4 — budget/pause authority is own + operator-any, the one role bit (D10).** A member declares or pauses their own; the operator may administer anybody's; every edit records its actor either way, so an administered budget is never anonymous. Both verbs audit **old→new**: `decisionPayload` gains two optional context members (`old`/`new`) for exactly the acts that change a stored value, and the first declaration's old side is an explicit `{"declared":false}` — an absence STATED, not a member left out.
- **OQ5 — pause is admission-stop + a stage-session park, and the third limb is named in code.** (i) The claim pass consults the flag LIVE, once per pass, and only for BACKGROUND candidates — so a person whose queued work is interactive is never even asked, and the switch cannot become a way to notice them. (ii) The execute leg checks between stage sessions and parks (`internal/stage/pause.go`); a stage boundary IS a D7 checkpoint boundary (§14 reading 8), so the approximation is coarser than S10.8's eventual gate but never wrong about where it can safely stop. (iii) `pausedPerPaidCallGate` names the remaining S10.8 seam in code and RIDES THE PARK'S OWN EVENT PAYLOAD, so the v0 approximation is visible in the log rather than only in a comment. **A failed pause-flag read never parks**: the honest failure direction for a switch that STOPS things is to keep working and say so loudly.
- **P-T08-4 is asserted field-by-field, not asserted about.** The preservation test compares the queue row (`status`, `priority_lane`, `enqueued_ts`, `hint_rank`) before and after a pause, and the parked run's state AND generation, then resumes and proves the preserved queue proceeds. Interactive work is asserted unaffected in the same fixture where background work of the same person is being held back, and one person's switch is asserted not to reach another's.
- **The pause is a PARK and the wall says so.** Nothing in `internal/stage/pause.go` names any terminal state (asserted by a source scan with its own vacuity guard), and part A's tree-wide `TestCancelIsReachableFromTheHTTPVerbsOnly` still allows exactly three files — none of them this packet's. The §31 watchdog import wall and the §35 benchmark no-kill wall are untouched.
- **The drag hint is a tie-break WITHIN one (user, class) bucket, and that is what makes it safe.** `priorityLess` applies `hint_rank` only when both candidates share an owner AND a class: `classBias` is then identical for both, so the hint cannot promote a class, and both rows belong to one person, so it cannot reach another's slots. Lower sorts earlier; 0 is "no hint". It is proven by a CONTROL/TREATMENT claim-order test over identical fixtures (a one-slot lane, a real hour of enqueue-age between the runs) — the control claims the older run, the treatment claims the hinted one, and the hint is the only difference. The negatives are asserted against the ordering function itself: a maximally-hinted background run never beats interactive, and a maximally-hinted run of another owner never reorders against mine. **Scope is OWN ONLY and the operator is inside that refusal**, not outside it: S15.5 sanctions reordering *one's own* queued tasks, and reordering somebody else's queue is a different act rather than an administrative version of the same one. A hint on a non-queued run is a 200 no-op saying the board is stale — an error there would imply an authority the drag never had. `hintRankBound` (±1000) is structural with its reason: a rank is a POSITION, and a boundary that accepts an arbitrary integer stores one.
- **OQ6 — one conformance verb, `acknowledge`, and it scopes to the RESULT it was given for.** The acknowledged red stops counting as flag-now attention and STAYS LISTED red; the card carries `acknowledged` and the inbox tier drops High→Medium. **NO verb path writes `last_result`** — asserted by a probe (the registry row is byte-identical after an acknowledgement) AND a source scan over every non-test file in `internal/api` for `UPDATE conformance_registry` / `INSERT INTO conformance_registry` / `SET last_result` / `.RecordResult(`, with a two-sided vacuity guard proving the scan catches its planted defect and does NOT fire on the derivation's legitimate `WHERE last_result = 'red'` read. **Recorded reading, narrower than OQ6's letter and deliberately so:** the acknowledgement's subject is `row_id#last_run`, so a LATER suite run — even another red — is a new failure that the old acknowledgement does not silence. That is the same cycle-scoping part A's drain D3 gave the co-approval derivation (a signature counts for the thing it was given for), and it is the safe direction: the alternative lets an acknowledgement hide a standing red obligation it was never given for.
- **OQ7 — a drift dismissal closes an INCIDENT, never a fingerprint.** The subject is `fingerprint#window-start-seq`, derived server-side from the card the operator can actually see (never from client input), so a finding in a NEW window opens a NEW card the dismissal does not touch. The horizon/cap constants are unchanged, and `drift.finding` rows are asserted still present in `run_events` after two dismissals — dismissing a card never deletes evidence (S16.2: a standing mute would be a watch deletion by another name).
- **Both card verbs DERIVE FROM THE LOG, with no side store and no schema change.** `decisionSubjects` reads the dismissals/acknowledgements back out of `decision.recorded`, bounded in SQL by `json_extract(payload,'$.card_type')` (and by the drift horizon where the card kind has one). `internal/watchlist` and `internal/conformance` are byte-untouched — the dismissal and the acknowledgement live where facts about human decisions live (S14.1). Both derivations read their decision set BEFORE opening their own cursor: the control plane shares one connection (S02.1), and a second query under a live cursor would deadlock.
- **Suppress: scope IS visibility, stated exactly.** The verb asks the LANDED open-flag derivation "which flags is this caller entitled to?" rather than re-deriving an owner rule that could drift from the projection's, and a flag outside the caller's scope is 404 — an unknown flag and another owner's must look the same from outside. The FULL anomaly class travels verbatim, suffix included (the §34 D5 regression: a transport that trimmed it to the bare rule would make run-less flags permanently un-clearable), proven by a round trip that suppresses `watchdog.spend:<owner>` and watches the card leave the list. The retune mechanics fire inside `internal/watchdog`, which is byte-untouched, and the transport adds nothing to them.
- **Resume is the ask-less park's release, and an open-ask park is REFUSED with the pointer.** For a run parked on a card, ANSWERING the card is the resume (§16; S07.7) — flipping the state underneath an open ask would leave a card nobody can act on attached to a run nobody is driving — so the verb refuses with the ask id, and the refusal fires nothing. The edge is the ratified parked→running with its generation bump (§8) and the acting PERSON on the transition, which is exactly the supersession the landed open-flag derivation already honors: **no projection changed to make the flag close.**
- **§29 — still a CO-PRODUCTION, still no new type.** The five acts with no landed canonical row (budget, pause, hint, dismiss, acknowledge) mint `decision.recorded` through part A's SAME mint site (`recordDecision`), so the contract row's provenance `file:line` stays accurate; suppress rides `watchdog.suppressed` and resume rides `run.state_changed`, and an inventory test asserts those two mint ZERO family-5 rows. The inventory is unchanged at **90 minted / 5 declare-only / 95 registered**. The contract row's note is extended to name the five acts (and its two `file:line` citations, stale since part A's drain, are corrected).
- **No new things.** NO new ⚙ key (`internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged, **118/33**), NO new dependency (`components.lock` **21**, `go.mod`/`go.sum` unchanged), and `internal/redact`, `internal/benchmark` (incl. `registered.go`), `internal/watchdog/suppress.go`, `internal/watchlist`, `internal/conformance`'s registry semantics and `internal/history` (bar the one sanctioned row) are untouched. `redactionEdgeFiles` stays at **three** by construction: every part-B response carries identifiers and status only, never card content lifted from a `run_events` payload, so the edge does not widen and the enumeration stays honest. **$0** — every test is hermetic and tier-F; nothing spawns a process, and the pause/resume batteries drive real FSM rows with no engine. `Spec/`, `Docs/`, `Research/` and `P3/STATE.md` untouched; nothing pushed.

#### §39-B drain round 1 (P3-B6-2B drain r1, D1–D8) — corrections

The fresh-context evaluation returned FAIL on 6 above-nit items plus 3 nits, having verified the migration, the dispositions, the walls and the ratified OQ3(d) basis first, and confirmed part A unregressed. All 8 triaged items applied; no referral was needed, and 0017 needed no schema change.

- **D1 — the comparator was not a strict weak ordering, and a person's drag could move another person's work.** The hint was a conditional limb inside `priorityLess`: with A and B in one (user, class) group whose hint order disagreed with their aging order, and an outsider C scoring between them, the pairwise relations formed a CYCLE (B≺A by hint, A≺C by score, C≺B by score). `sort.SliceStable` on a cyclic relation is undefined, and the evaluator drove **three different claim winners from three initial row orders, one of them the other owner's candidate**. Replaced with the coordinator-directed **score-multiset-preserving permutation** (`permuteHints`): each (user, class) group's own admission keys are collected, sorted best-first and handed back out in HINT order, and the claim pass then does ONE global sort by key. The relation is now a pure total order on a fixed per-candidate value, so it is transitive by construction; a group's key multiset is preserved, so every other owner faces the identical field; and keys never move between groups, so class boundaries cannot be crossed. Five property tests: row-order independence over every permutation of the cycle fixture, hint-honored-within-group, cross-owner position invariance, class-boundary invariance for interactive/human-blocked/scheduled neighbours, and identity-with-no-hints. **Both guard-removal probes trip**: grouping by user alone fails the class-boundary test, and restoring the old comparator limb fails the row-order test with the evaluator's own symptom (bob's run winning from one ordering).
- **D2 — `GET /api/meters` contradicted itself after a declare.** The shell's `projMeter.LaneMeter` passed `UndeclaredBudget()` unconditionally, so the Layer-0 view block reported a declared budget while the gauge block beside it reported none and served no pressure — and the S14.3 fleet snapshot had the same absence. The adapter now reads the declared budget per (person, lane) from the same store the verb writes; the stale doc comment is rewritten. Locked by a NEW HTTP-level test in `internal/shell` over the REAL adapters (`budgetAdapter` + `projMeter`): declare through the verb, then read `/api/meters` and require the two halves of one response to agree. It uses a fixed identity rather than the dev fallback, because the dev fallback would have declared for `dev` and metered `alice` and the two halves would have "agreed" by both being empty. Revert-probed.
- **D3 — the oversight cards advertised verbs as future work that now exist.** Watchdog / drift / conformance cards carried a hardcoded `answerable:false` and a future-tense reason, so a surface would render no control for an act the caller could perform. They now compute `answerable` PER CALLER from predicates that state the same authority the verbs enforce (`mayAnswerWatchdogFlag`, `mayAnswerPlatformCard`), name their live verb in `actions`, and give reasons that point at the LIVE route. The two benchmark kinds stay not-answerable-here with a reason naming part C. Deliberately NOT run through `applyTierRules`: their tier is a ranking, not a gate, and a card claiming a PIN step-up the suppress verb does not demand would describe a door that does not exist. The part-A D9 agreement test is extended to the new kinds.
- **D4 — the pause boundary existed on ONE leg.** OQ5(ii)'s own words are "the skeleton's leg loop checks the flag between sessions", and only the execute step loop did. Boundaries added to the verify drain, the compose ceremony, and each SPLIT SUCCESSOR inside a planned stage (a long split chain would otherwise ignore the switch until the whole step ended); the split path unwinds through a dedicated `errPausedPark` sentinel so the leg returns cleanly and files no crash corpse.
