The blind spot is now DEFUSED rather than mis-stated: every banned token in that file, in the token list *and* in the planted fixtures, is written split, so the file carries no unsplit banned literal at all — the gap costs nothing today and would cost nothing if glob behaviour ever changed. A test asserts the exclusion, so it stays a checked fact rather than a belief. **The allowlist widens at B6-8, in B6-8's own commit, with the S15.8 preview iframe it exists for — never quietly, and never here.** **CORRECTED (2026-07-30, P3-B6-8): the allowlist did NOT widen, and this sentence's promise was wrong rather than unkept.** The sanctioned channel landed as an iframe by `src`, which embeds a browsing context BY REFERENCE and trips none of the banned tokens — every one of them is a raw-HTML assignment or a code-execution call, so an iframe with a URL was never inside the set to be exempted from. An allowlist entry nothing needs would be a standing hole, so the allowlist stays EMPTY. What DID move is a hardening: `srcdoc` joined the banned tokens with its own planted probe, because raw HTML by attribute is exactly this list's class and B6-8 is the packet that introduced iframes.
- **Responsive skeleton** (S1.10 via S15.12): phone-first CSS that widens at a `min-width` breakpoint (a `max-width` query would mean the desktop is the base and the phone the exception — the two-products shape S1.10 rules out); no layout container carries a fixed pixel width; the nav wraps so every surface stays reachable at 375px; the connection LABEL never hides, only its detail. Asserted structurally from the stylesheet, because jsdom has no layout engine. Real-device behavior is B6-9's drill.
- **Pick probes (S16.4 #5) render, they do not merely import.** dnd renders a real Droppable/Draggable list carrying the library's own drag-handle attributes; react-diff-view parses a real unified diff through gitdiff-parser and renders hunks with insert/delete lines distinguishable in the DOM (the anchor model B6-8 needs); JSON Forms renders a Categorization+Control fixture; assistant-ui **round-trips a whole turn** through `useLocalRuntime` with a platform-owned adapter and a fetch spy that records **zero calls** — the first instalment of the checkable "assistant-cloud is never wired" negative.
- **Two harness facts worth carrying forward.** (1) `ResizeObserver` is stubbed as a no-op in the test setup: it is a LAYOUT API and a headless DOM has no layout engine, so no DOM double could implement it meaningfully — this is NOT the "jsdom is materially problematic" case OQ7 admits happy-dom for, because swapping the environment would not change it. (2) Vitest stubs CSS to empty by default — **including the `?raw` form**, which is the part worth writing down, because `import css from './index.css?raw'` looks like it should bypass CSS handling and does not. `test.css: true` is set so the responsive assertions read the real stylesheet. **RE-VERIFIED (drain r1, D5):** drain r1 raised this line as false on the reading that `?raw` sidesteps `test.css`. It was re-run rather than taken on either side's word — with `css: true` removed, all six responsive tests fail and the import yields an empty string (0 chars vs 3577). The setting is load-bearing and stays; the sentence now says WHY, so the same inference is not drawn again.
- **A note for B6-6, found by the JSON Forms probe:** a dotted ⚙ key emits as a NESTED scope (`a.b` → `#/properties/a/properties/b`, `internal/settings/uischema.go`) and the data object must be nested to match — a flat object keyed by dotted strings silently renders every control empty, because JSON Forms composes its data path by splitting on dots.

## 42. The oversight surfaces: mission control, the live board & the golden-fixture tie (P3-B6-5 part A; S15.5, S15.3, S15.12, S1.3, S2.5, S2.10, FC-v1 §1, §30/§37/§39-B/§41+§41-B)

- **The live wiring (OQ1(b), ratified and landed).** Views ride the tab's ONE **unfiltered relay** and select client-side; they subscribe with the event **TYPES** they consume and **no topic filter**. This is mechanical, not stylistic: `useConnection` subscribes topicless for the whole authed lifetime, so `desiredQuery()` is already `''` — a view that added topics would not narrow the connection, would not receive the server's per-topic `snapshot` frames, and could re-open the one connection on every navigation, putting EVERY subscriber back in debt (events.ts:176–180). Zero server change, zero `events.ts` semantic change. **The named door stays open:** distinguishing "wants nothing" (`topics: []` contributes nothing to the union — the shell's true need) from "wants everything" (`topics` undefined) is the one small `events.ts` change that would let the shell stop forcing the relay, and it is where to go if relay volume ever hurts. It was not built, because nothing today needs it.
- **REST is the truth; frames are the trigger.** The server's per-topic snapshot states are FACE-INCOMPLETE for S15.5 (no owner, no project, no stage, no effort, no cost), so every view needs its REST read regardless — and every landed read carries the `cursor` the tail resumes from. A frame therefore means "something you render changed" and the view **re-reads**; it never patches blind, which is the rule the transport already applies to itself. `useLive` (`web/src/live.ts`) is that shape as one hook, and it is the only way a B6-5 view talks to the feed.
- **Snapshot-then-tail starts at MOUNT, not at socket open** (a correction found while driving it). If the first read waited for `onResnapshot('connected')`, a host whose REST surface answers but whose feed does not would render a permanently blank view instead of honest data beside a disconnected indicator. The cost is one extra read per view per connect, which the in-flight coalescing already absorbs.
- **A resnapshot is cleared only by a read that STARTED AFTER the signal** (`owedAt`). A read already in flight took its snapshot on the wrong side of the gap, so settling with it would declare the view caught up on pre-gap data — the exact stale-poses-as-live failure the per-subscriber debt exists to prevent. A read that FAILS clears nothing: a view that could not re-read is not caught up, and the indicator stays out of `live`.
- **Frame bursts coalesce without a timer.** A frame arriving while a read is in flight sets a dirty bit; the next read starts when the current one lands. Nothing is scheduled, so §32's no-ticker rule is kept structurally rather than by a debounce somebody would have to justify — and a driven test asserts `schedule` is never called on a healthy feed.
- **Per-view declared types** (`web/src/live.ts`, chosen against `topicsForEvent`): the board consumes `run.created`, `run.state_changed`, `run.forked`, `run.wedged`, `stage.started`, `stage.finished`, `intake.state`, `routing.decided`, `limit.event`, `engine.gate_ask`, `decision.recorded`, `usage.recorded`; mission control adds `meter.utilization`. Each is there because it moves something the face renders — lifecycle moves the column, routing carries the effort mode, a limit event is what puts a park horizon on a card, a gate ask is what makes it blocked-on-a-human, a decision is what un-blocks it (and what a drag mints), usage moves the cost. `EventSource` has no catch-all, so an unnamed type is a frame the view never hears.
- **The S1.3 card face, served (OQ4(a), task-list grain ONLY).** `TaskListItem.latest_run` is now its own type, **`TaskListRun`** — `BoardRun` embedded (so the served keys stay at the same level) plus `stage`, `waiting_on_human`, `parked_until`, `cost_so_far_usd`, `effort_mode`, `downgrade_note`, `queue_hint_rank`. **`BoardSnapshot` is byte-untouched**: it shares `BoardRun`, its consumers are the SSE projections, and under OQ1(b) the topic-snapshot path is unreachable from the real app — decorating it would be dead work belonging to the door above. The five facts the card shares with the run list come from calling `decorateRunRow` rather than copying it (one derivation, two surfaces, no possible disagreement about a run's stage); cost is READ through `MeterReader.RunMeter` (the run-card precedent, never computed); the payload-derived strings redact at the existing serving edge with the rest of the body (§38 D10), because the task list already goes out through `writeReadRedacted`.
- **REPORT — the disclosed downgrade note has no discriminator in the landed payload.** `routing.decided` carries `effort` and a MANDATORY `plain_reason`, and nothing that says a downgrade happened (`degraded` is about the domain's verification maturity, not effort — reading it as a downgrade marker would be a false attribution). So `downgrade_note` is read from an explicit DISCLOSURE key (`downgrade_note` / `downgrade` / `effort_downgrade`) and is honestly ABSENT when no producer set one — which is every producer at v0, exactly as the receipt's own mode line already says ("no mode change (S10.6 downgrade ladder lands with routing S08/local tier S12)"). Promoting the mandatory routing reason into a downgrade note would have made the field always-present and always-wrong; R6 requires both directions, and both are tested from served fixtures. The field populates the moment the S10.6 ladder discloses one.
- **The recorded drag order, served and written (OQ6(i)+(ii)).** (i) `queue_hint_rank` rides the task list from a read-only `queue`⋈`runs` JOIN — the scheduler owns every write to that column and its claim-loop semantics are untouched; `null` means "no queued run", `0` is the scheduler's own "no hint". The own-queued lane renders hinted tasks first by rank, then the scheduler's default order, so **rendered order = recorded order after a reload** — which is the entire reason the rank is served at all. (ii) On drop the board POSTs **spaced ranks** (position×10, 1-based) for every own queued task whose EFFECTIVE rank changed, then re-reads; a drag that puts a card back writes nothing. **NAMED EDGE:** |rank| ≤ 1000 is the verb's structural bound, so spaced ranks reach it at position 100 and clamp there — past the hundredth own-queued task the tail falls back to the scheduler's default order, stated rather than worked around, because sending ranks the verb rejects would be worse than an honest limit.
- **The drag's structural negative.** Stage is FSM state owned by the control plane (S02), so the board has **exactly one `<Droppable>`** — the own-queued lane — and the stage columns are plain lists. There is no cross-column drop target to disable, and `hintPostsFor` refuses any source or destination that is not that lane, so even a synthesized drop result calls no verb (driven test). Non-own cards are not in the lane at all: **the operator is deliberately not excepted**, matching the verb, and a card that cannot be reordered must not look draggable. `applied:false` renders the verb's own stale-board `detail` and the board re-reads — nothing the drag believed outlives that read, and the UI never claims scheduling authority the hint does not have (§39-B).
- **The kanban vocabulary is DATA (OQ7, ratify-as-data).** The six landed values `{intake, executing, verifying, attention, done, cancelled}` are columns with display labels; producers are byte-untouched. **Forward-tolerant by construction:** a stored value the list does not know gets its OWN column, labelled with the raw string — a new producer string must never vanish a card, and inventing a friendly name for a value the platform has not decided on would be worse than showing the value (S14.2 rule 3, applied client-side).
- **The golden fixtures — one shape, two consumers (OQ2, ratified).** `web/src/fixtures/api/*.json` holds the canonical body of every read these views call, and `internal/api/apifixtures_test.go` asserts the REAL handlers still serve it. The vitest doubles IMPORT the same files as their response bodies (via `?raw`, so no `resolveJsonModule` and no config change), which means a Go handler that renames a field fails the **Go** suite in the same commit instead of silently becoming `undefined` in a view — the §40-C D3 twin-maintained-copy hazard, closed across the language boundary. **Compare-only by default**, so §2's "tests never write outside `t.TempDir()`" holds for every run CI can make; regeneration is `SINET_WRITE_API_FIXTURES=1 go test ./internal/api -run TestWebAPIFixtures`, and a test asserts CI never sets that variable. The fixture world is **fixed-clock** — every seeded stamp is a literal and no row goes in through a helper that stamps `time.Now()` — and `TestWebAPIFixturesAreStable` seeds twice and diffs, so a read that carries a live reading is caught naming the read rather than churning the committed file.
- **REPORT — the money scan could not tell a pointer from a multiply.** `TestNoMoneyIsComputedInTheAPI` flagged `CostSoFarUSD *float64` as arithmetic. The honest fix was to make the scan precise, not to exempt the line: **gofmt** writes a binary multiply either fully spaced (`a * b`) or fully tight (`a*b`), and a pointer type as space-before/none-after (`X *float64`) — and `gofmt -l` must be empty for this tree, so the third form cannot be arithmetic. The pattern now accepts the two arithmetic spacings and rejects the declaration one, with probes in BOTH directions so the tightening stays deliberate. Without it a nil-able money field — the honest absence a zero would have faked — was unimplementable.
- **REPORT — the Layer-0 view registry serves Go field names.** `/api/events/views` emits `Name`/`Question`/`OwnerColumn`/`Description`/`Order` because `history.View` carries no json tags, while its sibling `history.Query` does and emits snake_case. The client consumes both as served: renaming a landed read's keys is a contract change, not a tidy-up, and `internal/history` is byte-frozen this packet. Recorded so the inconsistency is a known fact rather than a surprise for the next consumer.
- **Client display constants, structural with named reasons** (no ⚙ key exists and the browser cannot read the registry before login — the §41 backoff precedent; interim under the standing settings-tab directive): `recentlyFinishedWindowMs` 24 h, because mission control answers "what happened since I last looked" and a day is the span a household's work is planned across — it changes what is SHOWN, never what exists; `rankSpacing` 10 and `hintRankBound` 1000, reasoned above.
- **Rendered honesty, made structural** (`web/src/parts.tsx`). An absence is rendered with its reason and never filled: a null cost is "no meter reading" and never $0; a park with no marker is "parked, no horizon given" and never a fabricated time; an undeclared budget shows no number and a pressure figure needs a declared denominator to exist at all. Money prints **as served** — the unit comes from the field name on the wire, and no view sums across owners or divides a person's burn rate across their lanes (§37; scanned with a planted probe). Timestamps render **verbatim**, deliberately unlocalized: every stamp the platform serves is UTC RFC3339, an ambiguous "2:00" on a park horizon is worse than an unambiguous instant, and human-friendly rendering is a whole-surface decision that belongs with the B6-9 S15.12 sweep. There is **no** progress-bar, percentage, fraction or ETA primitive in the tree, and a scan says so.
- **Mission control's buckets are DISJOINT, and one of them is a catch-all.** Blocked-on-a-human is a strict subset of parked (the server derives it as parked-with-an-open-ask), so a run waiting on a person appears under the human bucket only and "parked" is left meaning "waiting on a clock". The sixth bucket exists so a state the five do not name — `claimed` today, whatever a later packet adds tomorrow — is still on screen: **a run must never vanish from mission control because a client-side list did not anticipate its state**, and a driven test asserts every served row is placed.
- **The history panel is a QUERY INSTRUMENT, not a live projection, and says so on screen.** Everything else on mission control is kept current by the feed; an answer is a point-in-time reply to a question somebody asked, and re-running it on every frame would be noise at Layer 0 and a repeated model call at Layer 2. Asking again is an act, so the panel offers one. The choice surface is the SERVED registry (a hand-written list would be a second copy of one that already exists); a **disambiguation card and a refusal-with-audit render as ANSWERS**, never swallowed into an error banner, and Layer 2's lower-confidence flag renders wherever its answers render (G3 D3.5).
- **REPORT — scope reading on R7.** Follow-up lineage and `project_choices > 1` render on the TASK DETAIL, not the board card: `TaskLineage` lives only on `GET /api/tasks/{task}`, the task list serves neither, and OQ4's ratified field list does not include them — adding them would have exceeded the disposition. R7's project grouping (including the honest `(no project)` group) is on the board, in full.
- **Counters at part A:** migrations **0001–0019** untouched · event inventory **91/5/96** · ⚙ **118/33**, `internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged · `components.lock` **29** · `web/package.json` + `package-lock.json` byte-unchanged (dnd is consumed as installed; **liveness re-verified at execution — @hello-pangea/dnd 18.0.1 is still the registry `latest`, Apache-2.0, not deprecated**) · escape-scan allowlist still EMPTY · vitest **100 tests / 12 files** (was 57/8) · Go **43 packages** green.

### §42-B — task detail, fleet & the personal filters (P3-B6-5 part B; S15.5 ¶3–¶5, S1.4, S2.2, S2.4, S2.5, G2 D2.8, §38 ruling (a))

- **Every human decision, served (OQ5(a) as ratified).** `GET /api/tasks/{task}` gains an additive **`decisions`** block, derived server-side in two reads because the family arrives two ways: the run-scoped Human-decision rows (an accepted deliverable, an answered intake delta) are found BY RUN, while `decision.recorded` — which carries **no run_id**, its subject travelling in the payload — is found by matching that subject against everything the task IS: the task id, its run ids, and the **effects** those runs proposed (an effect approval names `effect:<effect_id>`, so the effects join is the only thing that ties it back). The card id's `<kind>:<native-id>` shape is matched on both halves, via `json_extract` (the oversight.go precedent). Bounded by `runDetailRecordCap`; every lifted string redacts at the serving edge through `redactTaskDecisions`, the `redactStageProgress` precedent (§38 D10 — "enumerated by key" is not "cannot carry a secret"). **The misses are documented on the field and are deliberate:** a decision keyed to none of those subjects is not a task decision — a person-level automation pause and a per-person budget edit are the live examples, and widening the match to "everything this owner ever decided" would put another task's approvals on this task's page. A driven test proves a decision about a different task does not appear.
- **`firstBool` joins `firstString`** (projection.go): the D10 co-approval limb is a payload BOOLEAN, and reading it as a string would have made every operator approval render as a member's.
- **§38 ruling (a) is one component, not a habit.** `StatusLine` renders the artifact status for the spec and the plan alike: an approved pair reads "confirmed", a draft reads `draft (not approved — nobody has signed this off yet)`. S15.5 calls this surface "the confirmed specification", which is the APPROVED view — presenting an unapproved draft under that heading is the one way this page could mislead about something a person is about to sign off, so the label lives in one place where it cannot be forgotten on one of the two artifacts. Both directions are driven from SERVED bodies (the fixture intake surface serves an approved pair for one task and a draft for another); `artifacts_absent` renders its reason and the task still reads.
- **Render-verbatim-as-served, with the scan that makes it structural (OQ3 as ratified).** The receipt prints `direct_use.label` and `mode.note` exactly as stored — **no view file contains either registered string**, and `scans.test.ts` proves it with the tokens split so the scan's own file carries no literal it forbids. The measured form is exercised through the same render path with a served row, so the path is checked without a client constant existing anywhere.
- **REPORT — no landed read serves the AGGREGATE done-directly figure.** `DirectUseAggregate` (with `measured (benchmark n=…)`) is produced by `benchmark.Practice.DoneDirectly` and reachable only through the readout; the `cost_done_directly` Layer-0 view CITES each receipt's own per-run block, and no REST route serves the aggregate. So at v0 the per-run heuristic line is what renders, which is exactly §13.2's "both labels side by side, per-run receipts keep the heuristic line" — the aggregate half simply has nothing to render yet. The UI needs no change when it lands: it prints the label it is given.
- **The fleet's absences are NAMED, not blank (OQ8 as ratified).** Local duty seats and GPU/VRAM render as "not wired at v0", each saying what it is waiting for. The distinction is the point: `0 seats` would be a reading nobody took, and saying nothing would let a reader infer the local tier is idle. No `internal/local` wiring was added — the fill is a B6-gate/bring-up item.
- **Accounts are never summed.** Every fleet row carries its owner and there is no total row: a cross-person total would be money arithmetic the client must not do, and it answers a question this platform does not ask (D2). The person/lane selectors narrow the DISPLAY only — the server already scoped the read (S15.2).
- **The four filters are `/?view=<id>` on mission control.** Stable, bookmarkable, push-`navigate`-able URLs that FILL the route table rather than adding to it — a new route family is explicitly not this packet's. `what-needs-me` links INTO `/inbox/:id` and `/deliverables/:id`: answering and reviewing happen on the surfaces that own them, and this filter says what is waiting without pretending to resolve it. `mine` sends `?person=<self>` rather than sieving rows after receiving them — for a member the two answers are identical, and for the operator the parameter is what makes "mine" mean mine.
- **`localMidnightISO` and the pinned test zone.** "Finished today" means midnight where the READER is, sent as the UTC instant the API compares against (the server normalizes `?since=` to UTC before comparing it lexicographically against stored UTC text, so a local-offset stamp would compare as characters rather than as an instant). **`vitest.config.ts` now pins `TZ: 'Pacific/Auckland'`** — in UTC that conversion is the identity function and a broken one would pass, so the suite runs where it cannot. The zone is pinned rather than left to the host so the result does not depend on where the suite runs.
- **The task detail's live subscription is deliberately UNNARROWED.** Filtering frames to "run_id is one of this task's runs" is the obvious saving and is wrong: a `run.created` for a run the task does not have YET is exactly the frame that would be dropped, and the new run would never appear. One task page is open at a time; correctness is worth more than the saving. Recorded because the narrowing looks right.
- **Golden fixtures extended to part B's reads** — `task-detail` (approved), `task-detail-draft`, `task-detail-bare` (artifacts absent), `receipt`, `approvals`, `deliverables-in-review`. The receipt fixture is **marshaled from the real `metering.Receipt`** rather than hand-written, so the view is built against the shape the platform actually produces; the test imports `internal/metering` for that, which production `internal/api` must never do and the money scan still forbids.
- **REPORT — a third instance of the untagged-struct pattern.** `metering.LineItem` has no json tags, so a receipt's `items` array carries Go field names (`Model`, `PricedUSD`, …) while the receipt's own fields are snake_case. Same shape of finding as `history.View` in §42; consumed as served, reported rather than renamed.
- **Counters at part B:** migrations **0001–0019** untouched · inventory **91/5/96** · ⚙ **118/33** byte-unchanged · `components.lock` **29** · `package.json` + `package-lock.json` byte-unchanged · escape-scan allowlist still EMPTY · vitest **134 tests / 15 files** · Go **43 packages** green.

#### §42 drain round 1 (P3-B6-5 drain r1, D1–D11) — corrections and the producer-fidelity root cause

- **ROOT CAUSE, recorded because it is the reusable lesson.** The B6-5 decisions-family fixtures were seeded by raw SQL with payload keys their author *imagined*, not keys any producer emits. A derive built against them passed its own tests and would have returned **nothing** in production. The OQ2 promise is that a fixture is what the handler serves; it is only worth that if the ROW under it is what a producer mints. The fixture world now DRIVES the real verbs — `review.Accept`, `gates.Journal.Propose`, `POST /api/approvals/{id}/answer` (read-the-card-then-answer, PIN and all) — and the three defects below fell straight out of doing so.
- **CORRECTION (D1) — a real `deliverable.accepted` is PLATFORM-scoped.** `internal/review/accept.go` appends it with **no RunID** and payload `{deliverable_id, revision, project_id, accepted_by, superseded}`. The first derive looked for it run-scoped, so "who accepted the deliverable" was silently empty for every real accept. The platform half now reaches it through the **deliverables table's own task linkage** — nothing in that payload names a task or a run, so the join is the only way back — and "who" reads `accepted_by`.
- **CORRECTION (D2) — `intake.delta_decision` names no actor at all.** Its payload is the S06.9 measurement hook (`{delta_id, origin, presented_items, presented_bytes, time_to_decision_s, decision, task_id, spec_plan_version}`). "Who" therefore falls back to the ROW's owner attribution, which is why the decision SELECTs now carry `user_id` (`QueryRunEventsByTypeOwned`). The one real run-scoped producer in this family was rendering an empty actor.
- **CORRECTION (D3) — the brief's "ask-answers/cancels mint run-scoped rows" is half-false, and absorbing it silently was the defect.** A CANCEL rides `run.state_changed` (`internal/api/approvals.go`: "cancels (carried by run.state_changed) are never double-minted"), which is a run-LIFECYCLE event outside the Human-decision family. It is correctly visible in `stage_progress`, where a state transition belongs. The miss is now documented on the `decisions` field itself, and the family is split by how its producers actually mint rather than by one assumed shape.
- **Naming the act from its TYPE is reading the log, not inventing.** A real accept carries no `decision`/`card_id`/`card_type` key, so `deliverable.accepted` renders as `accept` on `deliverable:<id>`. The alternative was three blanks on the family's own terminal act.
- **CORRECTION (D7) — the rank comparator, and the ratified disposition text was wrong about it.** The disposition said "hint≠0 first by rank"; the landed comparator (`internal/scheduler/workload.go`) sorts **plain ascending** with **0 as the NEUTRAL MIDDLE**. §42's "rendered order = recorded order" was therefore false in one direction: sending 0 to the end meant a task enqueued after a drag claimed FIRST from the scheduler while rendering LAST. The lane now sorts plain-ascending exactly as the comparator does, and a drop assigns spaced POSITIVE ranks across the whole lane so no 0 is left behind. Negative ranks sort ahead of the middle, as the comparator says.
- **BUILT (D4) — the live-activity feed, which did not exist.** R11 was half-shipped: stage boundaries say a stage started and ended, which is exactly the "started and ended" S2.2 rules out, and the comment claiming the runs carried the live feed was false. `api.run()` reads the run card; the task detail renders the active run's last-activity line, stage, tool and MONOTONIC counters, seeded from the REST snapshot and re-read on that run's own frames. `activityEventTypes` is broader than the board's set because "what is it doing right now" is every run-scoped frame, not only the ones that move a card between columns.
- **CORRECTED (D5) — R13 was mis-sourced.** The deliverables block rendered `lineage.succeeded_by` (the follow-up TASKS spawned from a deliverable) under the deliverables heading — a different fact wearing the same label — while the task's actual deliverables never listed. `GET /api/deliverables` gains an additive **`?task=`** filter (the OQ4-class additive-read discipline; owner scope untouched), and the block renders the real deliverables with their **served** numbered revisions rather than counting 1..N from `current_revision`. Lineage renders as lineage, in its own block.
- **DECLARED (D6).** `artifact.produced`, `deliverable.accepted`, `review.drained` join the board/mission type sets. Without them the review-ready feed and the task's deliverables only refreshed when an unrelated frame happened to arrive.
- **Two clock seams, added because determinism is a hard constraint of the fixture mechanism.** `api.Config.Now` (used by `recordDecision`) and `elapsedSeconds` now reading the projector's already-declared `now` — the same reason `review.Store.Now` and `gates.JournalConfig.Now` exist. Without them the two reads whose rows a real verb mints could not be reproduced, so they could not be committed, so the derive could not be proven against real producer output. nil is `time.Now`: production behavior is unchanged.
- **One reproducibility concession, named:** `gates.Journal.Propose` mints a UUID effect id, which no committed file can carry, so the id (a surrogate) is pinned after the real propose while the PAYLOAD and its journal-computed HASH — the things the approval verb actually checks — stay the producer's own. A hand-written `payload_hash` reads as payload DRIFT the moment the verb checks it, which is the landed S02.7 protection working.
- **PINNED (D8).** `redactTaskDecisions` was uncovered — removing the call left the suite green. The pin is probe-verified in both directions and also asserts the STORED row keeps its secret (store-raw / serve-redacted).
- **D9/D10/D11.** The inbox fixture now carries a genuinely PENDING approval-kind card beside the answered one (a second effect, proposed through the real journal), so what-needs-me shows all three kinds. The board's drag is driven through the library's own KEYBOARD drag — focus, space, arrow, space — so the notes, the `data-hint-applied` marker and the re-read are exercised by the component rather than around it; nothing synthesizes a DropResult. A failed re-rank now renders an honest error note instead of snapping back silently.
- **Counters after drain r1:** migrations **0001–0019** untouched · inventory **91/5/96** · ⚙ **118/33** byte-unchanged · `components.lock` **29** · `package.json`/`package-lock.json` byte-unchanged · escape-scan allowlist EMPTY · vitest **145 tests / 15 files** · Go **43 packages** green.

#### §42 drain round 2 (P3-B6-5 drain r2, R1–R4, FINAL under the cap) — the four residuals

- **CORRECTION (R1) to drain r1's D3 bullet, which was false about WHERE a cancel is visible.** The r1 text said a cancel is visible in `stage_progress`. It is not: `stageProgressTypes` reads only `stage.started`/`stage.finished`/`intake.state` — `run.state_changed` is not among them — and `stageOutcome`'s vocabulary is `{completed, split, error}` (`internal/stage/stageevents.go`), so a cancelled queued run appears in **no stage row at all**. Where a cancel IS visible: **`runs[].state`** on the task detail, and the **`cancelled` kanban column** (`internal/stage/skeleton.go:342` sets the status). Pointing a reader at a surface that would never show it was worse than leaving the miss unexplained; the field comment now says both places.
- **RESTORED (R2) — the D10 co-approval limb, and a distinction found while restoring it.** The r1 rewrite left `firstBool` → `ActorIsOperator` → the "(as operator)" render with zero coverage, because the driven world had one person answering her own card. Both halves are now driven for real, and they are **two different facts**: on the APPROVALS family the limb belongs to a **platform-level** effect — one attributed to no run, which is precisely what gives the operator a say (`mayAnswerEffect`) — and the fixture drives both signatures on `e-rotate`. On a **TASK page** that same card can never appear: `answerEffect` records the card's run id as the subject and a platform-level effect has none, so by construction it is nobody's task decision. What a task detail renders is the operator acting on their OWN task — the priority hint, whose subject is the task itself — so the fixture world gained an operator-owned task and drives that verb too. A test asserts BOTH: the limb on the operator's own task, and the platform-level effect's absence from `t-ship`.
- **GROWN (R3) — `activityEventTypes` now carries `engine.message`, `engine.done`, `tool.called`**, so the comment's "every run-scoped frame" is true and the activity line does not lag message-grain events. All three verified against the inventory rather than assumed: `engine.message` and `engine.done` are MINTED (`internal/adapters/adapters.go` envelope kinds); **`tool.called` is registered DECLARE-ONLY** — no producer emits it today, so declaring it is inert now and correct the day one lands, and it is registered, so this is not an invented name. The timerless coalescing already absorbs the extra frame rate.
- **PINNED (R4) — two overstatements became two tests.** (a) The additive `?task=` filter is now proven owner-scoped the standard three ways (operator both / owner theirs present / another member none), so "owner scope untouched" is checked rather than asserted in a comment. (b) `ApprovalLine`'s `answerable:true` branch now has a fixture: `answerable` is computed **per caller** (D10), so the "yours to answer" render only exists in a body an OWNER read — the golden set gained the same read taken as its owner, and the two directions are driven from two served bodies rather than one body and a hand-edit.
- **One doubled seam, named:** the priority-hint verb's SCHEDULER storage seam is the landed `fakeHints` double (constructing a real scheduler is out of proportion here). The verb, its D10 authorization and the `decision.recorded` row it mints are all real — the row is what this fixture is proving — and the queue row carries the rank the real scheduler would have persisted.
- **Counters after drain r2:** migrations **0001–0019** untouched · inventory **91/5/96** · ⚙ **118/33** byte-unchanged · `components.lock` **29** · `package.json`/`package-lock.json` byte-unchanged · escape-scan allowlist EMPTY · vitest **147 tests / 15 files** · Go **43 packages** green.

## 43. The decision surfaces: the approval inbox (P3-B6-6 part A; S15.6, S06.9, S14.4–S14.7, BENCH-REG §3/§12, S09.7, S01.9, §39+§39-B+§39-C, §41+§41-B, §42+§42-B)

- **The ten OQ dispositions, as landed.** OQ1 the ninth kind as a server-side fold; OQ2/OQ3/OQ5/OQ6/OQ7 belong to part B (the settings UI) and are **not built** — see the closing bullet. OQ4 the served benchmark vocabulary; OQ8 co-approval rendered verbatim from the served block; OQ9(b) the resume door inside the watchdog card's `actions`; OQ10 the action-first batch constraint. Each is recorded below where it landed.
- **The NINTH inbox kind — `memory_conflict` (OQ1(a), tier Medium).** One additive browse read, `memory.Store.OpenConflictsFor(affected)` (`internal/memory/browse.go`), folded into `GET /api/approvals` by `Server.memoryConflictCards` — in `approvals.go`, deliberately NOT in `InboxSnapshot`, because that struct is also the SSE `inbox` topic snapshot and goes out through `RedactJSON` while the memory family serves S09 content unwrapped (§40-C). Folding it there would have put one family's content under two different redaction postures.
  - **A STATED DEVIATION from every other kind's operator-sees-all, and it is the point.** A conflict card reaches its ADDRESSEE and nobody else, the operator included. `mayAnswerConflict` is the ONE expression of both visibility and authority (the B6-3C D2 line), the entry-detail page already hides the edge from third parties and the non-affected operator, and the resolve verb already refuses everyone else. The browse read is addressee-scoped AND the card's `answerable` is still computed through that same predicate, so the read cannot hand over a card the rule would refuse and the card still says the rule out loud.
  - **Three things the card deliberately does not carry**, each with its reason on the function: no `payload_hash` (the resolve verb is path-only and idempotent — a pin would describe a check nothing performs, and the generic answer verb's default correctly refuses the kind by naming where its verb lives); no `expiry_at` (a memory question releases nothing outward and does not lapse — a countdown would be a deadline nothing enforces); no staleness (the stored flag's served reason names one-click re-plan, which is meaningless here).
  - `applyTierRules` IS run, unlike the three oversight kinds: Medium here is a CLASSIFICATION (individual, un-stepped, releases nothing), which is exactly what that one function expresses — not a severity ranking, which is why those kinds skip it. **Event-mint stays barred** (F7: the row IS the record). A process with no memory store simply has no ninth kind — not a 503, because eight readable kinds beat failing the whole inbox over one unwired seam.
  - **Currency is honest, not compensated.** No SSE frame reaches the addressee on either edge (the Knowledge family routes to no topic; relay frames are owner-scoped to the WRITER, who is not the person waiting). The view renders a note saying so and rides the inbox's own re-read triggers; the idempotent already-closed answer is what makes a stale card harmless. No ticker, no poll.
- **OQ4 — the served answer vocabulary.** `BenchmarkVerdictForms` gains `choices[]`, `guess_sides[]` and `dispositions[]` (flat, via an embedded `BenchmarkVocabulary`), filled through one new `BenchmarkSurface` method, `AnswerVocabulary` — the `RegisteredValues` precedent exactly. `internal/api` declares the three FIELD NAMES and not one of the VALUES; `internal/benchmark` gains `VerdictChoices` / `GuessSides` / `AlarmDispositions` in `benchmark.go` (**`registered.go` stays byte-unchanged**), and `validChoice` / `validSide` / `validDisposition` now READ those lists, so the set a form offers and the set the package accepts are one list rather than two that agree today.
  - **The tie lives in `internal/shell`, and it had to.** The reverse import wall scans every `.go` under `internal/api`, tests included, so the fixture seam serves the vocabulary as literals and `TestTheServedVocabularyIsThePackagesOwn` — at the composition root, the one place both vocabularies are legitimately visible — holds BOTH the served response AND the committed `benchmark-verdicts.json` fixture to the package's constants. A §17 re-registration therefore fails the build instead of leaving the form offering last year's buttons. **The wall was not weakened.**
- **OQ9(b) — the resume door is IN the vocabulary.** The run-scoped watchdog card's `actions` is now `["suppress","resume"]`; a run-LESS platform flag keeps `["suppress"]`, because there is no run to resume. A surface that derived the control from the card's `run_id` instead would be synthesizing a verb out of a data field, which is what an action list exists to make unnecessary. Both directions and the door itself are driven.
- **TWO REPORTED FINDINGS in the landed card-shape derivation, both found by driving real producers into the fixture world.**
  - **FIXED — `readCardShape` read `decision.choices[].id`, a key no producer writes.** `internal/intake`'s S06.7 decision cards marshal `[]Option{Label, Value}` and the answer validator compares the answer's `choice` against **Value**. Every coverage / research / spec-doubt card therefore served an action list of EMPTY STRINGS, and a surface rendering from it would have drawn unlabelled buttons for verbs it could not name. Same finding as the B6-3B `verbs`/`choices` fix, on the other decision family: the Go field name was fine, the wire key was not. Pinned by a fixture whose card is a real `intake.Card`.
  - **REPORTED, NOT PAPERED OVER — a DELTA card declares no action vocabulary at all.** Its answer rides `{"action":"approve"|"reject"}` (`intake/delta.go`), but the issued card carries only `{origin, items, help}` — no `approval.actions`, no top-level `choices`, no `decision`. So its served `actions` is empty and the inbox correctly renders the card's content with NO controls and a stated reason. Naming the two verbs in `internal/api` would have put the pipeline's answer vocabulary in the transport, which is the copy the derivation exists to avoid; **the fix belongs in the producer that issues the card** and is not this packet's to make. The committed fixture carries a real delta card, so the gap is a checkable fact rather than a claim.
- **The inbox view (`web/src/Inbox.tsx`), and the four rules on its own doc comment.** The card is the authority (a control renders only where the card served the action AND `answerable` is true — never a dead button, never a door the view invented); the server ranks and the client does not (served order, and the S15.6 display classes are a LABEL per card, never a filter or a regroup — an unknown kind renders under its own served name, the §42 forward-tolerance); nothing is computed that was not served; the PIN lives only in the request.
  - **The expiry line is a display derivation of a served INSTANT and nothing else**, taken at render with no timer — the view re-reads on frames, so the figure moves when the data does. An absent `expiry_at` renders NO countdown. `describeSpan` divides a span the server handed over as two instants; there is no money, no denominator and no progress figure anywhere near it.
  - **OQ10 — the batch is ACTION-FIRST.** The person picks the one action, and only cards whose OWN vocabulary carries it become selectable, so a mixed selection is impossible rather than silently split. Each item carries its own `payload_hash` and its own answer; each outcome renders individually beside its siblings (driven with a 409 next to an applied item).
  - **The answer ENVELOPE is read off the card, not off the kind.** `answerEnvelope` inspects the stored snapshot — an `approval`/`delta` body takes `{action, contest}`, an S06.7 `decision` body takes `{choice, criteria}`, a question card takes the slot answers. A family it does not recognize returns `unknown` and the act path posts NOTHING, saying so: posting a guessed envelope would earn a refusal nobody could read. **Named boundary:** the slot editor for interview/clarification cards is not built — those cards render and their answer path sends only the force-proceed shape. Conversational answering is B6-7's.
  - **OQ8 — both approver limbs render from the served `approvals` block**, verbatim, with by-whom; the completes-nothing signature renders as recorded-and-waiting from the response's own `state` + `detail`. No client-side inference of who signed from frames.
  - **`ApiError` now carries the server's `code` and its whole JSON body**, so a caller branches on the classification the SERVER made rather than re-deriving one from message text (§30/§38). It exists for exactly one consumer: `staleCard`, which lifts the FRESH card off a 409 `stale_payload` so the next act is a re-render of what is actually there. A stale hash is never retried (driven: exactly one POST).
  - **`WithForms` reads the blind-pair form data ONCE for the whole queue, and only when a benchmark card is on screen.** A hook cannot be called conditionally, so the condition lives in what MOUNTS. An inbox with no benchmark card never asks a subsystem that may not be wired in this process.
- **Per-view declared types (`inboxEventTypes`, §42).** Twelve types, each either what PUTS a card in the queue or what TAKES one out, chosen family by family against the registry: `engine.gate_ask`, `intake.state`, `intake.delta_decision`, `decision.recorded`, `run.state_changed`, `watchdog.flagged`, `watchdog.annotated`, `watchdog.suppressed`, `drift.finding`, `benchmark.pair_recorded`, `benchmark.alarm`, `eval.score_recorded`. `EventSource` has no catch-all, so an unnamed type is a frame the view never hears.
- **The fixture world grew from two inbox cards to fourteen** (`internal/api/apifixtures_test.go`), and every one of them is producer-shaped: the ask snapshots are marshaled from REAL `intake.Card` values (the receipt-fixture precedent — a hand-written snapshot only proves the inbox can read keys somebody imagined), the memory conflict is produced by the REAL S09.7 detection through `POST /api/memory`, and the oversight rows go in as their producers write them. **Two named concessions:** the knowledge store mints a random entry id per entry, so the two ids inside the conflict card are surrogates pinned AFTER the real write ran (the effect-journal UUID precedent) — the detection, the row and the gate's own question sentence stay the producer's; and the real entry pair STAYS in the world because a knowledge entry is never deleted (the S09.5 audit trigger refuses it, which is the store protecting its own record).
- **Landed tests that moved, and why.** `routes.test.ts`'s built list gains `inbox` + `inbox-item`; `shell.test.tsx`'s deep-link test now asserts the real surface answers the URL instead of a stub echoing it; `filters.test.tsx` now FINDS the unanswerable card in the served body rather than assuming it is first (the operator's queue grew and its first card is one they can answer). The escape-scan floor moves 30 → 32 and names `./Inbox.tsx`; the **allowlist stays EMPTY** (this text said it would widen at B6-8; **it did not — see the §41-B correction dated 2026-07-30**). A test asserting the PIN is gone WALKS the DOM rather than serializing it, because serializing would put a banned token in the file.
- **Counters at part A:** migrations **0001–0019** untouched · event inventory **91/5/96** (no new type) · ⚙ **118/33**, `internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged · `components.lock` **29** · `web/package.json` + `package-lock.json` byte-unchanged (**JSON Forms is not yet wired — that is part B**) · `internal/benchmark/registered.go`, `internal/eventlog/contract.go`, BENCH-REG untouched · escape-scan allowlist EMPTY · vitest **185 tests / 16 files** (was 147/15) · Go **43 packages** green, `-race` green on `./internal/api` + `./internal/memory`.
- **NOT BUILT, and named rather than implied: part B, the settings UI (`/settings`).** R13–R22 and OQ2/OQ3/OQ5/OQ6/OQ7 are untouched — the JSON Forms renderer set, the nested-data bridge, every-setting completeness, bounds/badges/history/help, the price table, and the `eval.scores` catalog query that is the D4(b) unlock. The `/settings` route keeps its `B6-6` stub owner, so the shell still says which packet fills it.

### §43-B — the settings tab (P3-B6-6 part B; S15.9, S01.10, S18, FC-v1 §4, S10.3/S18.3, S14.5/S14.8, §40)

- **JSON FORMS 3.8.0, RE-VERIFIED AT THE PIN BEFORE WIRING** (the adopt-rail obligation, 2026-07-29): 3.8.0 IS the registry `latest` (published 2026-06-16), neither package deprecated at any version, still MIT in four independent places, upstream `eclipsesource/jsonforms` not archived with its most recent commit 2026-07-27 and a forward-looking ROADMAP, zero advisories in the GitHub Advisory DB / OSV / Snyk, and the committed `package-lock.json` integrity hashes byte-identical to what the registry serves today. **ALIVE — wired.** The RJSF fallback stays pre-registered and unused.
- **The renderer set is SINET-OWNED** (`web/src/settingsForm.tsx`), over JSON Forms' core theme-independent vocabulary only: Categorization → domain tabs, Category/Group → the emitted box, Control → one setting. **No theme package**, and a scan over IMPORT LINES says so (prose may name what it forbids; an import may not). Four testers, each matching ONE emitted element type at the same rank, so nothing competes and an element type outside the vocabulary finds no renderer — the honest outcome, since the emitter emits none.
- **The form is GENERATED and the tree hand-builds nothing.** A second scan proves it: no settings view file names a dotted ⚙ key at all. The completeness test derives its expectation from the SERVED data in both directions — every served key renders, nothing renders that was not served — and it walks all 33 tabs to do it, because tabs are the surface and a per-tab measurement would be a measurement of one tab.
- **OQ2 — the nested-data bridge, landed as `settingsData(values)`.** JSON Forms composes a control's data path by SPLITTING ITS SCOPE ON DOTS, so the emitted `#/properties/freshness/properties/max_age` reads `data.freshness.max_age`, while the API serves a FLAT list keyed by the dotted key. The pure builder binds each key's `effective`; the regression probe pins the defect in the direction that proves it — the flat object resolves NO path, so the bridge is load-bearing rather than decorative.
- **OQ2 — a write is an AUDITED ACT, so it never fires on a keystroke.** Typing moves the form model; **Save is a separate, explicit, per-key POST** (the landed write grain — no bulk write exists). Driven: a keystroke posts nothing and the page states the unsaved change instead of hiding it.
  - **DISCOVERED, and it changed the design: JSON Forms DEBOUNCES its `onChange` by 10 ms** (`debouncedEmit` in `jsonforms-react`). A parent mirroring the form model therefore lags the control, and a person who typed and immediately saved would have posted the value they replaced. **The Save act carries the CONTROL's own current value**; the mirror feeds only the unsaved-changes line, where lag is harmless. The test helper `settle()` exists for the same fact — `flush()` drains microtasks and can never reach a timer.
- **REPORTED — the registry emits a DRAFT 2020-12 JSON Schema and JSON Forms 3.8.0's bundled Ajv is draft-07**, so the default validating mode throws `no schema with key or ref …/2020-12/schema` before a single control renders. The page runs `validationMode="NoValidation"`, and that is the RIGHT posture rather than a workaround: **the registry is the one validator** (S01.10's one-artifact-set rule on the client side) — a second validator here could refuse what the registry accepts and a person would have no way to tell which answer was the platform's. The two alternatives were both refused: reaching for `ajv/dist/2020` would make a transitive dependency a direct one with no lock entry, and stripping `$schema` would be editing a served artifact.
- **OQ3 — a member gets the whole VIEW and no write affordance at all.** Every editor, both bounds fields, the `for_user` affordance and the price add are ABSENT rather than disabled (a disabled control still says "you could do this"), and the server's own `editable_reason` is shown ONCE, prominently. Driven from two served bodies, because `editable` is computed per caller — and the price table states its OWN authority for the same caller, so a member's page is two read-only bodies rather than one flag reused.
- **OQ6 — the price editor is a structured add-row form** over the known S10.3 fields, pinned by a golden fixture **marshaled from the real `metering.StoredPriceRow`** so the form cannot drift from the shape the store accepts. The store's validation is the authority and its refusals render verbatim. **There is no edit and no delete affordance because no such verb exists**: past rows are immutable by trigger, and immutability renders as the property it is rather than hiding as a limitation. The empty-table posture renders prominently — UNPRICED is the shipped stance, not a fault — and `deferred[]` renders as the stated decisions it is.
- **OQ7 — render-as-emitted, ratified.** The renderers rely on JSON Forms CORE's own rule evaluation and fabricate no rule, which is the emitter's own recorded reason applied one layer out: `auto`, `perUser`, `restartRequired` and `dormant` are BADGES a person reads, not visibility conditions, and the S18.2 relational clamps are validated on write rather than by hiding a control. The capability is structurally present the day the emitter has a ratified condition to emit.
- **OQ5 — the D4(b) unlock, with a REPORTED naming deviation.** One additive Layer-1 catalog query in `internal/history`, named **`verdicts.eval_scores`** rather than the dispositioned `eval.scores`: `Category` is a CLOSED, spec-named set (S14.10 ¶2) and every catalog name's prefix is its category, so inventing an `eval` category to match a name would have been the larger change. The dispositioned SHAPE is delivered exactly — `run_events WHERE type='eval.score_recorded'`, owner-scoped, newest-first by `event_seq`, riding the LANDED audited `GET /api/events/query/{query}` route with **no `internal/api` change at all**.
  - **The columns are what the producer MINTS**, and the disposition's list does not exist: there is no top-level `score`, `floor` or `pass` key. `result` is the green/red outcome, and the floor lives inside `metrics` **and only on the runbook path** — so a sweep result reports a NULL floor, which the surface renders as "not recorded on this path" rather than as a zero. Driven both ways.
  - **Owner scope:** the producer attributes these rows to `platform`, so the operator sees every row and a member sees none — the same answer the drift and canary entries give, asserted rather than assumed. **The catalog is now 50 entries, exactly at the S14.10 ¶2 band ceiling of 30–50** — the next addition needs the band revisited, which is a fact worth carrying rather than discovering.
- **A clock seam added to `internal/settings`, for the reason B6-5 added two.** `settings_events` is append-only BY TRIGGER, so a stamp cannot be normalized after the fact, and a row minted through the REAL write verb is otherwise uncommittable. `Registry.Now` is nil-defaults-to-`time.Now`; production behavior is unchanged; `index.go` stays byte-unchanged. The fixture world drives two real value writes, a real bounds edit and a real per-user write through the HTTP verbs, so the committed bodies carry a genuine `overridden`, genuine EFFECTIVE bounds that differ from the ratified clamp, and a genuine audit trail.
- **Counters at part B:** migrations **0001–0019** untouched · event inventory **91/5/96** · ⚙ **118/33**, `internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged · `components.lock` **29** · **`web/package.json` + `package-lock.json` byte-unchanged** (JSON Forms consumed as installed — wiring is consumption) · `internal/benchmark/registered.go`, `internal/eventlog/contract.go`, BENCH-REG untouched · escape-scan allowlist still **EMPTY** · history catalog **50** · vitest **209 tests / 17 files** · Go **43 packages** green, `-race` green on `./internal/api`, `./internal/settings`, `./internal/history`.
- **Still not built, named rather than implied:** the assistant (B6-7), the review surfaces and the escape-allowlist widening (B6-8), push/re-nag and the S15.12 sweep (B6-9). The member self-service question stays where B6-3 OQ8 left it — the settings-tab amendment pass — and this surface says so on screen rather than implying a self-service that does not exist.

#### §43 drain round 1 (P3-B6-6 drain r1, D1–D11) — corrections, and two ratified outside-range fixes

- **CORRECTION (D1) — the reveal was announced and then withheld.** The verdict branch printed "recorded, and revealed" and dropped the served `reveal` payload, so the voter was told the arm identities were readable and shown none of them — the one moment BENCH-REG §3.4 exists to hold back, withheld by the surface that announced it. `VerdictReveal` is now typed honestly (platform side, both observed models, the scored guess, epoch, recorded-at) and rendered; the test pins the CONTENT, not the phrase, and the late-reveal branch asserts no reveal block appears.
- **CORRECTION (D2), both halves — a blank unit price composed a silent $0 row.** `Number('')` is 0 and `Number('abc')` is NaN → null → Go's zero, so an operator who left a price field empty appended a row that PRICED its lane at nothing: every call on it charged $0 with no trace, which is exactly what the UNPRICED posture exists to bar (S10.1; P-T08-1) — and worse than no row, because no row says UNPRICED out loud. **The form** refuses to compose one (input-shape validation at the boundary), and **the store — the authority per OQ6 — refuses it too** (`validatePriceRow` now rejects non-finite, negative and zero DECLARED unit prices, and an all-zero row outright; an undeclared unit stays the zero value, because "this lane charges nothing for that unit" is expressed by omission). The store's refusal renders verbatim in the form, driven. A **SANCTIONED-NARROW** touch of `internal/metering`, ratified by the coordinator.
- **CORRECTION (D3) — history was behind the write flag.** The ratified OQ3 gives members the FULL view including history, and the server gates that read at nothing beyond the session, so hiding it was the surface inventing an authority the platform does not have. The button moved outside the `editable` gate; a member's read is driven end to end.
- **FIXED AT THE PRODUCER (D4) — a delta card was structurally unanswerable.** `internal/intake` issued `{origin, items, help}` with no action vocabulary while its answer path takes `{"action":"approve"|"reject"}` — so a pending delta could be rendered and never answered, from any surface that renders controls from the card, **while it held the task's `OpenAskID` open**. `DeltaBody` gains `Actions`, built by `DeltaActions()` from the same `ChoiceApproveDelta`/`ChoiceRejectDelta` constants `applyDeltaAnswer` validates against — one list, two readers — and `readCardShape` grows the limb that reads it, exactly as it reads the other three. Naming the verbs in `internal/api` was refused for the same reason as before: that would put the pipeline's answer vocabulary in the transport. A **SANCTIONED-NARROW** touch of `internal/intake` (the §40-B D6 precedent), ratified; the diff is the issuance site and the vocabulary and nothing else. Driven both sides: the producer test asserts the issued card carries the verbs its own answer path applies, and the inbox test answers a delta card end to end.
- **PINNED (D5) — the OQ10 constraint was vacuous.** Both batchable fixture cards shared one vocabulary, so removing the filter changed nothing observable. The fixture world gained a THIRD batchable card in the DECISION band (`ask-sweep`, trivial tier): the three now overlap on `replan` and diverge everywhere else, so `approve` offers two, `drop_criterion` offers one, and `replan` offers all three. **Probe run: with the filter removed, 3 tests fail; restored, 40 pass.**
- **ADDED (D6) — the dispositioned registered-vocabulary scan.** No view file may carry a §3.3 verdict value or a §12 disposition as a literal (split tokens, planted probe, plus an assertion that the SERVED fixture really carries them — so "no hits" means "rendered from data", not "the value never appears"). `A`/`B` are not scannable strings and are covered instead by the served-vocabulary render test, which asserts the radio set IS the served list.
- **CORRECTION (D7) — the resume path authored a story the server never told.** It discarded the served `ResumeOutcome` and printed "the flag was a false alarm", a judgement about WHY a run was released that this surface has no standing to make. It now renders the served `from → to` and the platform's own detail, like every other verb branch; the test asserts the invented sentence is gone.
- **CORRECTION (D8).** `composeAnswer`'s question-card branch sent `{force_proceed:true}` for ANY served action — answering a question the person did not answer, under a verb they did press. It now composes that body only for the action that means it, and returns null (nothing posted, honestly stated) otherwise.
- **NARROWED (D9).** The eval panel declared the whole inbox type set, subscribing a table of suite results to gate asks, drift findings and watchdog flags. It declares the one type that can move a row — `eval.score_recorded` — with its §42 reason: the same type S14.5 says is the only thing that can clear a red.
- **PINNED (D10).** `OpenConflictsFor` gained its package-local test, the ADDRESSEE WALL direction: two people who each have a real conflict (so "returns nothing" and "was never scoped" cannot look alike), the operator served zero, and a resolution taking only its own row out of the open list.
- **CORRECTION (D11).** `resolveMemoryConflict(card?.conflict_id ?? 0)` posted a zero-value guess at a row id on a malformed card. `conflictID()` returns null instead, the control does not arm, and the act path states the honest nothing-was-sent.
- **One scan tightening the drain forced:** the ⚙-key scan skips DECLARED event-type lists as a block and strips comments before matching — §42 requires every view to name the types it consumes, and a file is allowed to explain in prose the thing it never hand-places.
