#### §43 drain round 2 (P3-B6-6 drain r2, R1, FINAL under the cap) — the five-unit price form

- **CORRECTION (R1) — the same fabrication in a different syntax.** Drain r1 stopped a blank unit price from composing a $0 row, and then the form offered only THREE of the five unit classes. `EffectiveDatedTable.Price` folds every unit unconditionally with no per-unit unpriced trace (`internal/metering/price.go:157–161`), and `normalize` feeds `CacheCreationTokens` and `ServerToolCalls` from real provider blocks (`metering.go:147/151) — so every UI-added row silently priced genuine cache-creation and server-tool usage at a traceless $0 **inside a cost the receipt calls PRICED**. Worse than an empty table, which at least says UNPRICED out loud.
- **The ruling was FORM-ONLY and is applied as such.** The store's semantics are byte-unchanged from drain r1; the float64 free-vs-unknown limitation is a gate-list shape question and was not touched.
  - The form offers **all five** units (`unitFields`, `web/src/Settings.tsx`), and the list is **pinned to the SERVED row's own `unit_prices` keys** by a golden-fixture test — so it cannot drift from the shape the store accepts (OQ6).
  - Each unit is answered EITHER with a finite positive price OR with an explicit **"not charged on this lane"** act. The operator states the omission; the form never defaults it, and a unit left blank and undeclared keeps the append disabled with its own marker.
  - A declared-free unit is **OMITTED** from the composed row rather than sent as a zero, and the append summary names exactly which units are priced and which were declared free — nothing composes silently.
  - **All-declared-free stays permitted by the form and REFUSED BY THE STORE** on its own all-zero wall, rendering verbatim. That is deliberate: it is a thing an operator can coherently mean, and the authority stays where OQ6 put it.
- **The metering-side assert is the non-tautological half.** A UI-shaped row with all five units drives real cache-creation tokens and server-tool calls through the real pricing path, term by term; then the SAME usage runs against a three-unit row, which still prices — the row exists, so the lane is "priced" — and the two undeclared classes contribute exactly nothing, with the gap asserted to equal what those units were worth. Compared with a tolerance rather than for equality, because the two sums add the same terms in different orders and IEEE-754 addition is not associative — a fact about float64, not about the pricing.
- **Counters after drain r2:** migrations **0001–0019** untouched · inventory **91/5/96** · ⚙ **118/33** byte-unchanged · `components.lock` **29** · `package.json`/`package-lock.json` byte-unchanged · history catalog **50** · escape-scan allowlist **EMPTY** · vitest **217 tests / 17 files** · Go **43 packages** green, `-race` green on `./internal/metering` + `./internal/api`.

## 44. The S15.7 conversational assistant — backend (P3-B6-7 part A; S15.7, S15.2, S14.10, S12.4, S06.1/S06.3, S11.7, S01.9)

The **first of two parts** of B6-7 (the A/B split was pre-authorized in the queue row). Part A is the whole server side: the per-user session registry, the immutable transcript, the turn lifecycle, the JARVIS file exchange, the first-message auto-titling duty, and the S06 intake handoff. **Part B — the `/chat` widget on @assistant-ui/react — is a LATER packet; `/chat` is still the stub, the route's `owner` is unchanged and nothing is half-wired.** Part A opens **migration 0020** and registers **eight** new event types.

- **The S15.2 family table has no chat row, and the gap is CLOSED additively rather than papered over (OQ1(a), ratified).** S15.7 demands a "server-side per-user session registry, fail-closed" and names no root, no owner column and no storage. The resolution is a new resource family under `/api/chat` with a new leaf package `internal/chat` over storage+eventlog, held directly in `api.Config` (the `*review.Store` / `*memory.Store` reason, §39/§40-C). **S15.7 is the owning section; the family exists under S15.2's contract RULES** — owner-scoped server-side, every mutation on the event log, additive-first, no outward-effect verb — and a cosmetic table amendment naming the row is carried to the gate list, not taken here (§8: the executor never edits `Spec/`).
- **Message bodies are CONTENT, so they are table-backed; events mint LIFECYCLE FACTS, refs-not-blobs.** The rejected alternative was pure derive-from-log, which would have put transcripts in event payloads (against S02.2) and made every reload a log scan. `chat_messages` joins `asks.snapshot`, the S09 knowledge rows and the S13 revisions as stored content. Every `chat.*` payload carries ids, counts, closed vocabularies and a `text_len` — never a body, a title or an answer. A scan over the minted rows asserts it, and fails loudly if it read nothing.
- **FOUR tables, and the messages/turns split is load-bearing.** `chat_sessions` (owner-attributed, hard-deletable), `chat_messages` (INSERT-ONLY, `chat_messages_immutable` trigger — a record of speech that can be rewritten is not a record), `chat_turns` (a MUTATING lifecycle row: running → settled | abandoned), `chat_exchange_files` (the manifest, `UPDATE`-immutable — a stored byte string does not change its size or its hash). A turn is 1:1 with the message that opened it and was deliberately NOT folded into it: folding would force the immutability trigger to be column-selective, and the copy of a guard that has to know which column it protects is the copy that rots. **Immutable means never EDITED, not never removed**: the owner's hard delete takes the whole thread, and `chat.session_deleted` — carrying counts, not content — is the audit that it went.
- **VISIBILITY IS OWNER-ONLY, AND IT IS A STATED DEVIATION (OQ1, ratified).** The operator does not read another member's transcripts. This is the §40-C content-vs-telemetry line extended: §30's operator-sees-all is an OBSERVABILITY rule about runs and telemetry, and a conversation is personal content. It is **structural rather than policed** — every read in `internal/chat` takes a VIEWER and has no role parameter at all, so there is no operator limb to forget to omit — and both directions are pinned at the store and over HTTP: alice's own rows must be PRESENT (an all-empty handler cannot pass), bob and the operator get 404 on every read AND every mutation. `ErrNotFound` deliberately covers "not yours" as well as "not there", because an id that answers differently for a stranger is an existence oracle for someone else's conversation.
- **THE CLIENT ROUTES; THE SERVER ACTS (OQ3(a)).** A turn arrives carrying the verb the person's gesture chose, from the closed set `ask|view|query|open_sql|task` (a CHECK in 0020 and a Go gate). **No model classifies a turn** — a misfiring classifier could start a task nobody asked for or escalate to open SQL nobody escalated, and neither is a failure a person sees coming. Typed prose with no gesture DEFAULTS to `ask`, the Layer-1 floor, whose own disambiguation card **is** the honest refusal when a turn is none of the three S15.7 verbs — driven, with its choices asserted to be real catalog queries. The server then PERFORMS the act and records the outcome, which is what makes the transcript served state rather than something a browser assembled and might lose.
- **Nothing falls through to Layer 2, and the measurement is the audit count.** `chatapi.go` calls the LANDED `s.history` verbs and builds no SQL, derives no confidence and re-implements no guardrail — asserted by a comment-stripped source scan with the calls themselves as the non-tautological control. Four non-escalated turns (including an unresolved `ask` and an unknown view name) leave `history.query_audited` at **zero**; naming `open_sql` explicitly produces exactly **one** audit row, and its answer carries `lower-confidence` VERBATIM with its audit block at 200 (G3 D3.5). A populated answer arriving alongside an error is recorded as the ANSWER with the error folded into its notes — `writeAnswer`'s rule, for `writeAnswer`'s reason: the audit block is the artifact the guardrail exists to produce.
- **Auto-titling rides the EXISTING `utility` seat (OQ2(a)+(i)) and degrades mechanically.** S12.4's utility row already covers "summaries … drafting", its outputs are human-gated and "this seat never decides" — which is exactly an editable label. **No alias row is invented** (⚙ `local.alias` still holds exactly ten; `index.go` byte-unchanged, **118/33**), and a test pins the ten. The call is free-text drafting: `Classification:false`, no schema, no logprobs, **no margin gate** — `utility` is honestly uncalibrated by design, and a gate over an uncalibrated seat is a number pretending to mean something. It rides a short-lived platform advisory run through `chatAdvisory` (the history-intent / contradiction-screen / narrator precedent) and writes ONE $0 D7 checkpoint, asserted. Every degradation — absent stack, stopped admissions, non-servable seat, refusal, blank answer — lands the same deterministic fallback: a truncation of the person's OWN first message, `title_source='mechanical'`. That is a derivation of something they wrote, never a fabricated model output, and **nothing can fail a turn**: the caller discards the outcome. A human rename stamps `title_source='human'` and the duty's own `UPDATE` still requires the source to be unset, so a rename that commits while the duty is thinking wins the race.
- **The exchange: the manifest is the record, the filesystem is storage (OQ4(a)).** Bytes land at `<state>/exchange/<sha256(owner)[:32]>/<file-id>`. The owner segment is a DIGEST rather than a sanitized user id, because a sanitizer maps distinct identities onto one folder the moment two ids differ only in a character it strips — two people sharing a directory is the exact leak this family exists to prevent. The leaf is the generated id, never the uploaded name, so **a hostile filename never reaches the filesystem at all**; it is a label in the manifest. `SafeName` still refuses separators, `..`, absolute shapes and control characters rather than rewriting them, because a name quietly mangled into safety tells a person their file is called something it is not. **And the path builder resolves before it trusts** (S11.7): `objectPath` runs `EvalSymlinks` on the root and the OWNER DIRECTORY — the directory, because on an upload the leaf does not exist yet and a check that only runs on reads is not a check — and refuses anything landing outside. Driven three ways: parent references and absolute paths refused as names with nothing written, and a symlink planted where the store will look for the owner directory refused with nothing written through it. `TestNoSandboxMountsTheExchange` scans `internal/{sandbox,stage,preview,worker}` for any mention: the exchange is control-plane territory and S11.3 is allowlist-only.
- **Produced-files chips are a DIFF over stored rows, both halves (OQ7(a)).** The window half is computed once at settle — rows in `[started_ts, settled_ts]` excluding anything already attributed to a different turn — and stored on the turn. The late-arrival half is a read-time union over rows whose `origin_turn` names it, because a file produced after a turn settled still belongs to the turn that caused it and the manifest is where it says so; guessing from a timestamp is how a chip lands on the wrong turn. **No ticker and no watcher** — `TestNoTickerOrWatcherInThisPackage` scans for both with a read-something guard (§32). At v0 **only uploads write the folder**: the platform-side producer socket is NAMED, NOT BUILT, so chips are honestly sparse and the empty case is driven. A cross-owner origin ref is refused.
- **The turn transport is synchronous, and "in-flight" is a ROW (OQ5(a)).** A ladder answer arrives whole and no engine session runs for a chat turn (S12.1), so there is nothing to stream; a second stream shape would buy nothing and cost the one-EventSource rule. The POST returns the outcome when ready, and the minted `chat.*` events make other tabs current on the landed relay — **no `topics.go` change**, since a type in no topic still flows on the unfiltered relay and the client listens by TYPE. Survival across navigation is therefore server state: `RunningTurn` re-reads the in-flight turn, and the session detail serves it as `running`. One turn at a time per session, because two turns racing to settle would overlap their produced-files windows with no honest attribution.
- **Hard-stop is an ACT with a record, and it claims no lifecycle authority (OQ6(a)).** `AbandonTurn` marks the turn abandoned, mints `chat.turn_abandoned`, and cancels the driving request's context through an in-process registry — the `internal/stage` cancel registry in miniature (§39): in-process only, released on return, never the record of anything. **A completed-anyway result does not overwrite the abandoned row**: the settle `UPDATE` carries `state = 'running'` in its predicate, so the race is decided by the database and the record still says the person stopped. Driven, including the zero `chat.turn_settled` rows afterwards. A handed-off TASK is never cancelled — 4.5's cancel stays the task's own verb, asserted by zero `run.state_changed` rows from a stop.
- **The intake handoff is additive and cross-owner-refused (OQ8(a)+(i)).** `submitBody` and `intake.Request` gain `inputs []intake.Input` — a DESCRIPTOR (`ref`, `name`, `size_bytes`, `sha256`), never bytes and never a mount. `State` marshals its `Request` whole onto every `intake.state` event, so recording them on the Request IS recording them on the intake record (S06.3/S06.6) with no schema change. Refs are resolved against the CALLER's own manifest before Submit is reached, so a ref to another person's object is simply not found — the refusal is the same predicate that scopes every other read here, not a check somebody has to remember. Driven end to end: upload → hand → the descriptor on the body, and a cross-owner ref never reaches `Submit` (call-count asserted). The born task's OPEN INTAKE CARD rides the turn's outcome so the feed renders it in place, answered through the LANDED ask verbs — same vocabulary, no second answer path.
- **§29 — EIGHT new types, and the family fit was argued, not assumed.** `chat.session_created/renamed/deleted`, `chat.turn_started/settled/abandoned`, `chat.file_uploaded/deleted` — every mutation the family has, so "every mutation lands on the event log" is complete rather than sampled. All eight **ADMIT into family 12 (Platform)** under the same OQ2-(A) broadened reading that homes `worker.*`, `registry.*`, `preview.*`, `local.*` and `history.query_audited`: a chat session is an owner-attributed control-plane ASSET, a turn is a surface auditing itself, and none of it is a RUN event (a turn has no run, no objective and no receipt — the advisory run its titling duty meters against is the metering run, not the turn's). **No new family, so no S00.9 amendment.** `chat.turn_settled` is not a double-mint: a Layer-2 escalation's audit is `history.query_audited` (what the guardrail did), a handoff's task birth is `intake.state` (the task's fact), and Layers 0/1 mint nothing. Inventory **91/5/96 → 99 minted / 5 declare-only / 104 registered**, with `producerTypes()`, both `TestInventoryTotals` pins, the required-field goldens and the THIRD registry-size pin (`internal/history/importwall_test.go`, the §40 B6-3A precedent — one counter line, not a semantic touch) all moved in the same commit.
- **Migration 0020 and the FULL lockstep pin sweep.** Six sites, exactly as B6-3A recorded: four exact `user_version` pins (`internal/evals`, `internal/conformance`, `internal/api/meters_verbs_test.go`, `internal/api/benchmark_test.go`) and two filename sentinels (`benchmark_test.go`'s `> "0020_zzz"` glob and `internal/preview/conformance_test.go`'s never-adds-a-migration guard at `>= "0021"`). 0001–0019 stay byte-untouched.
- **Redaction: the edge widens to FOUR by enumeration, and the content line is unmoved.** `redactionEdgeFiles` gains `chatapi.go`, which redacts a turn's stored OUTCOME per serialization — for a query-layer turn that is a `history.Answer` whose rows are lifted out of `run_events`, a PROJECTION. The message bodies and session titles in the same response are chat's own CONTENT, read from chat's own tables, and go out unwrapped (§38 ruling (b); §40-C). Both halves are driven in ONE response by a planted secret: the answer redacts, the person's own words survive verbatim, and the stored row is untouched.
- **OQ9 — the N13 CARRY IS CLOSED, and the reading is (a): the GET wire shape STANDS.** All seven history routes keep `?q=`. The question is not a secret-class field, the surface is loopback-only behind the S01.4 front chain, every answer is served `no-store`, and **the audit row is already the server-side record of what was asked** — `history.query_audited` stores the question by design, so a POST would move the question out of one log and leave it in another. Adding POST siblings would have cost route duplication and fixture churn to buy nothing. The chat turn verb is a POST for its own reason — it MUTATES (it writes a message, opens a turn and records an outcome) — and it consumes the GET-shaped layers unchanged.
- **New bounds are structural constants with named reasons (no new ⚙).** `SessionListCap` 200 · `TranscriptCap` 500 · `MaxMessageRunes` 8000 (a message is speech; the exchange is where files go) · `MaxTitleRunes` 80 · `MaxFileBytes` 8 MiB · `MaxFilesPerOwner` 200 (retention is keep-until-deleted with NO sweeper per OQ4, so a bound that refuses loudly is the only thing between the folder and the disk — the retention-policy decision is a gate-list note) · `MaxFileNameRunes` 200 · `FileListCap` 256 (deliberately ABOVE the per-owner bound, so the list can always show the whole folder) · `titleMaxTokens` 48 · `maxChatUpload` = `MaxFileBytes` + 1 KiB (so an at-the-limit file is refused by the store with its own reason rather than by the transport with a vaguer one). Interim under the standing settings-tab directive.
- **`NewID` joins `Now` as a test seam, for the same reason.** Ids here are minted rather than caller-supplied, so a golden fixture produced through the REAL verbs would carry a fresh random string every run — and a fixture that churns is a fixture nobody reviews. nil is the crypto/rand generator, so production is unchanged and no caller can choose an id (which matters: an exchange file id is also its filesystem name).
- **Fixtures are produced by REAL VERBS through the REAL handlers.** `driveFixtureChat` creates a session, uploads a file, and posts three turns through the transport — a Layer-0 deterministic answer, the disambiguation card, and the S06 handoff with its open intake card — then leaves a FOURTH turn running, because "the turn survives navigation" is a render over served state and a render with no committed body is a render nobody drove. A second, empty session commits the honest untitled state. Three new fixtures (`chat-sessions`, `chat-session`, `chat-files`), all read as alice: **there is no operator body to commit, and that absence is itself the disposition.** The shared world's `cursor` moves 31 → 42 in the thirteen bodies that carry it, exactly as B6-5 (18 → 25) and B6-6 (25 → 31) moved it — the world grew by eleven events.
- **OQ10 — plain text at v0, so no dependency and no allowlist motion.** Answers are tables, cards and labels — structured, not prose — and escaped text renders them faithfully. `components.lock` stays **29**, `package.json`/`package-lock.json` are byte-unchanged, the escape allowlist stays EMPTY, and `web/` is byte-unchanged apart from the three new fixtures.
- **No new things.** NO new ⚙ key (`internal/settings/index.go` **byte-unchanged, 118/33**), NO new dependency (`components.lock` **29**, `go.mod`/`go.sum` unchanged), conformance rows **12**, and `internal/history`, `internal/local`, `internal/memory`, `internal/review`, `internal/redact`, `internal/settings` are untouched (`internal/history` gains ONE counter line in a test, declared above). `internal/intake` and `internal/stage` change only by the OQ8 additive field. **$0** — every test is tier-F and hermetic: the titling battery rides `local.FakeServer`, the query surface is composed with no duty caller, and nothing dials a network, spawns a process or prices a call. `Spec/`, `Docs/`, `Research/` and `P3/STATE.md` untouched; nothing pushed.

### §44 drain round 1 (P3-B6-7 part A drain r1, D1–D9) — corrections and readings

Nine evaluator findings actioned; F10/F11 were dropped by the coordinator and are not recorded here. Every item carries a test that FAILS on the pre-drain code, each verified by reverting the fix and watching the assertion fire.

- **D1 — a guard that guarded nothing.** The §30 redaction probe asserted `Contains(detail…) && Contains(got…)`: it could only fire when BOTH serving edges leaked, so deleting `serveTurns` from `chatSessionView` left it green while `GET /api/chat/sessions/{id}` served a planted `sk-ant-…` verbatim inside its answer rows. **Each edge is now asserted on its own**, plus a third assertion that the outcome under test is a POPULATED answer — otherwise an error outcome would pass the probe on emptiness rather than on redaction. Revert-probed separately at both edges.
- **D2 — CORRECTION: the record of a turn's ending now outlives the request.** The settle rode `r.Context()`. Chat is phone-complete (S1.10), so a dropped connection is the ORDINARY case, and a cancelled request made `SettleTurn` error: the turn stayed `running`, **zero** `chat.turn_settled` rows were minted, and every later turn in that session was refused `ErrTurnRunning` until a manual hard-stop — one dropped connection wedged the conversation. The settle and the response's session re-read now ride `context.WithoutCancel(r.Context())`, the **landed §37-C shape** (`internal/history/layer2.go`'s audit append, `internal/stage/stageevents.go`'s session-end append), not a new one. **The ACT still rides the cancellable context** — that is what the hard-stop verb cancels, and OQ6 is unchanged. Driven with an intake seam that drops the connection mid-act; on the pre-drain code the turn is left `running` with 0 settle rows.
- **D3 — CORRECTION to the produced-files window: it is a SEQUENCE comparison, not `[started_ts, settled_ts]`.** The old window was inclusive on both ends over `time.RFC3339` **second-resolution** stamps, so any upload landing in the same second as a turn's start was attributed to it — and in the fixed-clock fixture world, where every row shares one stamp, `cf-0000000000000002` was uploaded BEFORE turn 1 opened and appeared in `produced` on all three settled turns. That is fabricated authorship, against §44's own "chips are honestly sparse" and against honest-absence-over-fabrication.
  - **Migration 0020 is EDITED, not superseded** (0001–0019 stay byte-untouched, `user_version` stays **20**, all six lockstep pin sites unmoved): `chat_exchange_files` gains `seq INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT` with `file_id` as a UNIQUE column, and `chat_turns` gains `exchange_seq`. AUTOINCREMENT rather than the implicit rowid because these rows ARE deleted and a reused rowid could place a new object before a turn that started after it.
  - `BeginTurn` reads the watermark `MAX(seq)` **inside the transaction that opens the turn**, so no upload can slip between the reading and the recording. `producedSince` is `seq > exchange_seq`, STRICTLY greater; the upper bound is the CALL SITE (it runs once, inside `SettleTurn`), so no timestamp appears in the predicate at all. The origin-ref half is untouched.
  - `TestProducedFilesDiffIsAWindowAndAnOriginRef` now **freezes the clock** — which is the fixture world's own condition, not a contrivance — and uploads an object BEFORE any turn opens. A window that attributes correctly when every row shares one stamp is a window no timestamp collision can fool.
  - `chat-session.json` was regenerated through `SINET_WRITE_API_FIXTURES`. **At `e6850e1` it carried no `produced` list on any turn** — the honest v0 state (OQ7: only uploads write the folder, the platform-side producer socket is named-not-built) — which left part B with only the EMPTY chip render committed. **SUPERSEDED BY D10 BELOW:** the fixture now drives BOTH renders, one settled turn with files and the others without. Read the D10 bullet for the current state.
- **D4 — the operator limb is no longer vacuous.** `newBackend` opens an EMPTY auth store, so `isOperatorRead("op")` was false and `"op"` was a second member wearing the deviation's label. `newChatEnv` now registers **real** `role=operator` and `role=member` users, and the non-tautology lever is the ladder itself: `task_project` is the one Layer-0 view with no owner column, hence operator-only (S01.9), so the same transport that refuses op alice's thread **answers his own operator-scoped question and refuses bob's**. Both directions pinned in one test. The store-side half is structural rather than behavioural — there is no auth in `internal/chat` — so `TestStoreHasNoOperatorLimbAtAll` parses the package and asserts no exported `*Store` method takes a bool or a parameter named for a role/scope/operator: `"op"` there is a viewer because there is nothing else it could be.
- **D5 — the handoff fixture is tied to the REAL `taskView`.** The hand-built body emitted `{run_id, state, role}` and omitted `clearance`; production's `runSummary` is `{run_id, role, state, has_receipt}` with `has_receipt` **not** omitempty, and `taskView` carries the clearance the born card itself declares. `internal/api` cannot import the pipeline's view type (the IntakeSurface seam exists so it never speaks that vocabulary), so `TestFixtureHandoffMatchesTheRealTaskView` reads the shape out of `internal/stage/surface.go` where it is WRITTEN — every emitted key must exist on the real struct, every non-omitempty field must be present, nested runs checked the same way, and the view's clearance must equal its own open card's. A rename or a dropped field in surface.go now fails here instead of teaching part B a payload that does not exist (the B6-5 root cause).
- **D6 — upload is retry-safe, in the landed `applied:false` vocabulary.** `Upload` returns `UploadResult{File, Applied}`; an identical **(owner, name, sha256)** re-upload answers the already-resolved existing row — no second manifest row, no second quota slot, no second `chat.file_uploaded`, no second object on disk. The dedupe runs BEFORE the count bound, deliberately: a repeat is not a new object, so it must not be refused by a bound it does not consume. Driven with all three limbs of the triple as non-tautological controls (different bytes, different name, different owner each mint a NEW row — dedupe never crosses the household).
- **D7 — `MaxFilesPerOwner` is driven.** 200 uploads through the real verb, the 201st refused with `ErrTooMany` and a message naming its bound, nothing written; and bob's first upload still lands, because the bound is per owner.
- **D8 — resolve-then-deny reaches the LEAF.** `os.WriteFile` follows a symlink standing at the destination, so the directory-only resolution left the leaf open; the write path is now `os.OpenFile(…, O_WRONLY|O_CREATE|O_EXCL, 0o600)`, which POSIX refuses on an existing path including a dangling link. The existing directory resolve-then-deny is unchanged. Driven through the `NewID` seam — the same seam that makes fixtures reproducible is what makes a chosen id plantable — with the link removed afterwards as the control.
- **D9 — the sandbox-mount wall walks the whole tree.** The old scan read four directories non-recursively for the literal `"exchange"`, missing `internal/adapters` (the only package populating `sandbox.RWExchange`) and every subpackage. It now walks **all of `internal/` recursively**, keyed on the chat exchange home's own markers — `internal/chat"`, `chat.Config{`, and the `<stateDir>/exchange` join — with a read-something guard and the allowed callers enumerated: `chat` (owns it), `api` (serves it), `shell` (composes it), `eventlog` (the S14.2 declare-once registry names every minting package by construction, and mounts nothing). **`sandbox.RWExchange` is a DIFFERENT concept** — the engine's copy-aside dir, the S03.4 gate ctl dir — and `TestSandboxRWExchangeIsADifferentConcept` says so as an assertion rather than a comment: the adapter must still populate it and must carry none of the chat markers. Probe: a marker planted in `internal/adapters/adapters.go`, which the old scan could not reach, is caught.

- **D10 (round-1 addendum, coordinator-directed) — the NON-EMPTY produced render is now driven too.** D3 correctly removed a fabricated attribution, and the honest fixture that followed left part B with only the empty chip render committed. OQ7 records that uploads are the only v0 producer, so a file uploaded *while a turn is in flight* legitimately belongs to that turn — a real world, not a staged one. `driveFixtureChat` now lands a real upload through the real handler strictly between the handoff turn's `BeginTurn` and its `SettleTurn`, and the window diff attributes it. **The turn verb is synchronous**, so the only honest way a single-threaded fixture can interleave a second request is from inside the seam the turn is blocked on (`fixtureIntake.during`) — the interleaving is what is arranged, never the row. Turns 1 and 2 stay EMPTY: sparse chips are the v0 truth and papering over them would undo D3. `TestChatFixtureDrivesBothProducedRenders` pins the property the compare-only fixture cannot state — exactly one settled turn with files, at least one without, and every chip naming a file the served list actually carries. The world grew by one event, so `cursor` moves **43** in the thirteen bodies that carry it.
- **Migration 0020's in-place edit is RATIFIED, and the reason is durable:** no production DB has ever seen 0020 (it was authored the same day; the installed binary predates it), only per-run test DBs — and **B6-9's ratified disposition reserves migration 0021 for `push_subscriptions`**, so a superseding migration here would have taken that number and forced B6-9 to renumber and move the preview-guard sentinel again.

### §44 drain round 2 (P3-B6-7 part A drain r2, D11–D12 — FINAL round under the cap)

- **D11 — CORRECTION: turn windows can OVERLAP, and an ambiguous file attributes to NO turn.** `ErrTurnRunning` is enforced per SESSION, so one owner can hold running turns in two conversations at once; both recorded the same watermark and both claimed the same upload — the same file printed as produced by two different turns. That is the residual tail of the fabricated-authorship defect D3 closed, and `turns.go`'s own comment already forbade it ("two turns racing to settle would give their produced-files windows an overlap no attribution could honestly resolve"); the guard just did not reach across sessions.
  - **The rule is OQ7 as ratified, not a new invention:** the origin ref decides, and where none resolves it the file attributes to **no turn**. Honest absence over fabrication.
  - **`ErrTurnRunning` is deliberately NOT widened to per-owner.** A person with two conversations open should be able to run a turn in each, and part B renders sessions as switchable — a cross-session lock would be a real usability constraint bought to paper over a question OQ7 already answers.
  - **A turn's window now has a CLOSE as well as an open.** Migration 0020 (still edited in place; 0001–0019 byte-untouched, `user_version` **20**, the six pin sites unmoved) gives `chat_turns` a `settled_exchange_seq` beside `exchange_seq`, written by BOTH `SettleTurn` and `AbandonTurn` — an abandoned turn claims nothing but its window was still open while files arrived, and a window left open forever would silently disqualify everything after it. A file with no origin ref is claimed only when no OTHER turn of the same owner had a window containing it: still running, or closed over `(exchange_seq, settled_exchange_seq]`. Both overlapping turns therefore abstain, symmetrically, whichever settles first.
  - **The diff now runs INSIDE the settle transaction**, through the tx rather than the store (`producedSince` takes a `rowQuerier`), so the window closes and is measured atomically — and so a second reader is never opened underneath an open write transaction on the control plane's ONE connection (S02.1).
  - Driven: two sessions of one owner, two concurrent turns, one upload in the overlap — claimed by neither — plus an origin-ref upload that lands on its named turn and only there, plus a third non-overlapping turn that still attributes normally (the control, without which the rule could pass by attributing nothing ever). Probe: the r1b window claims the same file on both turns.
- **D12 — the D3 bullet above now says out loud that D10 supersedes its fixture claim.** A drain log is chronological, but §44 has to read truthfully standing alone: the sentence stating that no committed body carries a non-empty produced chip was true at `e6850e1` and false at HEAD.

### §44 post-cap coordinator items (P3-B6-7 part A, N3–N5 — implemented inline; the drain cap was exhausted)

The round-2 re-check confirmed D11/D12 and left three residuals. The two-round cap was spent, so the coordinator implemented them inline per the runbook's post-cap rule.

- **N3 — a crash mid-turn no longer disables an owner's attribution for good.** `ErrTurnRunning`'s guard and D11's disqualifier both key on `state = 'running'`, and exactly one path reaches neither `SettleTurn` nor `AbandonTurn`: the process dying mid-turn. That row keeps `settled_exchange_seq = 0` and stays an **unconditional disqualifier for every later upload by that owner**, across all their sessions, with no sweeper, timeout or recovery anywhere in the tree. Silent, permanent, and a *widening* introduced by D11 — before it, an orphan only blocked new turns in its own session.
  - **`Store.ReconcileOrphanedTurns` closes them once at bootstrap, before the surface serves.** The invariant it rests on is already documented on `inflight`: a turn is `running` only while its driving request is in flight IN THIS PROCESS, and the cancel map is "in-process only … the RECORD is the row". So a running row observed at startup is orphaned **by definition** — no timeout, no heuristic, no staleness constant.
  - **It abandons rather than settles.** What the turn was doing when the process died is unknown, and a settled record would claim an outcome nobody observed. The record survives with its window closed — the §37-C shape, where the ending is recorded even though the act did not finish.
  - **`chat.turn_abandoned` gains `reason`** (`hard_stop` | `process_restart`) on BOTH emitters, so the payload shape stays uniform while the audit distinguishes a person's act from the platform closing a window nobody closed. The contract entry moves in lockstep with the payload and the second emitter; **no type is added, so the inventory stays 99/5/104**.
  - **HONEST LIMIT, recorded rather than papered over:** the watermark the reconciliation writes is *exact* (uploads run in the same process, so nothing enters the manifest while it is dead), but it is **not behaviourally observable, and no test claims it is** — every running turn is reconciled in one pass and any later turn starts at that same watermark, so no window can overlap an orphan's. A coordinator probe binding `0` instead left the suite green; the probe that fails is the **state flip**, which is what actually unblocks the owner. The write records the truth; the comment says which half is load-bearing.
- **N4 — the abandon-close is now guarded.** Deleting `settled_exchange_seq` from `AbandonTurn` left the entire committed suite green while reintroducing D11's defect through the *ordinary* hard-stop path. No assertion about an abandoned turn's own produced list can catch it — an abandoned turn claims nothing either way; the damage shows on the OTHER turn, which then claims the ambiguous file unopposed. `TestAbandonedTurnStillClosesItsWindow` drives exactly that overlap.
- **N5 — symmetry is driven in both settle orders.** The committed D11 test drove only A-then-B; settling first must not be evidence of having produced anything. Probe: dropping the closed-window limb of the disqualifier makes the second-settling turn claim the file.
- **Non-tautology, each proved by breaking production code:** N4 → bind `0` in `AbandonTurn`, the surviving turn claims the file. N3 → reconcile without the state flip, the control fails with "a crashed turn must not disable this owner's attribution for good". N5 → drop `f.seq <= x.settled_exchange_seq`, A claims what B's window spanned.

### §44-B — the `/chat` assistant widget (P3-B6-7 part B; S15.7, S15.4, FC-v1 §3, S14.10, S06.1, S15.11–S15.12, §41-B/§42/§43)

The second part of B6-7 wires the FC-v1 chat pick over part A's served surface. `/chat` goes live — `routes.ts`'s `owner: 'B6-7'` → `''` and `chat` into `routes.test.ts`'s `built` — and **the pattern, the title and the nav placement do not move** (§41-B fill-never-rename). The B6-8/B6-9 stubs are untouched and the shell still walks them.

- **@ASSISTANT-UI/REACT 0.14.27, RE-VERIFIED AT THE PIN BEFORE WIRING** (the adopt-rail obligation, 2026-07-30): 0.14.27 IS in the registry, **MIT**, carrying **no `deprecated` field**, and its `dist.integrity` (`sha512-zV49HS6+…bqoDQ==`) is **byte-identical to the committed `package-lock.json`** — so the tarball behind the pin is the one the lock resolved. Upstream `assistant-ui/assistant-ui` is **not archived, not disabled, pushed 2026-07-29**, still MIT. The dist-tag `latest` is **0.15.1 — exactly what B6-4 recorded**, so the deliberate lag is unchanged and the pin stands. **ALIVE — wired.** The owned-primitives fallback stays named and unused. No patch, no `patch-package`, no bump.
- **ExternalStoreRuntime, and the boundary is the whole point (OQ5(i)).** `useExternalStoreRuntime` reads `messages` straight off the served session, so **the widget holds no transcript**: a reload restores the conversation by asking for it, a second tab sees the same rows, and a later swap to owned primitives loses nothing. Driven as a REMOUNT with nothing carried across — the honest shape of a reload — asserting the transcript comes back AND that it came back by re-reading. The pure edges (`chatFeed`, `convertChatItem`, `turnRequestFor`, `argumentReady`) live in `chatRuntime.ts` and are tested directly: a pure function cannot smuggle a fetch into a render.
  - **A turn item carries NO text part.** An outcome is a table, a card, a born task or an audited refusal; flattening one into a sentence would be this surface authoring what the platform said. The user item's text IS the person's stored words, rendered through the widget's own text part.
  - **The message identity IS the platform's identity** (`message_id` / `turn_id`), so nothing is generated and the render function finds its row by the id the store minted.
  - **The upstream ThreadList adapter is NOT used**, deliberately: upstream labels it "under active development and might change without notice", and the open conversation is a URL fact the router owns. The runtime owns the THREAD (messages, running, send, cancel) — which is the boundary FC-v1 names — and the session rail is Sinet's.
  - **assistant-cloud is never wired and never called**, as two checked limbs: an import-line scan over the whole tree for the package, and an app-code scan for the cloud SEAMS the pin re-exports under its own names (`AssistantCloud`, `useCloudThreadListRuntime`, `useCloudThreadListAdapter`, `CloudFileAttachmentAdapter`) — naming one would wire the cloud without ever naming the package. Both with planted probes that RUN THE SCAN. **The zero-network limb is NOT `scriptedFetch`'s throw** — `api.ts` catches everything `fetch` raises and turns it into `Unreachable`, so an unscripted request is swallowed into a rendered failure rather than failing a test. What enforces the limb is the explicit assertion that **every recorded call is an `/api/` path**: with a non-platform `fetch` planted, that loop is the one thing that catches it (drain r1 D9).
- **THE CLIENT ROUTES, AND EVERY ROUTE IS A GESTURE (OQ3(a)).** The composer carries a verb radio over **four** kinds — `ask` (default), `view`, `query`, `task` — fed for Layer 0/1 by the SERVED registries (a hand-written list would be a second copy of registries that already exist). **`open_sql` is not one of them**: Layer 2 is reached only by pressing the escalation control rendered on an answer, and that control escalates the question the person actually asked, verbatim. A task starts only by choosing to start one. **No model classifies anything**, and the closed set is asserted from both sides (the vocabulary and the rendered radios).
  - **An unpicked argument does not arm the act.** `isSendDisabled` — the pin's own gate that blocks sending while keeping the input usable — carries it, with the reason on screen. Sending a request the server could only refuse teaches nobody anything they could not have been told first.
  - **An answer already at Layer 2 offers no further escalation**, and a disambiguation card's choices RUN as new `query` turns (recorded like any other turn), asserted to be real served catalog names.
- **THE GESTURE DIES WITH ITS TURN.** The pick resets to `ask` after every send, and the reason is the same one that keeps a model out of the routing: a `task` pick that outlived the turn it routed would start a task from the NEXT typed line, which is OQ3's misfire arriving through a sticky radio instead of a classifier. Re-picking is one click; a surprise task is not undoable. Driven.
- **`AnswerView` is reused, not re-implemented.** The history panel's renderer draws every chat answer, so the layer, the confidence, the **lower-confidence flag (G3 D3.5)**, the notes, the audit block and the pickable choices are one code path with one place to forget them. Driven in-feed for all four shapes: a Layer-0 deterministic answer (flag ABSENT — the non-tautological control), the disambiguation card, the Layer-2 `unavailable` degradation, and an audited **`refused`** outcome — each rendering as the ANSWER it is at 200, never as an error banner.
- **THE ERROR-HUMANIZATION TABLE IS ONE MODULE, KEYED ON CLASS/STATUS/CODE (`chatFacts.ts`, R15).** `describeChatFailure` branches on `Unreachable` vs `ApiError{status, code}` and **nothing else** — a source assertion proves the module never calls a matcher on `message`/`detail`, which is the §38 rule made checkable rather than remembered. Ten classes: `transport`, `signed-out`, `not-found`, `turn-running`, `too-large`, `over-quota`, `overloaded` (429 and 503), `refused`, `not-wired`, `server`. **`overloaded` is R15's letter rather than a status this surface reaches today, stated rather than implied (drain r1 D5):** the only 429 in `internal/api` is the login PIN lockout, which chat never calls, and every 503 a chat route can produce carries `not_wired` — which the table deliberately gives its OWN class, because "not wired in this process" is a different fact from "busy". The arm is forward-tolerance for the day a lane refusal or provider overload surfaces through this family. **The served `{error, detail}` is preserved verbatim in every one**, asserted; the headline is ours, the detail is the platform's.
  - **`turnFact` reads a served turn's own fields** into six facts, and `empty` is the one worth naming: a turn that SETTLED WITHOUT AN OUTCOME is a real recorded state and a different thing from a request that never landed. That is the "empty-stream vs transport-error" distinction in a surface where answers arrive whole, and both are driven as two distinct renders.
  - **The model-fallback announcement rides the SERVED notes.** A comment-stripped scan bars a client-written switch sentence (`switched to`, `falling back to`, `fell back to`, `using … instead`, `downgraded to`) with a probe, and the POSITIVE half is asserted too — the committed body's own notes render verbatim in the feed, so "no hits" means "rendered from data" rather than "the mechanism is missing".
  - **The choose and escalate controls stay armed while a turn is running**, deliberately: the act is legitimate the moment that turn settles, and the server's 409 `turn_running` is an informative refusal this table humanizes — better than a control that looks pressable and does nothing (§43's no-dead-buttons rule, answered the other way round because here the door is real and merely queued). Driven.
- **THE SURVIVES-NAVIGATION INVENTORY IS AN IN-CODE TABLE A TEST PINS (`survivesNavigation`, R14).** Ten rows, each with the reason it survives or the reason shedding it is right; a row whose reason was "we cached it" would be the side-truth this contract exists to rule out. **Everything that survives does so because it is platform state or a URL fact** — the in-flight turn, its progress, the produced-files accumulation, the transcript, which conversation is open, the tab's one EventSource. **Shed on purpose:** the composer draft, the verb pick (a routing choice belongs to the turn it routes), the file list (re-read from the manifest), and a request-level notice — the sending line and any failure line, both of which describe THIS tab's request rather than the conversation.
  - **Driven both ways.** Away and back mid-turn keeps the running turn AND its chips, with the tab's `opened` counter **still 1** across the round trip and `FakeSource.made` still one connection; and the assistant really did unmount in between (asserted, so the round trip is not vacuous). Coming back is `navigate` to the SAME URL — what a bookmark and the back button do.
  - **NAVIGATION CALLS NOTHING (OQ6).** Driven as a negative — no POST during the round trip — plus a comment-stripped tree scan for `beforeunload`, `pagehide`, `'unload'` and `setInterval`. Hard-stop is a rendered act: the in-feed **Stop this turn** button and the widget's own composer Cancel call the SAME `AbandonTurn` verb (two affordances, one act), **and each renders only where it can act** — the composer cancel is gated on a STOPPABLE turn existing rather than on the thread being "running" (drain r1 D3). The ending renders from the SERVED state the re-read returns, not from what the button believed.
- **FOUND BY PROBING THE SURFACE, and fixed: pressing Send showed NOTHING for the length of the turn.** ExternalStoreRuntime holds no optimistic copy — that is LocalRuntime's job — so with the transcript owned by the platform, a person's words left the composer and appeared nowhere until a re-read landed. On a synchronous turn verb that gap is the whole act. **Two changes, neither of them a client-side transcript:** the surface fires the POST and then **re-reads immediately**, because the server wrote the message and opened the turn BEFORE the act began, so both are already readable (best effort by design — the reliable trigger stays the `chat.turn_started` frame, and a read that raced the commit comes back unchanged); and until that read lands it renders **this tab's own truth about its own outstanding request** ("Sending — the platform is recording your message"), which is a request-level notice the survives-navigation inventory already lists as SHED. Driven: the turn is the first thing sent, the re-read follows it while the POST is still outstanding, and the notice is on screen.
- **In-flight has two honest forms, and both are driven.** A `running` row on the served session (the fixture's own fourth turn) is what a re-entered view re-attaches to; an outstanding POST is what holds a second turn back before any row comes back. **`isRunning` is the OR of the two.**
- **The exchange sidebar: two gestures, ONE verb (R13).** Drag-drop and click-browse both call `uploadChatFile`, and **the body is the object itself** — this client never decodes or re-encodes a person's file, so there is nothing in the middle to corrupt it. Driven for both gestures with the Blob and its size asserted. The list renders name, size, sha256 and stamp as served, with delete on the landed verb. **Handing files to a task lives on the COMPOSER beside the task verb**, not on the list, because the exchange is control-plane territory: a file reaches a run only as an intake input descriptor.
- **Produced-files chips render the SERVER's attribution (OQ7 render half).** A chip names the file the manifest carries; a chip whose file is gone says so rather than showing a bare id as if it were a name. **Both states are committed ground** and both are driven, including the honest-empty case on every settled turn that produced nothing — at v0 only uploads write the folder, and a blank would read as "we are still working on it".
- **The S06 intake card, answered IN PLACE (OQ8(i)).** The born task renders its facts plus the pipeline's own OPEN card as data (questions, options, kind, tier), with deep links built by `hrefFor` to the task and to the decision queue. Answering goes through the **LANDED** `POST /api/approvals/{id}/answer` with the card's own `payload_hash`, **read off `GET /api/approvals` at the moment of answering** — the read the inbox already uses. Two things follow, and both are the point: **this surface never composes a card id** (it matches the queue's `<kind>:<native-id>` on the native half and then uses the SERVED `id` for both the verb and the link), and **it never guesses a pin** — a card the queue no longer carries says so plainly and posts nothing (driven, with the call count asserted). This also fills the slot editor §43 left named-and-unbuilt for interview cards. **CORRECTED post-cap (RES-1): the queue is no longer read only as an act.** This bullet said a conversation with no born task asks the approvals surface nothing — true when the card was read off the turn's outcome, and no longer true, because reading it there meant it could never appear in production at all. The conversation now holds a live approvals read; the answer path still re-reads the pin at the moment of answering, for the race that read exists to catch.
- **Session UI (R16).** List (newest-first as served), switch, create, rename, delete — all on the landed verbs. **An untitled conversation says "Untitled — nothing said yet"** through `Absent`, never a placeholder; a `mechanical` title beside `title_seat_wired: false` says WHY it was named after the person's own first line, so nobody is left wondering. **Delete is two-pressed** — it takes a whole thread and no undo verb exists, so the second press is the one that means it (driven: the first press posts nothing).
- **The open conversation is `/chat?session=<id>`** — built from `hrefFor` plus a query parameter, the landed `/?view=…` shape (§42-B): a stable, bookmarkable, push-`navigate`-able URL that FILLS the route table rather than adding a row to it. Every rail entry is a real `<a href>`, so middle-click and bookmarking behave with no JavaScript, and the round trip through `hrefFor`/`matchRoute` is asserted.
- **Live wiring: three subscriptions, types-only, on the tab's one relay (§42).** `chatSessionEventTypes` (the three session-lifecycle types — a create adds a row, a rename changes the label, a delete takes the thread) and `chatTurnEventTypes` (the three turn types plus **`chat.file_uploaded`/`chat.file_deleted`**, which are there because a turn's chips are a manifest diff: an upload while a turn is in flight changes what that turn will list, and an `origin_turn` ref joins a turn's chips after it settled) and `chatFileEventTypes` for the sidebar. **REST is the truth, frames are the trigger** — driven, with an undeclared type asserted to reach nothing. No topic filter anywhere (scanned), no second EventSource, no ticker: the schedule spy records zero calls on a healthy feed.
- **NO NEW STRUCTURAL CONSTANT, and that is worth stating** — part A enumerated ten bounds and part B adds none: this surface has no cap, window, timeout or ladder of its own. `composerVerbs` is a closed VOCABULARY (part A's five kinds minus the one that must never be a fallback), not a bound.
- **Fixture ground, and the ONE derived-body path with its reason.** Both new bodies are produced BY THE REAL VERBS through the real handlers:
  - **A fifth turn — the Layer-2 escalation** (`{"kind":"open_sql"}` through the transport). The fixture world composes `internal/history` with no read-only handle and no duty caller, so the honest outcome is `unavailable` at layer 2 with `lower-confidence`, its audit block and its served note — which is exactly a **ladder degradation**, one of the two facts part B must render as the answer it is. The world grew by three events (`chat.turn_started`, `history.query_audited`, `chat.turn_settled`), so the shared `cursor` moves **43 → 46** in the thirteen bodies that carry it, in lockstep (the two chat bodies changed for their own reasons and carry no cursor).
  - **`chat-session-empty`, the SECOND session's detail.** Not a spare: **one turn at a time is a per-SESSION rule**, so the rich body — which deliberately holds a RUNNING turn — is a conversation the composer is RIGHT to refuse a second turn into. That refusal looked like a harness problem and was not, which is why it is recorded here: a driven send belongs in the empty session, and the empty body is also the committed ground for the nothing-said-yet render.
  - **Derived bodies are used for exactly three renders, each stated at the assertion**: an audited `refused` outcome (the other guardrail outcome needs a live local tier no hermetic world has), a settled turn with its outcome removed (the empty-yield fact), and a failed turn's `{error, detail}`. Each derives from a REAL served turn and changes only the recorded outcome — the `mission.test.tsx:215` precedent.
  - **The approvals row for the born task's card comes from the REAL PROJECTOR.** Drain r1 D2 below RETRACTS the false justification this bullet originally carried and records what replaced it.
- **`scriptedFetch` now records a non-JSON body as the VALUE SENT** rather than throwing on it. The exchange upload is the one verb whose body is not JSON, and asserting on the real Blob is better evidence than a string serialized to keep a test helper happy. Every existing suite sees identical parsed JSON.
- **`Element.prototype.scrollTo` is stubbed in the test setup — the SECOND instance of the `ResizeObserver` class, and recorded as such.** jsdom implements scrolling on `window` and not on elements because there is nothing to scroll. The pin's thread viewport calls `div.scrollTo` from a `requestAnimationFrame`, so without the stub a mounted transcript throws from a frame callback AFTER the assertions passed — an unhandled error rather than a failure, which is worse than either. Scroll position is a real-browser concern and belongs to B6-9's drill.
- **The escape-scan floor moves 35 → 40 and names `./Chat.tsx`, `./chatRuntime.ts`, `./chatFacts.ts`.** A transcript is the one surface whose content is literally what somebody typed, and a chat widget is the first place a markdown renderer gets reached for. **OQ10 stands: plain escaped text, zero new dependency, and the ALLOWLIST STAYS EMPTY** (this text said it would widen at B6-8; **it did not — see the §41-B correction dated 2026-07-30**). Driven with a planted hostile message body: it renders as text and neither planted tag becomes an element.
- **REPORT — wiring the pick moved the bundle, measured rather than guessed:** `586.87 kB` (gzip `179.00`) → `851.19 kB` (gzip `255.88`) at the drain, each figure reproduced across two builds with an identical asset hash (the pre-drain entry mis-transcribed its own measurement — drain r1 D6). Vite's 500 kB chunk warning **already fired before this packet** and still does; nothing is code-split, because the SPA is embedded in the binary and served over a tailnet where a one-time 256 kB transfer is not a cost worth an architecture for. Recorded so the number is a known fact rather than a surprise at the B6 gate.
- **Counters at part B:** migrations **0001–0020** (`user_version` **20**, none opened — **0021 stays RESERVED for B6-9**) · event inventory **99/5/104** with `internal/eventlog/contract.go` byte-unchanged (no type minted) · ⚙ **118/33** with `internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged (no key added, no new read site) · `components.lock` **29** / **240** npm · **`web/package.json` + `package-lock.json` byte-unchanged** (the pick is consumed as installed — wiring is consumption) · conformance **12** · Layer-0 views **11**, catalog **50** · `internal/history`, `internal/local`, `internal/chat`, `internal/intake`, `internal/stage`, `go.mod`/`go.sum` byte-unchanged (the only Go edit is `apifixtures_test.go`) · escape-scan allowlist **EMPTY** · **44 Go packages** green · web **260 vitest / 18 files** (was 217/17) + typecheck + build · `Spec/`, `Docs/`, `Research/`, `P3/STATE.md` untouched. **$0**, hermetic, nothing pushed.

#### §44-B drain round 1 (P3-B6-7 part B drain r1, D1–D10) — corrections, one retraction, and a landed-machinery observation

Ten evaluator findings, all actioned. Every fix carries a test that FAILS on the pre-drain code, each verified by reverting the fix and watching the assertion fire.

- **D1 — CORRECTION: the survives-navigation inventory was substantially UNFALSIFIABLE, which is the one thing R14 asked it not to be.** The test checked that six named rows were `true`, that at least one row was shed, and that each reason was longer than 20 characters — so flipping `'the uploaded-file list'` to `survives: true` with the reason `'we cached it in a module-scope variable'` (the exact side-truth the module's own comment says the contract rules out) left the whole suite green. **Two changes carry it now:** the test pins the **EXACT PARTITION** — all ten rows, verdict by verdict, in order — so flipping, adding, removing or renaming ANY row fails; and the survive half is **DRIVEN BEHAVIOURALLY FOR ALL SIX** rows in one round trip (3 of 6 were driven before): the turn's node AND its recorded `running` state with the stop act still offered, the chip count, every message body back on screen with the turn count matching, the URL and the rail's active marker, and the `opened` counter still 1. The shed half is driven too — a half-typed draft is gone after the round trip. **The prose reasons are deliberately NOT machine-checked**, and the test says so: no honest scan can tell a real reason from a plausible sentence, so the partition and the drives are what carry. Probe: the evaluator's own flip now fails on the partition line.
- **D2 — RETRACTION, and the row now comes from the REAL PROJECTOR.** The recorded justification for hand-building the approvals row was **false**: `internal/api`'s only reverse import wall names `internal/benchmark`, and an external `package api_test` may legally import `internal/stage` even though `stage` imports `api`. Worse, the invented row drifted from what the server emits — `payload_hash: 'sha256:the-card-own-pin'` where the projector emits a bare 64-hex `gates.CanonicalHash`, and `actions: ['proceed']` where an interview card derives **no** action vocabulary at all, plus no tier, expiry or staleness. That is the B6-5 root cause in miniature, inside the packet that quotes it.
  - **The fix is a REAL `asks` row** for the born card, seeded with the SAME `fixtureChatBornCard` value the handoff view carries — one card, two renderings, so the queue and the feed cannot drift. `GET /api/approvals` now serves it through the same projection production uses, and the widget test reads the pin and the id straight off the committed `approvals-mine.json`. It asserts the pin is 64 hex, so the pre-drain literal fails.
  - **`asks.run_id` is a FOREIGN KEY**, so the born task and its intake run are in the world as rows too (`t-chatborn` in `intake`, `t-chatborn.intake` owned by alice). That is the honest direction rather than a cost: the chat fixture already claimed this task existed. **They are SEEDED rows, not produced ones** — this world's intake seam is the `fixtureIntake` double, so no handoff births anything here — and they go in exactly as the world's other eight tasks and runs do. The world grew by one task, one run and one ask; **no event is minted by a seeded row, so the cursor does not move.** **Drain r2 R1 corrected two things this bullet originally stated wrongly**: the run's state, which read `running` — a state a task holding an open interview card cannot be in — and the seeding POSITION, which was top-of-world and therefore put the born run inside the transcript's own first answer. See r2 below.
  - **Two landed assertions were moved to name the new row** — mission control's running bucket and the `running` personal filter. **Drain r2 R2/R3 RESTORED both to their original text**: with the run correctly `parked` those two reads are back to the world they had, and the born run appears instead in the waiting-on-a-human bucket, which is where a task waiting on a person belongs. The §43 precedent invoked here ("the operator's queue grew") does hold for the approvals bodies, which grew and still carry the row.
- **D3 — CORRECTION: the composer cancel was a DEAD BUTTON in exactly the window a person would press it, and a test pinned that as behavior.** `ThreadPrimitive.If running` was the wrong gate: the thread is also "running" while the POST is merely outstanding, and in THAT window no turn id exists — so the button called nothing. OQ6 **rejected** client-only forget, so there is no honest local abort to hide behind it either. The affordance is now gated on a **stoppable turn existing**, the sending notice says in words that there is nothing to stop yet and why, and the test that asserted the dead button now asserts its absence. A second test drives the cancel in the window where it CAN act, through the abandon verb.
- **D4 — DELETED: `navigationCallsNoVerb`.** A boolean nothing reads pins nothing. The guarantee is properly driven three ways (a round trip that posts nothing, the tree scan for the unload family, and the stop act's own test); its reasoning now lives on the inventory's doc comment, where a reader meets it.
- **D5 — CORRECTED, not removed.** The `overloaded` class's reachability was overstated; the handling STAYS because brief R15 mandates 429/overload humanization. See the corrected bullet above for what a chat route actually reaches today. The test that drives it is relabelled as the forward-tolerant arm rather than a reachable status.
- **D6 — CORRECTED: the bundle figure was mis-transcribed**, and the drain moved the code anyway. The entry now carries `851.19 kB` / gzip `255.88`, reproduced across two builds with an identical asset hash.
- **D7 — OBSERVED AND ATTRIBUTED, NOT CHANGED: the double read is LANDED `useLive`/`EventStream` behaviour, and this is simply the first tab-mount with more than one subscriber.** `EventStream.subscribe` hands a subscriber that JOINS an existing source an immediate synchronous `onResnapshot('connected')`; `useLive` has already fired its mount read, and its own debt rule — only a read that STARTED AFTER the signal may clear it — then forces a second. **Demonstrated with no chat code in the picture:** two bare `useLive` subscribers in one component read `["…/sessions","…/files","…/files"]` — the FIRST opens the source and reads once, the SECOND joins and reads twice — while a single subscriber reads once. The landed one-`useLive` views (fleet, inbox) read once for the same reason: their view's effect runs before the shell's, so they are the opener. **Nothing was changed** (§44-B's scope bars touching landed machinery), and no test cements the wart; it is a gate-list observation, and the door if it ever matters is in `EventStream.subscribe`.
- **D8 — the step-up assumption is now EXPLICIT and driven.** A served card declaring `step_up_required` is handed to the decision queue with its reason and its own served card link; nothing is posted. This surface has no PIN field and must never grow one — a High-tier step-up exists so approval identity is never inherited from an idle session (G3 Def.1) — and at v0 an intake interview card declares standard/medium and never asks, so the branch makes the assumption checkable rather than closing a hole.
- **D9 — the cloud negative's PROBES are now real.** The seam "probe" re-evaluated `raw.includes(...)` on a literal, which proves only that `includes` works; it now runs the actual scan over a planted file, the import-line regex has a planted probe of its own, and a third assertion shows the line matcher ignores a non-import mention (which is what makes the scan usable over a tree whose prose explains what it forbids). The enforcing mechanism for the network limb is corrected above.
- **D10 — the wide-layout assertion now asserts something.** It checked only that `.chat-body` appeared somewhere after the first `@media (min-width:`, which an empty block would satisfy, and its message read inverted relative to the check. It now reads the wide block's declarations and requires `flex-direction: row`, with a message that matches.
- **Counters after drain r1:** migrations **0001–0020** (`user_version` **20**, none opened) · event inventory **99/5/104**, `internal/eventlog/contract.go` byte-unchanged · ⚙ **118/33**, `internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged · `components.lock` **29** / **240** npm · `package.json`/`package-lock.json`/`go.mod`/`go.sum` byte-unchanged · conformance **12** · `internal/chat`, `internal/history`, `internal/local`, `internal/intake`, `internal/stage` byte-unchanged (the only Go edit remains `apifixtures_test.go`) · escape-scan allowlist **EMPTY** · **44 Go packages** green · web **262 vitest / 18 files** (was 260/18) + typecheck + build · `Spec/`, `Docs/`, `Research/`, `P3/STATE.md` untouched. **$0**, hermetic, nothing pushed.

#### §44-B drain round 2 (P3-B6-7 part B drain r2, R1–R7 + two leads — FINAL round under the cap)

Seven residuals, all but one traced to a single decision in drain r1's D2. Every fix carries a check that FAILS on the pre-fix code, each verified by reverting it and watching the assertion fire.

- **R1 — ROOT CAUSE, and the premise offered for fixing it is FALSE: there is no real `Submit` in this fixture world.** The residual asked whether `driveFixtureChat`'s handoff already creates a real `asks` row, with both branches resting on a real born run. Neither branch is available: **`fixtureIntake` is a hand-written double** — the `api.IntakeSurface` seam exists so `internal/api` never speaks the pipeline's vocabulary, and `internal/stage` imports `internal/api`. **That is the architecture's choice, not a compile constraint**, and this file says so two bullets above: D2 records that an external `package api_test` MAY legally import `internal/stage`. The seam is a design boundary honoured here, not a wall that would stop anyone (post-cap RES-2). So nothing in this world is born by a producer, there is no real run for a foreign key to point at, and "delete the seeded rows and let the real ones serve" would delete the queue row drain r1 D2 exists to provide. **Recorded rather than complied with.**
  - **What production actually does, read out of the pipeline (this is the fix's authority).** `stage.Surface.Submit` calls `intake.Pipeline.Start`, whose birth transaction inserts the task, its `<task>.intake` run and the first state event — **and no ask**; `Enqueue` then admits the run and the claim loop dispatches it. The interview card is issued LATER by `intake`'s `issueCard`, which inserts the ask row and **transitions the run to `parked` in the SAME transaction** ("gates wait", S06.1). **Therefore a task holding an open interview card has a PARKED intake run, and `running` beside an open ask is a state the intake pipeline cannot produce** — not a harmless wrongness either, because `waiting_on_human` is derived as parked AND an open ask, so the impossible row sat in mission control's running bucket and answered the `running` filter while the task was actually waiting on a person. That is the B6-5 root cause — a world that cannot exist — recreated inside the drain that quotes it.
  - **The fix: `parked`, seeded by `seedFixtureChatBornTask` AFTER `driveFixtureChat`.** The handoff view's own run summary moved with it, so the transcript and the world agree. The ordering move is real but its reason is R5's, not the foreign key's: **nothing in the chat drive reads these rows** (the handoff answers off the seam, not the DB) and the ask is only needed when the committed BODIES are read, which is after `fixtureWorld` returns. **Served order is unaffected either way** — `/api/runs` orders by `created_ts DESC, run_id DESC` (`internal/api/reads.go`), so the row's position never depended on when it was inserted. (The clause was recorded here without its `DESC`s; the substantive point stands, and `mission.test.tsx`'s expected order is right *because* it is descending — post-cap RES-9.)
  - **The guard reads the RULE, not a remembered value.** `TestFixtureBornTaskStateIsOneTheIntakePipelineProduces` extracts `issueCard` from `internal/intake/pipeline.go` and requires that one function both inserts the ask and parks the run, then asserts the seeded state and the handoff view against it. **Its granularity is the FUNCTION, not the transaction** — it would still pass if the two acts were split across two `WriteTx` calls inside `issueCard`; the coupling is what it can see, the atomicity is read by a person (post-cap RES-9). If intake ever stops parking on a gate, it fails there rather than leaving this fixture quietly wrong. Probes: the state flipped back to `running` fails on both limbs; the marker pointed at a neighbouring function fails on the source limb.
  - **Fixture movement, zero drift after regeneration and no cursor movement** (seeded rows mint no events): `runs`/`tasks` carry `parked` with `waiting_on_human: true`; `meters` alice/anthropic `parked_runs` 0 → 1 (`total_runs` 5 and `active_runs` 4 stand — `parked` is one of the six active states); the `state` cell in `history-view-answer` and `history-query-answer`; and `chat-session` loses the born row from turn 1 with `row_count` 6 → 5 and carries `parked` in the handoff view.
- **R2 — the positional pin is RESTORED, unchanged.** Drain r1 replaced `rows[0].textContent` containing `t-ship` with two independent `.toContain(true)` existence checks — which would not have caught the two ids landing on ONE row — and its comment never said the served order had changed. With the born run parked, the `running` filter serves one row again and the ORIGINAL assertion is back verbatim. Probe: with the run `running` it fails `expected 2 to be 1`.
- **R3 — the order-preserving assertion is RESTORED.** `bucketRuns` pushes in served order, so the bucket order IS the server's and `.sort()` de-pinned the one thing `toEqual` was carrying. `by('running')` is `['r-ship']` again, with no sort anywhere.
- **R4 — the blocked-bucket message now describes what is pinned.** It read "the run with an open ask is not under the human bucket", singular, in a world whose queue carries seven open ask items in alice's body alone — and what the line actually pins, together with the `parked` line under it, is that the bucket is the served `waiting_on_human` set (parked AND an open ask) rather than a state test. It now says that, and the bucket genuinely carries **two** rows in served order: `['t-chatborn.intake', 'r-audit']`. `r-ship` is running with two open asks of its own and is correctly not there.
- **R5 — the causality inversion DISSOLVED with R1's ordering, and is now guarded.** Turn 1's `cost_per_run` answer listed `t-chatborn.intake` while turn 3 is the handoff that gives birth to it: the conversation's first answer reported the run of a task it had not yet created. The answers are real reads, so the seeding position was the only fix. `TestChatTranscriptNeverAnswersWithARowItHasNotYetCreated` walks the committed turns and forbids the name before the handoff turn (allowing it ON that turn, which is where the birth belongs) and refuses to be vacuous if no handoff turn exists. Probe: run against drain r1's committed body it names turn 1 exactly. **What that guard is, precisely: the SECOND link, not the first** — it reads the committed body off disk, so re-introducing the seeding order fails `TestWebAPIFixtures/chat-session` first (the compare is what catches the world; this catches the body a reviewer reads). Nothing can land silently either way, because CI's fixture compare is authoritative and drift fails loudly (post-cap RES-8). The "renders its rows at the served grain" test still means what it says — it compares rendered rows to `answer.rows.length`, so it followed the body down to five without being touched.
- **R6 — both unguarded surfaces now carry a guard, and the one that cannot is named.** `fixtures.historyQueryAnswer` was the ONLY one of the 29 doubles nothing called, so `history-query-answer.json` looked guarded and was not (its rows grew 7 → 8 with the world). It is now consumed by a driven test of the History panel's **Layer-1 direct** leg — the picker's second select, which had no test at all — asserting the canned confidence, the rendered row count against the served rows, and the born run's served `parked` state. And **a scan makes the class a failure**: every key in `doubles.ts`'s `fixtures` object must be referenced somewhere in the tree, with a planted probe. Probe: run over the pre-fix tree it reports `['historyQueryAnswer']`.
  - **The meters figures are now cross-checked against the served runs body over the SHARED world.** `total_runs`, `active_runs` and `parked_runs` per (owner, lane), and `per_person.runs` per person — the server's own definitions read back, never a client computation. **`unpriced_runs` is NOT checkable from here** (it counts receipts, which the runs list does not carry) and stays guarded by the Go byte-compare alone, said out loud at the assertion rather than implied. Probes: each perturbed figure fails with the lane or person named.
- **R7 — RECORDED FOR THE B6 GATE, NOT FIXED (cross-surface, and both sides are outside part B).** `ask:ask-chatborn-1` is served `answerable: true` with **no `actions` field at all** — the only one of the twelve items in `approvals-mine.json` like that — and the landed `web/src/Inbox.tsx` therefore renders it `data-acts="none"`: *"this card declares no answer verbs, so there is nothing here to press."* For the very card the chat surface answers successfully. **Nothing here is a bug in either surface.** An `intake.CardInterview` genuinely declares no card-level verbs (its answer vocabulary is per-QUESTION options), `readCardShape` walks Approval / Verbs / Decision / Delta and faithfully derives none, and the chat feed answers it through the landed verb with a `{answers:[{id,value}]}` envelope built from the card's own options — which is exactly the slot editor §43 left named-and-unbuilt. **What the drain changed is that this became SERVED GROUND for the first time**: no interview card was in a committed approvals body before it. It is the same shape as the delta-card defect the B6-6 drain fixed at the PRODUCER, and the decision — whether the projector should derive an interview card's verbs, or the inbox should grow the slot editor, or the card should say what closes it — belongs to the gate, not to a widget drain (`internal/intake` and the landed inbox are both out of scope). R1 did not remove the card from the queue: it is still there, now on a parked run.
- **~~GATE ITEM~~ — SUPERSEDED BY THE POST-CAP FIX BELOW, and the mitigation recorded here was FALSE.** What was true and remains true: the real `Submit` returns after `Start` + `Enqueue`, when the intake run is `queued` and **no ask exists** — the card arrives later, from the worker leg — while the fixture's `Submit` answers with `open_ask_id` + `open_card`, so the committed transcript shows a card a real first response would not carry. What was **false** is the sentence that followed it: *"in production the feed would meet it on a re-read."* **No re-read can ever add that card to a turn's outcome.** `SettleTurn` is the ONLY writer of `chat_turns.outcome` (`AbandonTurn` and `ReconcileOrphanedTurns` touch state and window columns and never it), `chatSessionView` serves the stored outcome verbatim apart from redaction and never consults the intake surface, and the chat view did not declare `intake.state`, so the frame announcing the card was not even heard. The consequence was that **OQ8-(i)'s in-place card could never appear on a real platform** — it rendered in tests only because a double returned it. This is corrected in the post-cap section below by reading the card from the person's own decision queue; the disposition question this bullet deferred ("does the turn store a snapshot, or does the feed re-read?") **is answered there**, so nothing about it is carried to the gate.
- **Two leads raised at re-check, both DISMISSED with evidence.** (a) `t-chatborn` missing from `internal/api/oversightreads_test.go`'s list is not a broken invariant: that list names **four of the nine** served tasks — the ones whose card faces the test then inspects — and `t-stall`, `t-ops`, `t-notes` and `t-claim` are equally absent and all predate this packet. No test pins served task-list MEMBERSHIP, which is a pre-existing limit, not a widening. (b) The vacuity risk at `web/src/inbox.test.tsx` is real in shape and not live: `not_answerable_reason ?? ''` would assert `toContain('')` vacuously, but the card the finder selects is `effect:e-notify` (index 1 of 16), which carries a real reason, and the drain's own card sits at index 6 where it cannot be selected. The landed B6-6 inbox is out of scope, so it is recorded here rather than hardened.
