#### §44-B post-cap coordinator items (P3-B6-7 part B, RES-1…RES-9 — implemented inline; the drain cap was exhausted)

The round-2 re-check confirmed R1–R7 closed and D1–D10 still closed, and found nine residuals. The two-round cap was spent, so the coordinator implemented these directly, with each item's own probe re-run. **One of them is a real behavioural defect, and it is the reason the escalation was worth making instead of filing it as a gate note.**

- **RES-1 — OQ8-(i) WAS UNREACHABLE IN PRODUCTION, and the fix is to read the card from the DECISION QUEUE rather than from the turn.** The chain, each link demonstrated: production's `Start` births the task and its intake run with **no ask at all**, and `issueCard` inserts the ask and parks the run later, in a transaction of its own — so the real `Submit` answers **cardless**; `SettleTurn` is the **only** writer of `chat_turns.outcome`; `chatSessionView` serves that stored outcome verbatim apart from redaction and **never consults the intake surface**; and the chat view **did not declare `intake.state`**, so the frame that announces the card was not heard. The in-place card therefore rendered in tests and could never render on a real platform — a ratified disposition delivered only against a double. **`BornTaskView` now takes the open card from `GET /api/approvals`**, matched on the card's own `task_id` (nothing composed), which is also the only surface serving the `payload_hash` an answer must quote: **card and pin now come from one place**, and the card leaves the feed the moment it is resolved — including when it is answered in the inbox instead. The turn's outcome keeps being the record that a task was born; it stopped being asked what is open now, which is what "stale never poses as live" requires of it. A third branch was added because it is reachable rather than hypothetical: a matching card this surface cannot render (a delta decision, which carries no `questions`) is sent to the decision queue by name instead of being shown empty or, worse, reported as *nothing waiting*.
  - **`chatIntakeEventTypes = ['intake.state', 'decision.recorded']`**, with its §42 reason on the declaration: neither is a chat type, and that is the point — the pipeline issues the card after the handoff turn has already settled, so `intake.state` is the only frame that can announce it, and `decision.recorded` is what takes it away. Answering also re-reads both the conversation and the queue rather than making the person wait for their own act to come back through the relay.
  - **Driven four ways, and the first two FAIL on the pre-fix code** (verified by reverting the render source and watching them fire): a handoff turn whose outcome carries **no card and no ask id** — the production shape — renders the queued card with every question; an outcome that **does** carry a card renders **nothing** when the queue no longer does (the stale half); a born task with nothing open says so and asks the queue for no pin; and an `intake.state` frame triggers the re-read (this one fails when the type is dropped from the declaration).
  - **The vanishing-card race is now driven where it actually lives.** The pin is still re-read at the moment of answering, so the "no longer in your decision queue" branch is reached by re-scripting the queue empty **between render and press** — somebody resolved it in the inbox meanwhile — rather than by rendering from a snapshot the queue never carried.
- **RES-5 / RES-6 — the two remaining unguarded bodies now carry the guard R6 gave their sibling.** Flipping the born row's `state` to `running` in `history-view-answer.json` left all 265 tests green, and so did flipping `tasks.json`'s born `latest_run` to `running` with `waiting_on_human: false` — both bodies' own copy of the very state R1 exists to correct was held by the Go byte-compare alone. The Layer-0 views leg now pins the rendered row count and the born row's `parked` state where it is rendered, and the board suite pins the born card's served state, its `waiting_on_human`, and that its face shows *waiting on a person*. Both of the evaluator's exact flips now fail.
- **RES-7 — the dead-double scan now claims only what it enforces.** Its message said "never read by any test" while the predicate searches all of `web/src`, including `doubles.ts` itself: five bodies are reached only through that file's route tables, which is a real way to be exercised. It now says **reached from nowhere in `web/src`**, and says out loud that it cannot tell whether what reaches a body asserts anything about it — which is exactly why RES-5 and RES-6 needed per-body assertions, and why this scan could not see them.
- **RES-2, RES-8, RES-9 — three record corrections, made in place so each bullet reads truthfully standing alone** (§44's own D12 lesson): the R1 bullet no longer implies a compile constraint where there is a design boundary (D2, two bullets above, records that an external `api_test` may legally import `internal/stage`); the R5 causality guard is named as the **second** link, since it reads the committed body while the fixture compare is what catches the world; and `/api/runs`'s clause is restored to `created_ts DESC, run_id DESC` while the `issueCard` guard is described by what it can actually see — one FUNCTION doing both acts, not one transaction.
- **RES-3, RES-4 — DISMISSED with evidence, and not touched.** The `.sort()` on `mission.test.tsx`'s **queued** bucket is present at `8d04d2d`, the commit before this packet: it is landed B6-5 code, so retightening it is not a widget packet's business (the D7 call, applied consistently) — recorded for the gate. And the 29-files↔29-doubles tie the r2 deviation cited is enforced by nothing; it needed no defence, because `history-query-answer.json` is now consumed on its own merit by a driven test.
- **What this section cannot claim.** These items were implemented after the drain cap was spent, so **no fresh adversarial pass reviewed them** — the coordinator wrote them and probed them. Every behavioural claim above was verified by breaking the production code and watching a named test fail; that is the evidence, and it is weaker than an independent judge.

## 45. The S15.8 review surfaces — diff, comments, try-it & accept (P3-B6-8 part A; S15.8, S13.1–S13.4, S13.6, S13.8, S15.2, FC-v1 §2, §40-B, §41+§41-B, §42+§42-B, §43)

The **first of two parts** of B6-8. `/deliverables/:id` goes live — `routes.ts`'s `owner: 'B6-8'` → `''` and `deliverable` into `routes.test.ts`'s `built` — and **the pattern, the title and the nav placement do not move** (§41-B fill-never-rename). **Part B, the workforce map, was not built BY THIS PART** (corrected in place 2026-07-30, when part B landed — see §45-B): R11–R15, OQ3–OQ7 and the TBD-P3(workforce-map rendering) resolution were untouched here, `/workforce` was left carrying its `B6-8` stub owner, and the shell still said which packet would fill it. All of that is part B's, and part B has since landed it. NO migration (0001–0020, `user_version` **20**, 0021 still RESERVED for B6-9) and NO new event type — every act this surface performs has a landed canonical row.

- **REACT-DIFF-VIEW 3.3.3, RE-VERIFIED AT THE PIN BEFORE WIRING** (the adopt-rail obligation, 2026-07-30): 3.3.3 IS the registry `latest` dist-tag (the `next` tag is a 2.0.0-alpha and is not adopted), **MIT**, carrying **no `deprecated` field**, and its `dist.integrity` (`sha512-CPveApk6n7Zbk…877exoQ==`) is **byte-identical to the committed `package-lock.json`** — so the tarball behind the pin is the one the lock resolved. Upstream `otakustay/react-diff-view` is **not archived, not disabled**, still MIT, last pushed 2026-03-30 (quiet-but-alive — the FC-v1 single-maintainer risk, already accepted and bounded by the pin and by containment to one view). **Zero advisories** in the GitHub Advisory DB and OSV for the package. `gitdiff-parser` 0.3.1 arrives inside the closure, is the registry `latest`, MIT, and its integrity matches the lock too. **The closure is NOT all-MIT, stated so nothing here implies it (drain r1, D12):** `diff-match-patch` — which the tokenizer needs and which is what moved the bundle below — is **Apache-2.0**. `components.lock`'s entry never claimed otherwise (its licence check is path-scoped to the installed `react-diff-view` ref), and both licences are permissive and compatible; the fact is recorded because "MIT" appearing three times in one bullet reads like a claim about the whole tree. **ALIVE — wired.** The monaco-diff upgrade path stays pre-registered and unused. No patch, no bump.
- **⚠ DECLARED DEVIATION — TWO SURGICAL FIXES IN `internal/review`, WHICH THE BRIEF LISTED AS UNTOUCHED. The packet as specified was not achievable without them, and both are one landed defect.** `gitDiff` wrote the two contents to a fresh `os.MkdirTemp` directory and ran `git diff --no-index` over them, so the served `unified` text's file headers named **`/tmp/sinet-review-diff-<random>/old`**. Three consequences, all load-bearing here: the served body **differed on every read of the same immutable revision pair** (so no golden fixture of a comparison could exist, and every `useLive` re-read looked like a change); the widget's `parseDiff` reads a file's IDENTITY from those headers, so the review surface would have labelled every file with a host temp path while the comment anchors keyed on the real one; and a host filesystem path rode out on a served body. **The fix:** `gitDiff` now takes the logical `name` and rewrites the temp paths in the header lines to it (by exact string, so no diff BODY line can be touched — a temp path cannot appear in content written to that temp path moments earlier); an empty name leaves git's output alone. **The second half:** `contentDiff` used to write a `=== <name> ===` separator between per-file diffs, which existed only BECAUSE the headers named a temp file — and which **crashes gitdiff-parser** (`Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'changes')`, reproduced against the committed body before and after). With real headers the separator is a duplicate of the header and makes the result a non-standard unified diff, so it is gone. Pinned by `TestGitDiffHeadersNameTheLogicalPathAndNothingElse`, which asserts the real path IS present, that no temp-directory name survives, that two runs over the same contents are byte-identical, and — the non-tautological control — that an empty name leaves the temp paths in place. **This is a REFERRAL: the coordinator rules on it. Neither change touches a verb, a wall or a schema; `internal/review`'s exported surface moves by nothing.**
- **OQ1 as landed — the allowlist STAYS EMPTY and the FIVE landed promises are corrected in place.** An `<iframe src>` embeds a browsing context BY REFERENCE; every banned token is a raw-HTML assignment or a code-execution call, so the sanctioned channel was never inside the set and needed no exemption. The five sites are **§41-B, §43, §44-B, the scan file's own header and the scan file's assertion message**, each carrying a dated correction. (The count read "three" here and "four" in the scan header, over the same five names, until drain r2 R8 made the two framings agree; the enumeration was always the complete part.) **The hardening limb landed:** `srcdoc` is now a banned token with its own planted probe (four planted violations, four hits), because raw HTML by attribute is exactly this list's class and this is the packet that introduced iframes. The scan floor moves **40 → 42** and names `./Deliverable.tsx`. A consequence worth recording: the scan reads raw text, so a file may not name a banned token even in prose — `Deliverable.tsx`'s own doc comment describes the inline-document attribute without spelling it, the same discipline `escape-scan.test.ts` and `scans.test.ts` already apply to themselves.
- **OQ2 as landed — "synced navigation" is SHELL-DRIVEN, and the limit is on screen.** The preview origins are a tailnet subdomain or a loopback port, so they are **cross-origin to the SPA**: the shell can neither read a frame's location nor observe a click inside one. So `SyncSemantics{mode:"path"}` is honoured by one shared path control that composes `<side.url><path>` and sets BOTH `src` values together; a click inside a frame moves that frame alone and **the surface says so**, with a re-sync control that re-asserts the shell's path on both. No postMessage protocol (it would mean injecting script into somebody else's application) and no proxy rewriting. `single_instance:true` renders ONE frame plus the served no-before reason with sync disabled — never a faked second pane. `frameSrc(base, path)` is the whole composition rule and is exported so it is testable directly; a scan asserts the tree contains exactly ONE `<iframe` element and that nothing carrying content (a comparison, a comment, a card, a detail) reaches it.
- **OQ2b as landed — `GET /api/deliverables/{deliverable}/objects/{sha}`, and it is not a sha oracle.** The sha resolves ONLY against that deliverable's own revisions' `ObjectRef`s, so a hash that is real but pinned elsewhere answers 404 — asserted with the pinning deliverable's own read as the non-tautological control. The scope is `deliverableScope`'s, so the route joined the landed `deliverablesRoutes` inventory and inherits its not-wired, session-required and unversioned walks rather than getting copies of them. **The inline line, with the exact header set and why each one is there:** `Content-Type` comes from a CLOSED allowlist of four raster image types (the recorded label is a producer's word, not a wire header, so two spellings of each are admitted) and everything else is `application/octet-stream` + `Content-Disposition: attachment` (RFC 6266, stdlib-encoded over the base filename only, because the name is a producer-supplied path); **`image/svg+xml` is DELIBERATELY ABSENT** — an SVG is a scriptable document wearing an image type, and it takes the attachment lane with the rest, asserted by name. Plus `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` (the Fetch-Standard sniffing path is how octet-stream bytes become HTML), `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox` (applies to the response itself: navigated to directly it lands in an opaque origin with scripts and forms disabled — the belt to nosniff's braces, and the inline lane cannot rely on a disposition), `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin` and `X-Frame-Options: DENY`. `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable` because the sha in the path IS the hash of the bytes, so what a URL answers can never change (the §41 hashed-asset reasoning), and `private` keeps a shared cache out of an owner-scoped read. A row pinning bytes that are gone answers 500 `content_drift`, the posture `reviewErr` already takes.
- **THE CLIENT KEEPS NO COPY OF THE INLINE RULE, and that is why the image trio is honest.** Duplicating the server's allowlist in the view would be the §40-C D3 twin hazard in a second language. Instead the surface renders `<img>` on the surface the SERVER chose (`image-pair` means review's own type test said these are images) and learns the rest from the actual response: an `onError` renders the absence with the object's RECORDED type and points at the download. Driven by firing a real load failure.
- **OQ8 as landed — what is committed ground and what is derived, each named AT its assertion.** Golden, producer-driven: the detail with its doors (**both limbs of the request-revision door, from two deliverables** — a single deliverable cannot serve both, because the door's state IS the deliverable's state), the comparison surfaces (line-diff, the `old=0` pre-task base, the labeled extracted-text FALLBACK, image-pair, binary-cards, and the PDF extraction-failure degrade with its reason on the label — the last two are DIFFERENT renders, because the PDF lane degrades past the fallback diff to the metadata cards), the placed comments, the accept card, and the live-session list. **Derived, with the reason in each case about the world rather than convenience:** the accept OUTCOMES — **and the reason first recorded here was FALSE, corrected at drain r1 (D8)**: it claimed a real applied accept needs a git remote and a broker push and that a collision needs HEAD engineered to move, when `internal/accept/accept_test.go` already does all three HERMETICALLY (`TestAcceptEndToEnd` applies against a `t.TempDir` bare repo and asserts the remote sha, the succeeded class-A effect, the real trailers and the routed-runs fan-out; both card paths are driven; the `fakePusher` is already composed in this fixture world). The TRUE reason is the one the very next bullet states for the snapshot pins: a real accept produces a commit whose sha and timestamps are wall-clock, and neither can live in a fixed-clock golden body. The accept CARD — where the pin, the trailers and the acceptability come from — is real, the preview dispositions and the comparison (a real launch spawns a process and binds a port, which no hermetic test may do — and the point of the five non-backed states is that they render an ANSWER, so they are driven as the server's own state vocabulary over the real `Session` shape), an UNKNOWN `surface` value (no current producer can mint one), a SERVED `pixel_diff` aid (a nil seam at v0, so the absent branch is the only real one and the served branch has to exist for the day a producer fills it), and the THREE object/fallback-surface DETAILS — image, binary and notebook (their comparisons are all real committed bodies; further committed details would prove nothing the first does not). The count read "two" before drain r1 (D12) and `doubles.ts` derived three.
- **THE FIXTURE WORLD GREW A REVIEW WORLD, AND ONE SHARED HANDLE MADE IT POSSIBLE.** `fixtureReview` used to build a store per server over a fresh `t.TempDir()` — so a revision minted through the real verb was invisible to the next request, which is exactly why the review reads had only ever been fixtured off hand-written SQL rows with invented content hashes. The store is now composed ONCE per world; `MintRevision`, `AddComment`, `AddFindings` and `Drain` are therefore the producers of every committed body. `accept.Accepter` and `preview.Manager` are composed too — neither is CALLED by a committed read (`acceptable()` asks whether the orchestration exists, not what it would do, and nothing launches), and composing them is what makes the served doors say what a real process says instead of "not composed in this process", so both directions of the doors are driven. Host-hazard posture unchanged: `NewCaddyClient("", "")`, this package's portpool range 47900-47919, nothing binds a port.
  - **The revision content is load-bearing, not decoration.** Rev 2 makes two edits far enough apart to land in two hunks under `--unified=3` plus one MODIFIED line: the distance is what produces a `mapped` placement (an anchor in the untouched middle maps by the diff's own delta) beside a `drifted` one (an OLD-side anchor of revision 1, whose old side is the pre-task base the S13.5 topology has not materialized, so the ladder skips the map and finds the quote near the claimed position), and the modification is what gives `markEdits` a delete/insert pair to mark — a diff of pure insertions gives the tokenizer nothing to do, so the committed body could not have exercised the client-side highlighting at all. **All five statuses are ASSERTED in the seeding**, not hoped for: a content edit that quietly collapsed two of them would leave a render nobody checks while the body still looked plausible, which is an absence failing silently.
  - **The snapshot pins are LITERALS, and that is the honest choice.** What makes a revision repo-backed — and therefore acceptable — is the column being filled; a real `git` sha would carry the wall clock into the committed bodies through the commit's own timestamps, and the accept orchestration never resolves these against a repo because no fixture read calls it. The `engine_sessions` row is added so the card takes the PRIMARY provenance path (recorded substrate) rather than the lane fallback, and the `repo_registry` row is what gives `protected_ref` something true to say.
  - **The rework card is a real `verify.Card`** (category `AC-BLOCKER`, whose landed vocabulary carries `revise_with_guidance`), because the door reads the card's own `choices` (§40-B) and a hand-written snapshot would only prove the door can read keys somebody imagined. Its ask, its `gates.CanonicalHash` pin and its route are all the producer's own.
- **`answerAtDoor` exists because a card id is a COMPOSITE and the door serves its halves separately.** `door.ask_id` is the native half alone; the answer verb's path takes `<kind>:<native-id>`. Assembling that composite in the client would be re-deriving a transport identity it was already handed — the mistake §43 recorded on the other decision family — so the surface posts to the route the DOOR named, with the door's own pin and the door's own `revise_with_guidance`. The one check is a boundary check, because a served string is becoming a request target: the route must be an `/api/` path. Found by driving it: the first cut called `answerApproval(door.ask_id)` and posted to `/api/approvals/ask-brief/answer`, a path that does not exist.
- **The comment loop, and the invariant that makes it worth having.** A comment with a live position renders AT that position through the widget's own `widgets` map (keyed by `getChangeKey`, resolved from the parsed hunks — so a placement the widget cannot reach is deliberately NOT forced in) **and** in the list, and every comment WITHOUT one renders on the synthetic strip with its quote and its original-revision link. **The invariant is a driven test over the five-status body: every served comment id appears in the DOM.** Selecting a diff line composes the claimed anchor from the rendered change — the side from the change type, the line number from the rendered numbering, and the quote from `change.content`, which gitdiff-parser gives WITHOUT the diff marker (a leading `+` or `-` would make every claim fail to confirm against the revision's own line, and a test asserts the marker is absent). The response's own `anchor_status` is what renders afterwards, including the server-corrected case. **No update, no delete, no drain verb** — a DOM walk over every comment card and a source scan for `PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE`/`/drain`.
- **Presentation follows AUTHORSHIP, and the server stays the authority.** `acceptable` carries NO caller limb — the operator genuinely reads a card that says the act is open — so the accept FORM renders for the owner only and a non-owner gets the card plus the authorship line. Both directions are driven from two sessions over ONE served body, and the 403 is driven through the verb as well, because that refusal is what actually stops a non-owner. The PIN rides the same request and is cleared the instant the request carries it; a DOM walk asserts it survives nowhere.
- **Per-view declared types (`deliverableEventTypes`, §42).** Ten, each either what changes what this page renders or what takes something off it: `artifact.produced` (a new revision moves the lineage, the default pair and every placement), `review.comment` (the loop's own row, both ingresses), `review.drained` (what stamps the finding numbers and flips a comment to consumed), `deliverable.accepted` (the terminal act closes the doors), `verdict.recorded` (a revision's verdict ref), `preview.started`/`preview.stopped` (the session list this page offers a stop on), `engine.gate_ask` and `decision.recorded` (what OPENS and CLOSES the rework card the request-revision door hangs on), and `run.state_changed`. Driven both ways: a declared frame re-reads, an undeclared one reaches nothing; and the view re-reads after its own verb.
- **New structural constants, each with its reason** (no ⚙ key exists for any of them and the browser cannot read the registry before login — the §41-B backoff precedent; interim under the standing settings-tab directive): `defaultViewType` **split**, because reviewing IS comparing two states and inline is one click away for a narrow screen; `defaultImageMode` **2-up**, the reading that needs no interaction to be useful; `defaultSwipeAt` **50** and `defaultBlend` **0.5**, the only positions that show both sides at once. The swipe value is carried as a whole-number CSS width so no arithmetic sits between the control and the style. **No cap and no window:** every list on this surface is bounded by the server.
- **Landed tests that moved, and why — each is a fixture-world growth, not a relaxation.** `routes.test.ts`'s `built` gains `deliverable`; `chat.test.tsx`'s built/unbuilt table now expects the ONE remaining unbuilt row; `taskdetail.test.tsx` derives the task's deliverable list FROM the served body (with a non-empty guard) instead of a hand-copied pair, because `t-ship` now carries five; `mission.test.tsx` derives the waiting-on-human and parked buckets from the served flags the same way, with a floor so the derivation cannot pass over an empty set; and `oversightreads_test.go`'s `?task=` scope test now asserts the operator's and the owner's answers are the SAME SET rather than counting to two — an owner-scope test that fails because a world grew is failing for the wrong reason.
- **Counters at part A:** migrations **0001–0020** (`user_version` **20**, none opened — **0021 stays RESERVED for B6-9**) · event inventory **99 minted / 5 declare-only / 104 registered** with `internal/eventlog/contract.go` byte-unchanged (no type minted) · ⚙ **118 keys / 33 domains** with `internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged (no key added, no new read site) · `components.lock` **29** / **240** npm · **`web/package.json` + `package-lock.json` byte-unchanged** (the pick is consumed as installed — wiring is consumption) · conformance **12** · Layer-0 views **11**, catalog **50** (no query added — OQ2b is a transport read, not a catalog entry) · `internal/benchmark/registered.go`, BENCH-REG, `internal/settings`, `internal/accept`, `internal/preview`, `internal/history`, `internal/worker`, `go.mod`/`go.sum` byte-unchanged · escape-scan allowlist **EMPTY**, floor 42 · **44 Go packages** green · web **316 vitest / 19 files** (was 269/18) + typecheck + build · `Spec/`, `Docs/`, `Research/`, `P3/STATE.md` untouched. **$0**, hermetic, nothing pushed, no live-Caddy touch.
- **REPORT — the bundle moved again, measured rather than guessed:** `851.73 kB` (gzip `256.04`) → **`950.52 kB`** (gzip **`286.23`**). **CORRECTED at drain r1 (D9):** the first cut recorded the "before" as `851.19 / 255.88`, which is §44-B's SUPERSEDED drain-r1 measurement — the very line that warns the pre-drain entry mis-transcribed itself. What B6-7 part B shipped is `851.73 / 256.04` (§44-B's landing figure), and that is what the delta is against. Wiring react-diff-view pulls in its `diff-match-patch` closure, which the tokenizer needs. Nothing is code-split, for the same reason recorded at B6-7: the SPA is embedded in the binary and served over a tailnet. Vite's 500 kB warning still fires, as it has since before B6-7.
- **NOT BUILT BY THIS PART, and named rather than implied: part B, the workforce map (`/workforce`)** — LANDED SINCE, 2026-07-30, in §45-B; this bullet is the record of what part A left, corrected in place so it cannot be read as a claim about today. R11–R15 and OQ3/OQ4/OQ5/OQ6/OQ7 were untouched here — the roster read, the version→outcome join, equipment, multi-stage connections, per-version quality/cost views, and **the dated TBD-P3(workforce-map rendering approach) resolution, which was part B's to record and which §45-B now carries**. Part A left the `/workforce` route with its `B6-8` stub owner; part B flipped it. B6-9 (push, re-nag, the S15.12 sweep and the escape AUDIT) is still untouched; the stable URLs both parts landed are what it will deep-link.

### §45 drain round 1 (P3-B6-8 part A drain r1, D1–D14) — corrections, and one prescription overtaken by the code

Ten evaluator findings plus seven nits, all actioned; nothing dropped. Every fix carries a test that FAILS on the pre-fix code, each verified by reverting the fix and watching the assertion fire. The referred `internal/review` deviation was independently reproduced and **RATIFIED**; D7 hardens the input that fix introduced, which the ratification puts in scope.

- **D1 — the preview frame shipped with NO `sandbox`, and that contradicted the platform's own contract.** `review.EscapeFirst()` names the channel `sandboxed-rendered-document-view` and S13.3 calls it "the sandboxed rendered-document view", so this was a conflict with the spec text rather than a missing hardening. Cross-origin blocks a frame reading this document's DOM; it does not stop the frame assigning `window.top.location`, and the surface it would navigate away from is the one that collects a PIN. The frame now rides `previewSandbox` = `allow-scripts allow-forms allow-same-origin allow-popups`, with **every token's grant reasoned on the code** and the withheld ones reasoned too — `allow-top-navigation` (and both sibling forms) is the capability most deliberately absent, and `allow-popups-to-escape-sandbox`, `allow-modals` and `allow-downloads` are named as withheld rather than merely missing. **`allow-same-origin` is safe here for a STRUCTURAL reason, said out loud rather than left to luck:** a preview is served from a different origin than the SPA (the tailnet `preview-<id>.<host>` subdomain, or a loopback port from the preview pool — **`internal/portpool`'s structural default `47600–47619`**, corrected at drain r2 R5: the figure first recorded here was `47900-47919`, which is `internal/api`'s TEST range and not a production port at all; the conclusion was re-verified against the real range and is unchanged, but a security argument must cite the range that ships), so the token restores the FRAME's origin and never this page's — and if a preview were ever served same-origin with the SPA the token would have to go. `referrerPolicy="no-referrer"` joins it: the SPA URL carries the deliverable id and was leaving as `Referer`, and the external preview link already carried `rel="noreferrer noopener"`.
- **D2 — the synthetic strip dropped comments the widget could not reach, on this packet's OWN golden fixture.** The strip asked only whether a placement had a line number; the widget map additionally required the file to be one of the rendered ones AND the line to fall inside a rendered hunk. A placement satisfying the first and failing the second rendered nowhere but the flat list — on the committed body, comment 2 (`mapped`, `site/release.tsx:10`) exactly. That is the invisible-comment class (P-T12-2) on the surface built to prevent it, and the code comments and §45 both claimed the opposite. **`widgetReach` is now the ONE function that answers "will a widget render this here?"**, used by the widget map and complemented by the strip, so the two cannot drift again. A surface with no diff at all passes an empty file list, so nothing has a live position and everything is on the strip — which is the right answer for an image or a binary.
- **D3 — the invariant test could not fail, which is why D2 survived it.** Its first half asserted a `[data-comment]` node exists for every served comment, which is a property of `comments.map` running; its second half recomputed the production predicate verbatim and compared the implementation to a copy of itself. It is now driven from the DOM: for every served comment, the id must appear in a `.diff-widget` **or** on the strip, both sets read out of what rendered — plus the complement check, that no id is in both, and floors on each set so neither half can pass over nothing. Verified together with D2: on the pre-D2 code it fires naming comment 2.
- **D4 — the prescription was overtaken by what the code actually requires, and the real defect was larger.** The finding was right that a durable, undeletable blocker comment was written before the answer and then reported as "nothing was sent", duplicating on retry. But the prescribed reorder (answer first, then guidance) would have LOST the guidance: `verify.ResumeWithGuidance` calls `RecordGuidance` and then `DrainOpen` **inline inside the answer request**, so a comment posted afterwards arrives after the batch it was written for was already consumed. And the deeper defect: `ValidateAnswer` **refuses `revise_with_guidance` with an empty `guidance` list**, so the landed client — which sent `{choice}` and nothing else — could never have worked at all; the test passed because it scripted a 200 for a body the server would have rejected, which is the B6-5 producer-fidelity root cause repeating inside this packet. **The fix is neither ordering: the guidance rides the ANSWER**, as `{choice, guidance:[{text}]}`, so there is exactly ONE write, the platform records and drains it in the correct order itself, a refusal means nothing was written, and a retry cannot accumulate. Severity is no longer this surface's to pick — `Answer.Comments()` marks a guidance point `Blocking` because guidance always names a problem to fix — and the form says so instead of offering a control the schema does not have. The empty-guidance case disarms the button with its reason, and the stale message now says "NOTHING was written — not the guidance, not the answer".
- **D5 — a second subscription whose data was discarded and whose §42 debt was settled anyway.** `key: served ? '' : …` disabled nothing: `useLive` uses `key` only as an effect DEPENDENCY, so the read and the `stream.subscribe()` both ran. Fixed at the CALL SITE, as directed — `useLive` is untouched: `OwnedCommentsBlock` mounts the hook and is rendered only by the surfaces that own the read, while the diff surface passes its own feed down. **A measurement worth recording rather than hiding:** this view's comment read went 4 → 2, not 4 → 1, because ONE subscribed `useLive` mount on this page costs two calls. **The MECHANISM recorded here was inverted, and the assertion built on it had a blind spot — both corrected at drain r2 R3.** It said "a child-mounted `useLive` reads twice per mount (the chat view's exchange sidebar does the same)", but child-ness is not the variable: on `/chat`, `GET /api/chat/sessions` is 1 and `GET /api/chat/files` is 2 — two SIBLING hooks in one component. The real rule is §44-B's own (drain r1, D7), demonstrated there: the FIRST subscriber opens the source and reads once, and a LATER one JOINS and reads twice because `EventStream.subscribe` hands a joiner an immediate synchronous `onResnapshot('connected')`. This page's detail hook is the opener, so previews and comments are both joiners at two. The test asserted `comments === the live /api/previews count` rather than a literal, on the reasoning that a literal would be "a claim about the framework" — but §44-B already pins that claim with its own demonstration, so the literal is a fact the repo proves, and the comparison form failed twice over: a doubled previews subscriber made it 4 === 4 and **passed silently**, and any change to `TryItBlock` failed this test while naming the comment feed as the culprit. Both were reproduced. It is a literal **2** now, with the §44-B pointer as its reason.
- **D6 — `parseDiff` ran bare in render with no boundary anywhere, so one malformed body took the whole app down.** The irony is load-bearing: this packet's deviation exists BECAUSE the server served a body that crashes this parser, and the consumer was left unguarded. `parseUnified` now catches and the failure renders as an ANSWER with its reason and the served text beside it, as text — the same forward-tolerance the unknown-`surface` arm already had. The regression feeds the exact pre-fix shape (a separator line before a real `diff --git` block) and fails on the unguarded code with the original `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'changes')`.
- **D7 — the fix this packet added interpolated an unvalidated producer string into a wire format.** Not reachable at v0 (every mint passes a constant) and reachable the moment revisions carry producer-named trees, which `MintInput`'s own comment anticipates. `diffLabel` is the boundary check: a name carrying a newline, a carriage return or a tab, or containing `" b/"` (which makes the header ambiguous about where the old path ends), yields the EMPTY label — leaving git's own output untouched rather than emitting a mangled or forged header — and a leading separator is stripped so no `a//abs/path` can compose. **`relabel`'s own comment is corrected to the honest claim:** what makes the exact-string replacement safe is UNPREDICTABILITY (a random temp dir per call), not impossibility.
- **D8** — the accept-outcomes downgrade reason was false and is corrected above; the true reason is the wall-clock/varying-sha one the snapshot-pin bullet already gave. **The other four downgrades were re-checked and all hold.** ⚠ **This correction reached THIS FILE ONLY — `deliverable.test.tsx`'s own header kept the false reason until drain r2 R1.** For a declared downgrade the assertion site is the place that matters (this bullet's own rule: the reasons are named "at its assertion"), so a bookkeeping-only correction was not a correction.
- **D9/D10/D12/D13/D14** — the bundle "before" figure was §44-B's superseded drain-r1 measurement and is corrected to the landing one. `Content-Length` now comes from the FILE via `http.ServeContent` rather than from the recorded `ref.Size`, so the length and the body cannot disagree. **Its DRIVER changed at drain r2 R9**, which made a drifted object a refusal rather than a 200 — so the length claim can no longer be driven by drifting the bytes, and it is driven by a **Range read** instead (206 + `Content-Range` + a partial length is arithmetic only `ServeContent` over the served bytes can produce; a handler setting the recorded size and copying answers 200 with the whole body, verified). Record corrections: three derived object/fallback details not two (**this one also reached this file only — the assertion site still said "two" until drain r2 R2**), **FIVE** forward-promise sites in the scan header not three (§44-B was omitted from the count and so was the scan's own assertion message; this round said "four" and drain r2 R8 corrected it to five over the same enumeration), `SessionPanel` links rather than "links and embeds", and the closure is **not all-MIT** — `diff-match-patch` is Apache-2.0, recorded because three MITs in one bullet read like a claim about the tree. All eight banned tokens now carry a planted probe (four were unprobed), with a per-token loop that fails when one is missing. `answerAtDoor` resolves the route and requires the RESULT to stay under `/api/` on this origin, which is what its comment always claimed — `startsWith` admitted `/api/../evil`.
- **D11 — `scriptedFetch`'s fail-loud design does not reach reads, and that is now recorded rather than discovered again.** `api.ts` catches everything `fetch` raises and turns it into `Unreachable`, which `useLive` renders — so an unscripted READ inside a hook becomes an error state on screen instead of a failed test (B6-7's own F9 hit this). The image and binary surfaces' comment reads are scripted, and a test now asserts those surfaces render the real comment state rather than "The control plane is unreachable".
- **⚠ REFERRAL, adjacent to D10 — RAISED HERE, RULED ON BY THE COORDINATOR AND CLOSED AT DRAIN R2 R9 (see below).** The object route streamed without the hash re-check `review.Store.readObject` performs ("content is never trusted from disk without the pin check"), so a drifted object was served with a now-correct length rather than refused with the `content_drift` answer the handler already gives for missing bytes. This round's cost estimate — hashing on every read being "a real cost for the image trio, which re-reads the same objects once per mode" — was **pessimistic on both halves**, and r2 R9 measured why. The referral was raised rather than silently chosen, which is the part that was right.
- **Counters after drain r1:** unchanged from part A except the web suite and the bundle — migrations **0001–0020**, inventory **99/5/104**, ⚙ **118/33** byte-unchanged, `components.lock` **29** / **240** npm, `package.json`/`package-lock.json` byte-unchanged, catalog **50**, escape-scan allowlist **EMPTY** with the banned set at **8** and the floor at 42 · **44 Go packages** green · web **325 vitest / 19 files** (was 316/19) + typecheck + build. The bundle is **`951.73 kB`** (gzip **`286.71`**) after the round, measured — the sandbox/referrer attributes and the guarded parse cost `1.21 kB`. **$0**, hermetic, nothing pushed.

#### §45 drain round 2 (P3-B6-8 part A drain r2, R1–R9 — FINAL round under the cap)

Nine residuals from an independent re-check that corrected its own earlier report. **Two of them are the same failure mode: a drain-r1 correction that landed in this file and never reached the code it was about** — for a declared downgrade the assertion site is the only place that matters, so a bookkeeping-only correction was not one. Every code fix carries a check that FAILS on the pre-fix code, each verified by reverting it and watching the assertion fire; R1/R2/R5 are record corrections at their assertion sites, where the check is the fact each now cites (named below). Nothing already closed was weakened: D1–D7 and D9–D14 were re-run, and the two whose DRIVERS this round changed (D5's assertion form, D10's length driver) are re-driven rather than dropped.

- **R1 — D8's correction never reached the assertion site, and now does.** `deliverable.test.tsx`'s header still told a reader that a real applied accept "needs a git remote, a broker push and a collision engineered against a moving HEAD" — the exact claim `internal/accept/accept_test.go` refutes. Verified this round: `TestAcceptEndToEnd` applies a real accept against a `t.TempDir` bare remote through the **real broker server + client** (not a fake), `TestAcceptCollisionMergeCard` engineers the collision, and a `fakePusher` is composed in `internal/api`'s fixture world besides — so none of the three is the obstacle. The header now carries the TRUE reason, the one §45's snapshot-pin bullet already gave: a real accept mints a commit whose **sha and timestamps are wall-clock**, and neither can live in a fixed-clock golden body.
- **R2 — D12's correction likewise.** The header said "the **two** object-surface DETAILS … a third and fourth committed detail would prove nothing" while `doubles.ts` derives **three** — `imageDetail`, `binaryDetail`, `notebookDetail`, three `objectDetail(` call sites, counted. It names all three and says a FOURTH would prove nothing.
- **R3 — D5's recorded mechanism was INVERTED, and the assertion built on it had a blind spot that passed silently.** Both fixed; see the corrected D5 bullet above for the mechanism (§44-B drain r1 D7's rule: the first subscriber opens the source and reads once, a joiner reads twice — child-ness is not the variable, and `/chat`'s two sibling hooks at 1 and 2 are the counter-example). **The assertion is now a literal `2` with the §44-B pointer as its reason**, because §44-B pins that number as landed, demonstrated behaviour — a literal is a claim the repo proves, not a claim about the framework. **Both failures of the old comparison form were reproduced, not argued:** with a second `/api/previews` subscriber AND a doubled comments mount planted, the old `comments === live previews count` form **passed at 4 === 4** while the literal form fires with "4 calls where one subscribed mount costs 2"; and with only the previews subscriber doubled, the old form fails on a change to `TryItBlock` while naming the comment feed. Both `D5:` tests share one named constant, so the number has one home.
- **R4 — the superseded tautological invariant test is DELETED.** Its first half asserted a `[data-comment]` node per served comment, which is a property of `comments.map` running; its second half recomputed the production strip predicate and compared the implementation to a copy of itself. **Proved decorative rather than assumed so:** with the D2 defect re-introduced (`widgetReach` treating "has a line number" as reach), the deleted form **passes** while the surviving DOM-driven `D2/D3` invariant fires naming comment 2. Two tests where one cannot fail invites trusting the wrong one; a comment in its place says where the invariant lives.
- **R5 — a wrong number inside a security premise.** D1's `allow-same-origin` argument cited "this package's **47900-47919** pool" as the loopback preview origin. That is `internal/api`'s TEST range and not a production port at all; the shipping default is `internal/portpool`'s **47600–47619** (`portpool.go:40-41`). The conclusion — a preview is a different origin from the SPA, so the token restores the FRAME's origin — was re-verified against the real range and is unchanged, but a security argument must cite the range that ships. (§45's fixture-world bullet also says 47900-47919 and is CORRECT there: that one is the test world describing itself.)
- **R6 — `diff.go`'s label comment contradicted its own code, twice over.** It claimed a leading separator "would compose `a//abs/path`, so the label is always" repo-relative, but `TrimPrefix` strips exactly **one**: `//abs/path.ts` composed exactly that header and `///x.ts` composed `a///x.ts` (the landed test only ever tried one slash). **`TrimLeft` makes the claim true**, driven over one, two and three separators. And the second half: **`../../etc/passwd` survived verbatim into the header the anchor model keys on, and is now REFUSED** — not as a traversal fix (nothing resolves the label against a filesystem) but because the header IS the file identity, and a label that names something outside the tree the revision describes is the same class of lie the absolute form was. Refusal yields the empty label, which leaves git's own output untouched, exactly as the newline and `" b/"` limbs do. A name that merely contains dots (`site/..hidden/v1.2.ts`) still passes, so it stays a boundary and not a wall.
- **R7 — `apiPath` admitted percent-encoded traversal, which is the one thing its comment claimed it did not.** `new URL` does not decode `%2f`, so `/api/..%2fevil` resolved to a path still under `/api/` while Go's mux — which unescapes and THEN cleans — routes it to `/evil`. The guard's claim is about the RESULT, so it now decodes once (once, because the server decodes once: `%252f` stays a literal there too) and re-resolves before deciding; a malformed escape is refused rather than raised into the caller. `..%2f`, `..%2F`, `%2e%2e/` and `%2e%2e%2f` are all refused, and an ordinary encoded segment (`ask%3Ax`) still passes. **One expectation written for this fix was WRONG and is recorded as such rather than made to pass:** `/api/%2f%2fevil.invalid/x` is **admitted**, correctly — decoded it is `/api///evil.invalid/x`, which the mux cleans to `/api/evil.invalid/x`, still on the machine surface. It is a route that does not exist, not a route that escapes; only the `..` families leave.
- **R8 — the scan header's count is FIVE, and the two framings now agree.** `escape-scan.test.ts` said "FOUR landed texts" and enumerated five (its own header, its own assertion message, §41-B, §43, §44-B) — the off-by-one **preserved** from the original, which said THREE and enumerated four. §45's OQ1 bullet framed the same set as "three". The enumeration was always the complete part, so the count moved to five in both places, and drain r1's D10 clause is corrected to five with the reason it was four.
- **R9 — THE REFERRAL, IMPLEMENTED: the object route VERIFIES BEFORE IT SERVES.** A content-addressed route answering with bytes that hash to something other than the sha in its own URL is the response contradicting its own identity — a correctness question, not a hardening preference, and the same family as honest-absence-over-fabrication. Ruled on by the coordinator and implemented as a **narrow exported verified read on `internal/review`**, `OpenObjectVerified`, ratified as a further sanctioned-narrow touch of that package on the same grounds as the diff-header deviation: it is the package that owns object identity. No new vocabulary — a drifted object takes the same **`content_drift`** posture (500) the handler already gave for missing bytes, and the same one `reviewErr` gives for a drifted revision.
  - **Measured, because r1's cost estimate was the reason it was deferred.** Streaming sha256 on the build host runs **3.0–3.9 GB/s**: **68 µs** for a 200 KiB image, 0.56 ms for 2 MiB, 5.4 ms for 20 MiB. And the image trio's "re-reads the same objects once per mode" is **already suppressed by the route's own `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable`** — which is honest precisely because the sha in the path is the hash of the bytes.
  - **The cheapest honest posture, and why it is this one rather than a `ReadFile`.** The hash is taken over the **already-open handle** with `io.Copy` and the handle is then rewound, so it is **constant-memory** and — the part that matters more — what was verified and what is served are the **same inode**: an object replaced by path between two passes cannot slip through, which a verify-then-reopen would not give. `ServeContent` still streams from that handle, so HEAD and Range keep working.
  - **Driven by actually drifting a stored object**, as directed: the manifest row is immutable by trigger, so the BYTES on disk move under a hash that still names the old ones. The route answers 500 `content_drift`, **not one drifted byte reaches the caller**, and a sibling object still serves — so the refusal is about those bytes and not a route that stopped working. Probe: with the verify reverted to a plain `os.Open` the test fires with `got 200`.
  - **D10 is re-driven, not dropped.** Its old driver WAS the drift that is now a refusal, so the length claim is carried by a **Range read**: 206, `Content-Range: bytes 8-13/14`, `Content-Length: 6`, plus a HEAD carrying the full length with an empty body. Probe: with `ServeContent` replaced by a hand-set `ref.Size` length plus `io.Copy`, it fires with `got 200` and the whole body.
