- **REPORT — the bundle moved by `0.15 kB`, measured:** `951.73 kB` (gzip `286.71`) → **`951.88 kB`** (gzip **`286.74`**). The only client change with weight is R7's decode-and-re-resolve in `apiPath`; R1/R2/R3/R4/R8 are comments and tests, which do not ship. Vite's 500 kB warning still fires, as it has since before B6-7, for the reason recorded there.

#### §45 post-cap coordinator items (P3-B6-8 part A, R10–R14 — implemented inline; the drain cap was exhausted)

The final re-check returned **PASS** — all of D1–D14 and R1–R9 hold together after four commits over the same files, **nothing was weakened** (assertion counts rose in every touched test file: `deliverable.test.tsx` 238→240, `objects_test.go` 25→32, `anchor_internal_test.go` 35→39, the escape scan unchanged at 10), and **the Range path was verified closed rather than assumed** — ten ranged shapes against a drifted object, all refused, no drifted byte out, a clean sibling still serving. Five nit residuals survived; the cap was spent, so the coordinator implemented them.

- **R13 — the corrected reason was right in its conclusion and wrong in its premise, which is exactly the thing R1 existed to fix.** The replacement sentence claimed a real accept produces a commit "whose sha **and timestamps** are WALL-CLOCK". The timestamp half is false in this world: `internal/project/accept.go` records `When` as UTC "for determinism", `internal/accept/accept.go` passes `s.when` from `eff.ApprovedTS`, and the fixture world pins the journal clock — so that half would in fact be stable. What is genuinely unpinnable is the **sha**, and for a reason one level down: `internal/accept/helpers_test.go` seeds the base commit with no `GIT_AUTHOR_DATE`/`GIT_COMMITTER_DATE`, so the PARENT's sha varies per run and the accept sha varies with it. The reason now says that and says the timestamp half would be stable. **The downgrade decision never depended on the false half** — it stands on the sha alone — but a justification that is only accidentally correct is not a justification, and this one had already been rewritten once for the same reason.
- **R14 — "Three families are DERIVED" over four bullets → "Four".** Pre-existing (the same line reads identically at `4a44f72`), and named anyway because it is the **count-vs-enumeration defect R2 and R8 were each chartered to eliminate, sitting in the very paragraph R2 edited**. Fixing two instances of a class and leaving the third in the same file is how a class survives.
- **R10 — `OpenObjectVerified`'s comment narrowed to the claim it can actually make.** It said an object replaced between the verify and the serve "cannot slip through"; that is true for a **rename** — which is the mechanism `putObject` uses, and the reason the open-handle shape was chosen — and false for an in-place `pwrite` to the same inode, which changes bytes already verified with nothing here to see it. The comment now says both halves, and says why the narrower claim is the honest one: objects are written once by rename and never reopened for writing, so the gap is a filesystem the platform does not have. A guarantee stated wider than its mechanism is the kind of sentence a later reader builds on.
- **R11 — RECORDED, NOT FIXED (landed shared machinery).** An object-route failure logs under the label `"intake surface"`, because `writeSurface` (`internal/api/intake_handlers.go`) hardcodes that string for **every** family that uses it — so this route is one of many mislabelled, and it is pre-existing. Fixing it properly is a one-line change where all the families can be weighed together; fixing it *locally* would mean this one route logging differently from every sibling, trading a cosmetic label for a real inconsistency. Left alone on the same reasoning as §44-B's double-read and queued-`.sort()` calls, and carried to the B6 gate list.
- **R12 — ACCEPTED with its reason.** `diffLabel` refuses `..` segments but passes `.` through (`./rel.ts` → `a/./rel.ts`). `..` is refused because the label lands in headers the anchor model keys on, where a traversal segment would be a path claim; a `.` segment is a cosmetic redundancy in a string that never touches the filesystem. No action, stated rather than left as an apparent oversight.
- **One movement the re-check flagged that the coordinator is recording rather than reversing:** deleting R4's decorative invariant moved a floor nominally from `toBeGreaterThan(1)` to the survivor's `toBeGreaterThan(0)`. On the re-check's own evidence this is not a coverage loss — the surviving invariant quantifies over all served comments, keeps the `toBeGreaterThan(4)` fixture floor, and its complement check makes a degenerate strip detectable — but it is a literal 1→0 and it happened inside a deletion, which is where a weakening would hide.
- **What this section cannot claim.** These five were implemented after the cap, so **no fresh adversarial pass reviewed them**. Four are prose; one is a single word. None changes behaviour, which is why the coordinator took them inline rather than opening a third round.
- **Counters after the post-cap items:** unchanged — migrations **0001–0020** (no 0021; it stays reserved for B6-9) · inventory **99/5/104** · ⚙ **118/33** · `components.lock` **29** / **240** npm · conformance **12** · fixture `cursor` **64**, zero drift · escape allowlist **EMPTY**, banned **8**, floor **42** · **44 Go packages** green · web **324 vitest / 19 files** · `/workforce` still carried its `B6-8` stub AT THIS POINT, part B being unbuilt until 2026-07-30 (§45-B). **$0**, hermetic.

### §45-B — the S15.10 workforce map (P3-B6-8 part B; S15.10, S08.1/S08.2/S08.4/S08.7/S08.8/S08.9, S15.2, §40-C, §41+§41-B, §42+§42-B, §45)

The **last build work of B6**. `/workforce` goes live — `routes.ts`'s `owner: 'B6-8'` → `''` and `workforce` into `routes.test.ts`'s `built`, which is now **11 ids: the whole table minus `not-found`** — and **the pattern, the title and the nav placement do not move** (§41-B fill-never-rename). **No route in the SPA is unbuilt any more**, which is a stronger statement than the one it replaces and is asserted in three places rather than implied. NO migration (0001–0020, `user_version` **20**, 0021 still RESERVED for B6-9), NO new ⚙ key, NO new event type and NO new dependency — and a view-only map performs no act, so it has no audit row to name.

- **TBD-P3(workforce-map rendering approach) — RESOLVED 2026-07-29, recorded here as the dated note the S19.5 TBD row asks for.** The map ships on **OWNED rendering**: semantic HTML and CSS grouped by domain and kind, cards, the §42 view idiom — plus owned inline SVG only if a connection drawing were ever genuinely needed, which it was not. The grounds, in the order they bind: the roster is **household-scale** (S08.8's own "~15 active candidates" line) and **EMPTY on a fresh host** (compose-when-earned, S08.6); the surface is **view-only with no interaction physics** (S15.10 parks editing to 15.5); and a graph-layout library of the reactflow/dagre/elkjs class would be a **NEW adoption needing the full S16.4 rail plus gate ratification** — indefensible for rendering an empty-to-tiny read-only list. **No unratified dependency entered:** `components.lock` is 29 entries / 240 npm and `web/package.json` + `package-lock.json` are byte-unchanged. A layout dependency remains a coordinator/operator matter and was never an executor decision to take.
- **OQ3 as landed — a narrow exported `Roster` read in `internal/worker` (`roster.go`), served at `GET /api/workforce`.** This is a **REPORTED widening** of a leaf package, declared and not silent, on the `internal/memory/browse.go` precedent. It is there rather than in the transport for a reason that is structural, not stylistic: a worker's equipment lives in its version's **hash-verified FILE**, and the walk that reads one — `activeDefinition` — performs the S08.3 tamper check on every read. A transport assembling that from SQL would be a second reader of one identity (the §40-C D3 twin hazard) and a reader with no tamper check at that. The **delivery policy** has the same property, so the roster CALLS `Store.Delivery` rather than re-deriving degraded-mode consequences beside it: one expression of S08.7, two consumers.
  - **The visibility line, and why it is deliberately NOT memory's.** `browse.go` has no operator limb, because house authority (D10) is the right to curate the house scope and not a read into somebody's personal store. A **worker is different in kind**: it is audited platform machinery whose approval, promotion, guardrails and domain maturity are all D10 operator acts, so **the operator reads the whole registry** — the runs/telemetry posture (§30). The content-vs-telemetry line still binds conversations (§44) and personal memory (§40-C); it does not reach the machinery that does the work. A member gets S15.10's own rule: **own personal workers plus the household roster**. `rosterVisible` is the ONE expression of that predicate, and a roster read with no viewer is REFUSED rather than answered unscoped.
  - **Driven three ways, and the member limb is the one it exists for.** op / alice / bob against one world, asymmetric in both directions: alice sees the household worker and her own draft and NOT bob's personal automation; bob sees the household worker and his own automation and NOT alice's draft. The non-tautological control runs the same query with the member predicate DEFEATED and asserts bob's automation then reaches alice — without it the three-way test could have been passing because the world happens to carry what it carries.
- **OQ4 as landed — the version→outcome join is DERIVED at the transport, and it is not a catalog query.** S08.4 says "version→outcome is a JOIN, not a system", so this is a bounded scan of `routing.decided` rows that name a worker VERSION ⋈ that run's `verdict.recorded` rows ⋈ the metering seam, on the §42-B decisions-block precedent: it spans the worker registry, the verify family and metering, and none of those is the others' home. **The Layer-1 catalog did not move** — it sits at its 30–50 band ceiling (50/50) and a query there is a gate matter, not this read's. Layer-0 views stay **11**, the catalog stays **50**.
  - **MONEY IS READ, PER RUN, AND NEVER TOTALLED.** Each routed run carries the figures `MeterReader.RunMeter` served and nothing else. There is **no per-version total and no per-worker total**, on the fleet's own reasoning (§42-B): a cross-run sum would be arithmetic nobody performed, and it answers a question this platform does not ask, because each person's account burns separately (D2). The shapes carry no `total`/`sum`/`average`/`aggregate` field for a reader to mistake for one, checked over a REAL served body by a KEY-position regex — a worker's task class is legitimately `summarize`, and a scan that read a value as a field name would be noise rather than a check (§42's path-vs-division lesson in a second shape). Probes in both directions.
  - **⚠ THE FIRST CUT SERVED A FABRICATED ZERO IN PRODUCTION AND PASSED EVERY TEST — found by an adversarial pass, and it is the packet's most useful finding.** `err == nil` was read as "there is a reading". It is not: `Ledger.RunConsumption` FOLDS a run's checkpoint rows, so a run that exists and has recorded no usage yet — **every run between its routing decision and its first checkpoint**, which is the state the newest rows are in — folds to zero tokens, zero cost and zero unpriced calls **successfully**. The surface would have printed `USD 0` for work nobody has measured, which is the exact fabrication §37 exists to forbid. It was invisible because `fixtureMeter` had only TWO arms — a hardcoded figure and an error — so the third, and commonest, production state had no fixture at all. **The fix is in two places and the fixture half is the load-bearing one:** `readMeter` renders an entirely empty reading as the absence it is ("no usage recorded for this run yet"), distinct from a refusal ("no meter reading for this run") because they are different facts; and `fixtureMeter` gained the empty arm for `r-claim`, whose state is `claimed` and which has therefore genuinely run nothing. All three arms are now in the committed bodies, and the probe fires with `got USD 0`. **Recorded as a class, not an incident:** a seam double whose arms are narrower than the seam's real behaviour makes every consumer of it untestable, and this one had been narrow since B6-5.
  - **TWO SCOPES, SERVED AS SENTENCES, because they genuinely differ.** The ROSTER scope is the worker's (household or own); the OUTCOME scope is **the RUN's owner** — a member reading the shared worker sees her own routed runs and not bob's, because another person's run and another person's money are not hers to read (the `readPerson` rule). `roster_scope` and `outcome_scope` ride the body as text, so a narrower reading can never pose as the wider one, and both are rendered on screen. Driven: the operator's reading of the same version is strictly wider than the member's, and the version whose only run is somebody else's renders an **explained** absence rather than an unexplained empty.
  - **FOUR BOUNDS, EACH ON A DIFFERENT COST, and one of them is not about response size at all.** `workforceRosterCap` (200) is the page a person reads. `workforceRoutingCap` (1000) bounds the SQL scan, because the control plane shares ONE writer connection (S02.1). `workforceRunsPerVersion` (20) bounds what one VERSION serves — without it a single hot version consumes the scan budget and every other version reads as "never routed to", which is the shape of lie this surface exists to avoid. And **`workforceMeterCap` (100) bounds the METER fan-out**, which is the expensive part of the whole read and was the first cut's real cost: `Ledger.RunConsumption` opens a **write transaction per run** on that same single connection, so an unbounded loop over routed runs would have been up to a thousand serialized write transactions per page load, on a view that re-reads on thirteen event types. Beyond the bound a row says its figure was not read rather than carrying a number nobody took, and the reading is memoized per run id so a run routed twice is metered once.
  - **THE VERDICT SCAN IS BOUNDED PER RUN, NOT PER REQUEST, and that is what makes "no verdict recorded for this run yet" a sentence a reader can trust.** A global `LIMIT n` over a multi-run scan lets one talkative run consume it, and the runs it starves then render an absence the READING invented — a false statement about verified work rather than a smaller one. A `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY run_id …)` keeps every run's newest rounds reachable regardless of what its siblings carry, and a run whose own rounds were cut says so. Driven: 25 rounds on one run, all newer than a sibling's single round, and the sibling still gets it — with the probe reverting to the global bound and watching the sibling's verdict disappear.
- **OQ5 as landed — "helpers" is a structural statement, and nothing is invented.** S15.10 names "(tools, knowledge, permissions, helpers)"; the **S08.1 schema has no helper field**, because helper selection is per RUN through the same routing pipeline that picked the worker (S08.8 step 5). So the card renders the registry's own vocabulary — tools / skills / knowledge selectors / connectors as REQUESTED, the guardrails row as GRANTED — and says out loud that there is no standing helper roster, pointing at where a helper spawn really is visible: **the `helper-spawn` routing cause on a routed run**, which the outcome rows already carry. A test asserts the served body contains no `helpers` key at all, so "no roster rendered" means the schema has none rather than that the view dropped one.
- **OQ7 as landed — "multi-stage connections" grounds to the registry's own multi-stage data.** A `kind=automation` worker renders its parsed `automation.Workflow` chain: the dialect, the ONE named service, and the ordered steps with their **explicit approval nodes marked** — an outward step becomes a gated proposal and never a direct call (S08.9; D7). An agentic worker has no chain, so what renders instead is what actually connects it to work: its selectors, task classes, trigger phrases and duty/effort profile. The S06 task pipeline stays B6-5's story. Both arms exist in the fixture, so "marked" means something: the read step is unmarked and the outward one is marked, and a defect that marked every step fires the test.
  - **STEP ARGS ARE DELIBERATELY NOT ON THE WIRE.** The map renders how the stages connect; an arg is a value flowing between them. Leaving them out keeps a view-only audit surface from becoming a window onto payload content, and a test asserts no `args` key reaches the body.
- **⚠ ONE ADDITIVE SEAM IN `internal/worker`, DECLARED: `Config.NewID`.** `CreateDraft` and `insertVersion` MINT their ids from `crypto/rand`, so a golden fixture produced through the real verbs would carry a fresh random string on every run — and R18 requires those verbs, because a worker's equipment is parsed from a file the store itself wrote and a hand-seeded row would point at a file nothing wrote. The alternative was rewriting the minted ids across six tables plus a file path afterwards, which is surgery on the thing being tested. This is the **`internal/chat` precedent verbatim** (B6-7 added the same seam for the same reason, beside the same clock seam), nil = the existing generator, **production behaviour unchanged, and no caller can choose an id**. Nothing else in `internal/worker` moved: no verb, no wall, no schema, no exported signature.
- **`dry_run_ref` IS NOT ON THE MAP, and the fixture stability check is what found it.** Both halves are said because one of them was discovered rather than reasoned: `TestWebAPIFixturesAreStable` fired on `/api/workforce`, and the unstable value was the validation record's `dryrun_ref`. For **kind=automation the column is not a pointer at all** — an automation's station-3 dry run has no transcript, so what is stored is the whole `automation.Report` inline, step OUTPUTS and the journaled proposal's UUID included. The reason it is right to DROP rather than pin: that column is the transcript pointer the **S08.6 station-4 approval card** owns, which is where a person acts on it, and a view-only map has no business surfacing a run's outputs. The battery's lint/audit findings are absent for the same reason — validation asserts structure, powers and a witnessed run, explicitly not quality.
- **TWO THINGS THE REVIEW ASKED FOR THAT WERE ANSWERED BY REMOVING CODE RATHER THAN ADDING IT.** (a) An unread S08.7 delivery policy was serializing as the ZERO policy — `requires_review: false`, which tells a reader a worker needs no human review, the one direction this field must never fail in. The fix is not a nullable field: the state is **structurally unreachable**, because `worker_templates.domain` is a foreign key into `domains`, `domains` carries a no-delete trigger (migration 0005), and Delivery's other read is the active version's guardrails, which exist for any version `Approve` or `Repoint` could have pointed at. So the branch that invented a half-answer is gone and the read FAILS instead — handling for a state the schema forbids is handling that can never be driven, and a nil-able field would have been a second unreachable arm. (b) `rows.Err()` is now checked at all three scan sites. `Close()` reports the DRIVER close; the ITERATION error lives only in `Err()`, so a scan that failed mid-way was serving a SHORT roster, version list or outcome set as a complete one — with `truncated:false` beside it, on a read whose entire contract is that an absence means something. The rest of `internal/api` already checked it; this was a deviation from the package's own convention.
- **THE FIXTURE WORLD GREW A REGISTRY, AND EVERY ROW IN IT CAME THROUGH S08's OWN VERBS.** `InstallSkill` → `CreateDomain` → `CreateDraft` → `RunBattery` → `Approve` → `NewVersion` → `Approve` → `Promote`. Three workers, each carrying a render the others cannot:
  - `wt-notes` — agentic, `software` (FULL), **promoted to household by the operator**, two versions (v1 superseded, v2 active). It is what a member reads through the shared limb rather than through ownership, and it is the worker whose two versions carry DIFFERENT outcomes.
  - `wt-digest` — **bob's PERSONAL** automation in `chore`, which `CreateDomain` makes **DEGRADED by construction** (S08.7 maturity honesty). It renders the marked approval node, the degraded consequence, and — being another member's personal worker — it is what must be ABSENT from alice's body.
  - `wt-audit` — alice's DRAFT, battery never run: no active version, no granted enforcement state, no validation record. The honest state of a composed-but-unapproved worker, and a render the map owes, because an empty roster is not the only truthful absence.
  - **The join has real ground on both sides.** Four `routing.decided` rows naming a worker AND a version, across two owners and two versions, plus one `verdict.recorded`. That is what makes the populated arm and the honest-absence arm BOTH driven — a fixture world with no routed run would leave "empty" indistinguishable from "broken", which is the hazard this packet was told to attack — and what makes the member/operator scope difference observable rather than asserted.
  - **The automation's proposal is left standing, and its id pinned.** Station 3 for an automation is a sample-payload execution whose outward step journals a gated proposal (S08.9; D7 makes it free), so the world genuinely acquires one — `approvals.json` gains bob's `effect:e-digest` card, which is the consequence of validating an automation and not an artefact of the fixture. The id is pinned exactly as `e-publish`'s is: the journal mints a UUID no committed body can carry, while the PAYLOAD and its journal-computed hash stay the producer's own.
  - **`seedFixtureWorkforce` runs LAST in `fixtureWorld`, deliberately.** The S08 verbs append their own `worker.*` rows; seeding them after everything else keeps every `event_seq` the other committed bodies carry exactly where it was, so the drift is what the world really gained rather than a renumbering. The routing rows carry each run's OWN stamp, because a routing decision happens when the run is routed.
- **REPORT — the fixture `cursor` MOVED, and it could not have done otherwise.** 64 → **84**, in lockstep across **19** bodies (17 before, plus the two new ones). The launch counters asked for `cursor` still 64 *and* for workers created through the real `CreateDraft`→`Approve` producers; those two are not simultaneously satisfiable, because a producer-driven worker appends real `worker.*` events and the cursor IS the head `event_seq`. The producers won, which is the rule §42's drain r1 recorded as the reusable lesson. **Zero drift through the real regeneration path** after the change: `SINET_WRITE_API_FIXTURES=1` re-run produces no diff.
- **The other committed bodies that moved, and what each move IS. CORRECTED IN PLACE at drain r1 (D2): this sentence was FALSE as first written, because it omitted the one move that was a defect.** `approvals.json` gains the automation's proposal card (above). `tasks.json` / `runs.json` gain `effort_mode` and `last_activity_ts` on the four runs that are now routed — a routed run HAS an effort mode and HAS activity, so the previous nulls were true only because nothing had been routed. **And `tasks.json` flipped `t-claim`'s `cost_so_far_usd` from `null` to `0`** — an honest absence turned into a fabricated figure on a LANDED surface, which the original wording did not mention at all. That one was a defect, not growth; it is fixed at drain r1 and the golden is back to `null`. Every other file moved by `cursor` alone.
- **Per-view declared types (`workforceEventTypes`, §42).** Thirteen, each either what puts something on this page or what takes it off: `worker.template_created` and `worker.version_created` (a row appears), `worker.validated` (the validation record, the dated revalidation stamp, and the release of a flagged version), `worker.approved` (the ONLY writer of the granted guardrails block — it also repoints the active version and seeds first-N), `worker.promoted` (which changes WHO is served this row at all), `worker.repointed` (rollback moves which definition is shown), `worker.flagged` / `worker.retired` (status, and with it the delivery policy), `worker.graduated`, `worker.domain_maturity` (one flip re-marks every worker in a domain), and the join's own three — `routing.decided`, `verdict.recorded`, `usage.recorded` (what moves a routed run's meter reading). **`worker.gap`, `worker.review` and `worker.automation_run` are deliberately NOT declared** — all three are real facts and none changes anything this page renders; a test asserts their absence, which is what makes the list a choice rather than a copy. Driven both ways: a declared frame re-reads, an undeclared one reaches nothing.
- **ONE `useLive` MOUNT, AND THE COUNT IS A LITERAL 1 WITH ITS REASON.** §44-B's drain-r1 D7 rule as §45's R3 restates it: the FIRST subscriber OPENS the source and reads once; a later one JOINS and gets an immediate synchronous `connected` resnapshot, so it reads twice. React commits child effects before the parent's, so this view's hook opens the source and the shell's connection indicator is the joiner — the same ordering the deliverable page's detail hook has. The assertion is the literal with that rule as its reason, never a comparison against another feed's count (the form that passed silently at 4 === 4 in part A). Probe: planting a second `useLive` mount fires it.
- **ZERO MUTATION AFFORDANCES, PROVEN TWO WAYS BECAUSE ONE WAY IS NOT A PROOF.** A **DOM walk** over the whole rendered surface — no `form`, `input`, `textarea`, `select`, submit or `button` anywhere inside it, no link pointing at an act, and not one non-GET request made rendering the entire map — with a planted-element probe per shape so the selectors are shown able to match. And a **source scan** in `scans.test.ts`, because a DOM walk cannot see a handler that never fired: no `post(`, no `onClick`/`onSubmit`, no `<form`, no PUT/PATCH/DELETE, and **exactly one `api.` member reachable from the file** (`api.workforce`). Both scans carry probes; both fire on a planted defect.
- **Forward tolerance, by construction (§42).** The grouping key is the SERVED domain string, so a domain this client has never heard of gets its own group labelled with the value the server sent — a worker must never vanish from the map because a client-side list did not anticipate its domain, the rule the kanban vocabulary already follows. The same holds for an unknown `kind`, an unknown `status`, an unknown `maturity` (which renders the value and does **not** acquire the degraded consequence it never claimed) and an unknown workflow `verb`; and a dialect the platform's own parser refused renders the parser's reason rather than a card that silently shows no stages. All five are driven.
- **One display choice, with its reason** (no ⚙ key exists for it and the browser cannot read the registry before login — the §41-B backoff precedent; interim under the standing settings-tab directive): **superseded versions open COLLAPSED** in a `<details>`, the active version does not. What a worker IS lives on its active version; a superseded one is history, and a wall of permission blocks would bury the thing a reader came for. **No cap and no window:** every list on this surface is bounded by the server, and **the THREE truncation flags** it serves are rendered as statements so a short list is never read as a complete one. (Corrected in place at drain r2: this said "the two", and it was two — the third, `verdict_tally_truncated`, is r2's, because the verdict tally was bounded with nothing on screen to say so.)
- **Landed tests that moved, and why — each is a table that ran out of rows, not a relaxation.** `shell.test.tsx`'s "every unbuilt surface has a reachable stub" **quantified over an empty set** the moment this route flipped, and said so through its own floor; it is replaced by a direct drive of `Stub` — title, owning packet, the captured identity, and **zero API calls** — which is the property it was really checking and which stays true for the day a later packet publishes a route ahead of its surface. `chat.test.tsx`'s built/unbuilt table moves from "the one remaining unbuilt row" to **none**. `routes.test.ts` gains `workforce` to `built` and a second test recording that nothing is unbuilt, while keeping the two-sided assertion that still admits an unbuilt row naming its packet. **Assertion counts per touched file, before → after: `shell.test.tsx` 33→35, `chat.test.tsx` 217→217, `routes.test.ts` 16→18, `escape-scan.test.ts` 10→10, `scans.test.ts` 22→29, `apifixtures_test.go` 62→79, `api_test.go` 38→38.** Nothing went down.
- **`Stub` stays, and App.tsx's own comment is corrected rather than left false.** Every route is built, so the terminal arm is unreachable today — but the URL contract moves BEFORE the surface does (a route is published so deep links and push `navigate` payloads can be written against it), and between those two moments the stub is what answers. Both files now say that instead of the "the workforce map is still a named stub" sentence part A left behind.
- **Escape posture unchanged, and the floor names the new view.** The allowlist stays **EMPTY**, the banned set stays at **8** with all eight probed, and the floor moves **42 → 44** naming `./Workforce.tsx` — which is inside the scan by name because it renders worker DEFINITIONS: a description, a selector set, tool and connector names and a routing reason, all of which can be composer-written, which is model output. No iframe, no `srcdoc`, nothing raw.
- **Counters at part B:** migrations **0001–0020** (`user_version` **20**, none opened — **0021 stays RESERVED for B6-9**) · event inventory **99 minted / 5 declare-only / 104 registered**, `internal/eventlog/contract.go` byte-unchanged (no type minted) · ⚙ **118 keys / 33 domains**, `internal/settings/index.go` byte-unchanged · `components.lock` **29** / **240** npm · **`web/package.json` + `package-lock.json` + `go.mod` + `go.sum` byte-unchanged** · conformance **12** · Layer-0 views **11**, catalog **50** (no query added — OQ4 is a transport derive, not a catalog entry) · `internal/review`, `internal/accept`, `internal/preview`, `internal/history`, `internal/settings`, `internal/benchmark/registered.go`, BENCH-REG byte-unchanged (the Go edits are exactly six files: `internal/worker/roster.go` + `store.go` + `lifecycle.go`, `internal/api/workforce.go` + `api.go`, `internal/shell/shell.go`, plus tests and fixtures) · escape-scan allowlist **EMPTY**, banned set **8**, floor **44** · **44 Go packages** green (gofmt empty, vet + build clean, no skips introduced) · web **352 vitest / 20 files** (was 324/19) + typecheck + build · **zero fixture drift** through the real regeneration path, `cursor` **84** in lockstep across **19** bodies · `Spec/`, `Docs/`, `Research/`, `P3/STATE.md` untouched. **$0**, hermetic, nothing pushed, no live-Caddy touch.
- **REPORT — the bundle moved by `16.07 kB`, measured:** `951.88 kB` (gzip `286.74`) → **`967.95 kB`** (gzip **`290.32`**). It is all view code — no dependency entered — which is the shape a surface rendering a registry's full provenance, permission and outcome blocks takes. Vite's 500 kB warning still fires, as it has since before B6-7, for the reason recorded there: the SPA is embedded in the binary and served over a tailnet, so nothing is code-split.
- **B6-9 is what remains.** Declarative Web Push + re-nag, PWA completion, the S15.12 completeness sweep, the escape AUDIT and the final "the SPA consumes every API" check. The stable URL this part landed is what it will deep-link, and migration **0021** is still its own.

#### §45-B drain round 1 (P3-B6-8 part B drain r1, D1–D11 + two dispositions) — corrections, and one defect class that repeated inside its own fix

An independent evaluation returned FAIL with 13 findings. **Two executor positions were upheld and closed:** the `cursor` 64→84 deviation (the cursor IS the head `event_seq`, so producer-driven workers necessarily advance it, and "cursor 64" and "real producers" cannot both hold) and `Config.NewID` (both mint sites converted, nil defaults to the existing generator, production injects nothing). Every fix below carries a check that FAILS on the pre-fix code, verified by reverting the fix and watching the assertion fire; the two prose-only items say so. **CORRECTED IN PLACE at drain r2: that sentence was a blanket claim and it was not true of D5's SERVER half** — forcing `ScheduleAttachable: false` in `workforce.go` passed every `-run TestWorkforce` test and only the golden-drift check fired, which is a real net but not a localized one. Drain r2 adds the localized assertion (`TestWorkforceScheduleBarIsAServedFactOnEveryGrantedBlock`), so the sentence is true as written now rather than by charity.

- **D1 — THE PACKET'S OWN HEADLINE FINDING, SHIPPED AGAIN THREE LINES AWAY.** §45-B calls the fabricated-zero discovery "the packet's most useful finding" — and the same commit rendered `Budget: USD 0` in **every granted block on the surface**, from its own committed golden. `worker_guardrails` stores both ceilings NOT NULL with a `>= 0` check and `Approve` copies the version's REQUEST verbatim, so a version that asked for no ceiling is granted **0** — and the registry's own request type says exactly that, carrying `omitempty` on both fields. The platform's precedent was already in this package (`api.go`: "no operator budget is persisted anywhere … the ABSENCE is what the meters surface serves — never a zero", S10.1). **Fixed at the boundary:** both ceilings are nil-able on the wire, nil when the row stores 0, and the view renders the reason. **The guard was pointed away from where the defect landed** — `not.toMatch(/USD\s*0\b/)` was scoped to `.routed-cost` cells only — and now quantifies over **every** money span the surface produces. The fixture drives both arms for real: v2 of the release-notes writer REQUESTS ceilings and the automation requests none, so the figure and the absence are both produced by `Approve` rather than by a synthesized body. Probes: serving the stored 0 fires `TestWorkforceUndeclaredCeilingIsAnAbsenceNotAZero`, and the re-pointed span scan catches it in jsdom.
- **D2 — the same defect, flipped the other way, on a LANDED surface.** The new `fixtureMeter` arm (`r-claim` → zeros with no error) is consumed by `reads.go`, which treated `err == nil` as "there is a reading" — the identical defect fixed in `readMeter` one file away — so `tasks.json`'s `cost_so_far_usd` for `r-claim` went `null` → **`0`** and the `/board` card began rendering `Cost so far USD 0` where it had rendered an honest absence. The whole web suite passed in both directions; nothing noticed. **Fixed at the consumer, not by narrowing the double back** (narrowing would recreate the untestable-seam problem §45-B already records): `meterReading` on the projector is now **THE** expression of "does this run have a cost reading", and `reads.go`, `projection.go` and `workforce.go` all go through it. `RunCounters.APIEquivCostUSD` became nil-able with it — tokens stay a plain counter beside it, because a monotonic count at zero is a true reading while a money zero is a price nobody set. Ripple swept: one golden (`run-detail.json`, unchanged — `r-ship` has a real reading), one web type, one render site already using `<Money>`. The §45-B sentence describing what moved **was false as written and is corrected in place** above. Probe: `TestMeterReadingIsOneExpressionAcrossEverySurface` fires when the helper returns `true` unconditionally, and it checks all three surfaces answer the same way for the same run — plus the other direction, that a run WITH a reading still shows it. **THE RIPPLE SWEEP WAS INCOMPLETE, corrected in place at drain r2 (R3/R4):** "all three surfaces answer the same way" held for the ABSENCE and not for the reading. `WorkforceRoutedRun` carried the seam's `Unpriced` marking and `RunCounters` did not, so an unpriced run still printed a bare `USD 0` on the run card — the fabrication class, one surface over — and the helper's own discriminator had a sign-flipped false negative besides. Both are r2's R3 and R4.
- **D3 — the chain was served without its connections, on a premise the dialect contradicts.** The comment said "an arg is a value flowing between them, not a connection". A `{"$from":"steps.fetch.summary"}` arg is not a runtime value: it is a **definition-time edge**, and in the fixture it is the ONLY place `post`'s dependency on `fetch` is written down — so the served chain was a sequence with approval flags and no connections at all, under-delivering the one thing S15.10 names ("how multi-stage procedures connect"). **Serialized selectively:** `$from` references are kept as `needs` (argument name → reference), literals are dropped, because a reference is a connection and a literal is a value. The false premise is corrected at the comment, and `workforce_test.go`'s assertion — which pinned the wrong reasoning by requiring no `args` key at all — now asserts the two edges by name and keeps the no-raw-args check as what it really is. Probes: dropping `needs` fires the chain test; keeping literals fires `TestWorkforceStepReferencesKeepEdgesAndDropLiterals`, which drives a definition carrying one of each. **CORRECTED IN PLACE at drain r2 — the replacement comment was itself false, in both directions, and the fix narrowed the defect instead of ending it.** It claimed "the shape is the dialect's own (automation.resolveArgs)" while running a stricter decoder of its own, so a legal edge was still dropped and a literal was still drawn as an edge; see R1 below. The conclusion of this entry stands; its premise did not, which is exactly the failure mode a correction has to avoid.
- **D4 — a worker that FAILED its S08.3 integrity check rendered none of its granted powers.** The permissions block lived inside the `definition !== null` arm, so a tamper failure hid exactly the half an auditor needs: the powers are recompiled from the guardrails TABLE on every invocation (S08.2) and are unaffected by an unreadable file. A person learned the file cannot be trusted and not what the worker is still allowed to do. **The two are separate renders now** — the REQUESTED equipment goes absent with the file, because that is where it lives; the GRANTED block does not. Probe: re-gating the block fires the test, which drives a single-version worker with `Bash`/`WebFetch`/`C3`/`unrestricted` — grants that appear nowhere else in the body, so no sibling can satisfy it.
- **D5 — S08.7's third consequence was missing, and the card could contradict it.** S08.7 names three; the marking rendered two. Meanwhile the granted block rendered `Attachable to a schedule: yes` unconditionally from a row `Approve` copies **unclamped** from the request — so a degraded-domain worker that requested attachability could say "yes" beneath a marking that never stated the bar. The third consequence is now in the marking, and the schedule row carries the bar when the domain is degraded. **The fixture had to reach it:** all three workers granted `false`, so the automation now REQUESTS `ScheduleAttachable` and the contradiction is produced by the real verb. Probes: removing either half fires the test, and the full-domain worker's row is asserted to carry no bar so the wording is a consequence rather than a constant.
- **D6 — one sentence for two different facts.** "no routed run in this reading" was served both for a version nobody has ever routed to and for one whose only run belongs to another owner, while §45-B claimed the latter renders an **explained** absence. It explained nothing. **Now they differ:** the operator's reading covers every owner, so its empty answer is about the version ("no run has been routed to this version"); a member's covers her own runs, so its empty answer is about what she ran ("no run of yours has been routed to this version"). That is also the most a member's answer may honestly say — "there is a run here you cannot see" would disclose the existence of another owner's run to the person the scope exists to withhold it from — and the test asserts the member's wording carries no such disclosure. Probe: collapsing the two arms fires `TestWorkforceEmptyOutcomeSentencesDifferByReading`.
- **D7 — the meter budget was spent in randomized map order.** `for ver, rows := range grouped` gates on the cap, so past 100 distinct runs WHICH rows carried a figure varied between identical requests — the determinism family — and unlike this surface's three other bounds it was not newest-first. The join now walks the scan's own order (globally newest-first) and applies the per-version bound in it, so the budget is spent newest-first and the map walk is gone. Probe: `TestWorkforceMeterBudgetIsSpentNewestFirstAndDeterministically` seeds 140 routed runs — the cap is unobservable below it — and fires on the map-walk shape with "kept an older row over a newer one".
- **D8 — PROSE ONLY, no code change, and the honest exception to the probe rule.** "which is what keeps a roster of N workers from becoming 2N queries" is true of the JOIN and of nothing else. The roster path is frankly an N+1: per template a domain read, a delivery policy that re-reads the template, and a versions query; per version guardrails, latest validation and latest revalidation. It is left that way deliberately — household scale, and the alternative is hand-written joins duplicating reads the registry owns — so the comment now says what the path is and that **the real bound is the caps, not the query count**. Bolting a grep-for-a-sentence guard onto a prose fix would be a check that tests nothing.
- **D9 — the new card, and the witness it quietly weakened.** `effect:e-digest` is the first committed card with `card.kind: "automation-step"`; nothing asserted on it, and `inbox.test.tsx`'s `toContain('effect')` — previously witnessed uniquely by `e-notify` — became satisfiable by either. **The kind checks are now witnessed BY ID**, both of them, so a card cannot disappear behind its sibling; and a new test pins what the automation-step proposal actually renders through the generic fallback (the verb, the step and the service all reach the screen, beside its High tier and its `approve`/`deny` actions). **B6-6's card vocabulary was NOT grown from inside this packet** — the legibility of the generic fallback is the coordinator's gate question; what this adds is the evidence to decide it against. Probe: renaming `e-notify` in the golden fires the witness assertion.
- **D10 — a §45-B claim made true.** `fxPinDryRunProposal` pinned by `ORDER BY rowid DESC LIMIT 1` while §45-B said "pinned exactly as `e-publish`'s is", and `e-publish` pins by explicit id. It now selects by the proposal's own identity (service + step + verb, unique within a workflow). It failed safely today and would have pinned the wrong effect the moment a second automation joined this world. Probe: pointing the identity at the wrong step fires `TestWebAPIFixtures`.
- **D11 — two of R14's three named readings were missing.** R14 asks for "runs routed, verdict outcomes, cost figures". Only the third was served. Added: `runs_routed`, counted BEFORE the per-version bound so it stays true when the list is cut and scoped like everything else (a member's count is her runs, asserted strictly narrower than the operator's), and `verdict_tally`, the recorded rounds per verdict value, sorted so two identical requests answer identically. **The line held: a count of rows is a reading; money is not summed.** No total, no average, no derived money — the aggregate key scan still passes and still probes both directions. Probes: dropping either reading, or letting the count escape the owner scope, each fire `TestWorkforceServesRunsRoutedAndVerdictOutcomes`. **CORRECTED IN PLACE at drain r2 (R2): both readings were wrong, and "a count of rows is a reading" is exactly how.** `runs_routed` counted routing DECISIONS while its name, its Go doc and its render all said runs — a run re-routed to one version reported as two — and the tally re-counted a duplicated row's rounds AND was silently bounded by `workforceVerdictsPerRun`. A count whose name does not describe what it counts is the fabricated-figure class in another currency; both are fixed in r2 and neither claim above should be read as having been true when it was written.
- **F9 — ACCEPTED, recorded as a known blast-radius property.** A `Domain` or `Delivery` error in `rosterEntry` fails the WHOLE map rather than degrading one worker. That is deliberate and it is safe for a structural reason: `worker_templates.domain` is a foreign key into `domains`, `domains` carries a no-delete trigger (migration 0005), `Delivery`'s guardrails read is guarded by `ActiveVersion`, and `Repoint` requires an approved version — so neither read can be missing for a template that exists. Handling a state the schema forbids is barred by the scope guardrail, and the alternative (a nil-able policy) would have been a second unreachable arm whose only possible default said `requires_review: false`.
- **F12 — THE OPERATOR LIMB RESTS ON A READING, and the reading is recorded rather than asserted flat.** S15.10's parenthetical — "personal workers to their owner, the shared roster to all" — describes the ordinary identity scoping; the coordinator's ruling is that it does not silently repeal S01.9's platform-wide role model, and that worker identity is already operator-visible through `routing.decided` on the runs surfaces, so hiding the roster would be a fig leaf rather than a boundary. **The OQ3 disposition stands on that reading, not on the sentence alone**, and it is carried to the B6 gate as an explicit operator ratification item. The evaluator was right to raise it: OQ3's own text invited the flag.

#### §45-B drain round 2 (P3-B6-8 part B drain r2, R1–R11 — FINAL round under the cap)

An independent re-check confirmed both HIGHs closed and not coming back, and eleven residuals survived. All eleven are actioned below; **R10 is accepted with its grounds and deliberately not changed**. Every fix carries a check that FAILS on the pre-fix code, each verified by reverting the fix and watching the assertion fire — the probe is named per item. **R9 is prose only and says so.** Three of these are the same defect as this packet's own headline finding in a different currency, and two of them were introduced by the fix for the first one.

- **R1 — THE STEP-EDGE PREDICATE WAS A SECOND OPINION, AND IT WAS WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** Drain r1's D3 narrowed the missing-connections defect rather than ending it: the replacement comment claimed "the shape is the dialect's own (`automation.resolveArgs`)" while `StepReferences` ran a `json.Decoder` with `DisallowUnknownFields` and a `From *string` of its own. Driven end to end through the real dialect: `{"$from":"steps.fetch.summary","note":"annotated"}` PARSES (`Step.Args` is `map[string]json.RawMessage`, so the document loader's strictness never reaches inside an argument value) and the executor RESOLVES it — `digest` really does receive step `fetch`'s output — while the reader returned `map[]`, showing no connection at all for a step that consumes the previous one's. And `{"$from":""}`, which the executor passes through as the literal it is, rendered as an edge to nowhere. Both are false statements about how the stages connect, which is the one thing S15.10 asks this surface for. **Fixed by deletion, not by a better copy:** the predicate is EXPORTED from the dialect as `automation.Reference`, `resolveArgs` calls it, and `worker.StepReferences` calls it — one expression, the twin-hazard rule this codebase already applies to `rosterVisible`, `meterReading` and `acceptable()`. The false comment is corrected where it stood. Probe: restoring the local decoder fires `TestWorkforceStepEdgesUseTheDialectsOwnPredicate` on both limbs at once (`map[blank:]`).
- **R2 — TWO COUNTS THAT DID NOT COUNT WHAT THEIR NAMES SAY.** `runs_routed` counted routing DECISIONS. A run can be routed to one version more than once — `override` is a cause this fixture world already carries — so re-routing `r-notes` to the same version served **`runs_routed=4` for 3 distinct runs**, while the field name, the Go doc ("how many **runs**") and the render ("N routed in this reading") all said runs. **That is the fabricated-figure class in another currency**, and this packet has now been shown it four times. The tally had the same defect squared: the duplicated row re-counted that run's rounds, so ONE recorded SHIP tallied as **`SHIP: 2`**. And it was **silently bounded** — 25 REWORK rounds tallied as 20, because `workforceVerdictsPerRun` cuts what reaches the tally and `verdicts_truncated` lives per run where the tally never looks. **Both fixed:** the count is of DISTINCT runs (a decision naming no run counts as no run, and its row still renders with both absences stated); each run contributes to a version's tally ONCE; and `verdict_tally_truncated` says the tally is a floor whenever either bound cut it, rendered beside the numbers, because a bound nobody can see is a silent cap. `Runs`'s own doc now says it is one row per DECISION, which is what makes two numbers the right answer rather than a contradiction. Probes: counting rows fires with "runs_routed = 4 for 3 distinct runs"; dropping the per-run dedupe fires with "SHIP: 2 for ONE recorded SHIP round"; dropping the marker fires with "25 recorded rounds tally as 20 with nothing saying the number is a floor".
- **R3 — THE UNPRICED `USD 0`, ONE SURFACE OVER FROM WHERE THIS PACKET KEPT LOOKING.** A subscription lane prices UNPRICED, so the seam serves cost `0` with `Unpriced: true` — and `RunCounters` carried no `unpriced` field, so `GET /api/runs/r-ship` served `{api_equiv_cost_usd: 0}` and `TaskDetail.tsx` rendered a bare **"USD 0"**. That asserts the run was free when the cost is genuinely unknown: reachable in production, and the exact class the nil-able field beside it exists to prevent in the other direction. The map's own `WorkforceRoutedRun` had carried the marking since part B landed, so §45-B's "`meterReading` is THE expression shared by the task list, the run card and the map" was true of the helper and **not true of what the three surfaces render** — corrected in place above. **Additive only:** `RunCounters.Unpriced` mirrors the landed field, `projection.go` sets it beside the figure, nothing else in `reads.go`/`projection.go` moved, and the ripple is one web type plus one render site. **The guard was pointed away from it too** — drain r1's quantified money scan is scoped to `.workforce .money` and `taskdetail.test.tsx` had no money guard at all — so the run card gets its own, quantified over every money span it produces, both directions. Probes: dropping the Go assignment fires `TestRunCardCarriesTheUnpricedMarkingWithTheFigure`; dropping the render fires both new web tests.
- **R4 — THE SAME DEFECT SIGN-FLIPPED, and it withholds a TRUE figure.** `meterReading` keyed on folded MAGNITUDES, and the ledger deliberately prices a local duty call a true $0 on the permanent free tier (the zero-allowance row, S12.1). A run whose recorded work was local folds to zero tokens, zero cost and zero unpriced calls — a real measurement, indistinguishable from a run nobody has touched, served as an absence. **Carried, because it is additive and confined to the seam plus its one shell composition:** `api.RunMeter.Calls` is what the ledger FOLDED, `projMeter` fills it from `RunConsumption.TotalCalls`, and `meterReading` reads `Calls > 0` first. The magnitude arms STAY as the fallback: a seam that does not report a count reports 0, and withdrawing a figure it did serve is the worse of the two errors. Probe: `TestMeteredZeroIsAReadingAndUnmeteredIsNot` seeds a real local checkpoint through the wire contract `internal/local` writes, asks the REAL ledger (1 call, $0 priced, 0 unpriced), and fires on the magnitudes-only predicate with "a MEASURED zero was served as an absence" — plus the other direction, that a run with no checkpoints still has no reading.
- **R5 — A LITERAL PIN THAT PINNED ITSELF.** `if len(first) != api.WorkforceRunsPerVersion` moves both sides together, so it could not fail — the tautology class part A removed twice. It is the literal `20` now, beside `:814`'s real literal. And the export earns its place instead of being deleted: the assertion below it reasons "20 rows is far inside the 100-run meter cap", which is a RELATION between two independent bounds, so that relation is now checked rather than assumed — without it, lowering `workforceMeterCap` would make the budget check fail for a reason unrelated to what it tests. Probe: moving `workforceRunsPerVersion` to 19 fires the literal; the self-reference could not.
- **R6 — THE RENDERED TALLY NUMBER WAS UNPINNED WEB-SIDE.** Every tally in the committed body carries exactly 1 round, so rendering the constant `1` instead of `String(t.rounds)` passed the whole suite. The Go side pins the content, so this was a layering gap rather than an unpinned fact — but a render bug printing the wrong count would have shipped. Pinned with counts that are neither 1 nor each other (7 and 2), asserting the exact rendered pair and the `data-rounds` attribute. Probe: the constant fires it.
- **R7 — A POLICY CONSEQUENCE DECIDED IN THE BROWSER, AND FORGOTTEN ON HALF THE BLOCKS.** `Workforce.tsx` derived the schedule bar from `w.domain.maturity === 'degraded'` — a client-side side-truth the standing readings bar, and a forward-tolerance hole in drain r1's own new code, because a maturity this build has never heard of that ALSO bars schedules would go unmarked even when the server knows it does. The flag also had to be handed to `Grants` by its caller, and `VersionBlock` did not, so **a superseded version in a degraded domain rendered "granted" with no bar at all**. **Served instead:** `WorkforceGrants.ScheduleBarred` is decided where the maturity vocabulary is defined and rides the grants row, so every render of a granted block carries its own and there is no prop to forget. One golden line moved (`wt-digest`'s block gains `schedule_barred: true`) through the real regeneration path. Probes: forcing the server field false fires the Go test; ignoring the served field fires both web tests, including one driven with a maturity string (`quarantined`) no client vocabulary knows.
- **R8 — D5's SERVER HALF, LOCALIZED.** Forcing `ScheduleAttachable: false` passed every `-run TestWorkforce` test and only `TestWebAPIFixtures` fired. Golden drift is a real net, so this was about localization rather than safety — but it made drain r1's blanket "every fix carries a check that FAILS on the pre-fix code" untrue as written. **The assertion was added rather than the claim weakened**, which is the resolution that leaves the sentence true; the r1 preamble is corrected in place anyway, because the claim was false when it was made. No grep-for-a-sentence guard was added. R6's pin closes the other half of the same blanket claim.
- **R9 — PROSE ONLY, and named as the exception.** `api_test.go`'s "nil = counters best-effort (zero)" was falsified by drain r1: a nil meter now serves the cost as an absence. Corrected in place, and so are the two production comments that said the same thing (`MeterReader`'s doc and `Config.Meter`'s), which the finding did not name but which were false for the same reason. No probe: a comment fix carries no check, and bolting a text guard onto one would be a check that tests nothing (the D8 precedent).
- **R10 — ACCEPTED, recorded, not changed.** D10's identity pin (`fxPinDryRunProposal`) drops the `LIMIT`, so two automations sharing service + step + verb would silently pick one row. It is **strictly better than the `ORDER BY rowid DESC LIMIT 1` it replaced** — that one picked the wrong row the moment a second automation joined, this one only when two automations share all three identity fields — and it is unreachable in the fixture world, which carries exactly one automation. Left as it is.
- **R11 — THE INERT-PROBE DISCLOSURE, CLOSED WHERE IT IS THIS PACKET'S.** The re-check swept the class and found most instances are pre-existing B6-5/B6-6 tests, NOT charged here and carried to the gate. The one that is this packet's: `workforce_test.go`'s "non-tautological control" verified a claim about **production** `Ledger.RunConsumption` by asking `fixtureMeter{}` — a stub written to match the claim, so it could only agree. **It asks the real ledger now** (`r-claim` is a real run row with no checkpoints: zeros, `err == nil`, `TotalCalls == 0`), which also grounds R4's discriminator. Probe: pointing it at a run the ledger does not have fires it. Separately, five `workforce.test.tsx` test NAMES claimed server properties over hand-built bodies — correct as render tests, over-claiming in prose — and each now says what it drives and where the server property is driven instead.
- **REPORT — a landed number that does not reproduce.** Drain r1 recorded `workforce_test.go` at **143** after its round; re-measured at that commit it is **145**, by `grep -cE 't\.(Error|Errorf|Fatal|Fatalf)\('` and by three other spellings of the same count. The other three files reproduce exactly. The r1 record is left standing as the dated statement it is; the method is written here so the next round measures the same thing, and 145 is the baseline r2 counts against.

#### §45-B post-cap coordinator items (P3-B6-8 part B, RES-1 — implemented inline; the drain cap was exhausted)

The final re-check returned **FAIL on a single residual**, and everything else it examined it closed by re-driving rather than by reading. **The sharpest question of the whole packet was answered here and the answer was a refutation of the coordinator, backed by a differential rather than an argument.** Drain r2 fixed a *rendering* gap by exporting the dialect's edge predicate and re-pointing `resolveArgs` at it — which meant a live automation-execution path moved on the last round of a drain, and since the two predicates provably differed, unifying them *necessarily* moved one of them. The re-check lifted the old inline predicate byte-for-byte from `ec76960` and ran the old executor form, the new `automation.Reference` and the old strict roster form across **23 argument shapes** — `{"$from":""}`, sibling keys in both orders, nested objects, non-string `$from` (number, bool, array, null), non-object args, whitespace, trailing content, duplicate keys, case variance. **The executor predicate changed on 0 of 23 inputs; the renderer changed on 4.** The one that moved was the renderer, onto the executor, and automation argument resolution at runtime is bit-identical. One of those four is a correction the round-2 review itself had missed: `{"$from":"payload.a"} trailing` drew a **phantom edge** under the old `json.Decoder.Decode`, which stops at the first value, and is now rejected — which is what the executor has always done.

- **RES-1 — the attribute was pinned and the RENDERED number was not, so the two could disagree and ship.** `workforce.test.tsx`'s per-version walk asserted `[data-runs-routed]`'s attribute against the served value and never read the element's text. Replacing the rendered `{String(v.outcomes.runs_routed)} routed in this reading` with a constant `99`, leaving the attribute correct, **passed the entire suite**. This is the same gap R6 closed for the verdict tally one bullet down, left open on the sibling number — the count-vs-enumeration shape again, fixed in one place and not its twin. The walk now also asserts the element's text contains the served count. **Probe: with the constant planted the assertion fires with `Received: "99 routed in this reading"`; reverted, the suite is green.** This is the one post-cap item, and unlike part A's five it carries a check that fails on the pre-fix code.
- **NOTE-1, recorded rather than actioned (arguable either way, and behaviour).** An unknown domain maturity that *ought* to bar schedules renders "granted" with no caveat, because the server computes `ScheduleBarred: g.ScheduleAttachable && dom.Maturity == degraded`. §45-B's own claim is honest as written — it says the decision now lives where the vocabulary is defined, one change away, not that unknown maturities are handled today — and **inventing a bar for a maturity nobody has defined would fabricate policy**, which is the failure this packet spent four rounds removing. Left as the known shape of the forward-tolerance edge.
- **NOTE-2, recorded and explicitly NOT this packet's.** `encoding/json` matches field tags case-insensitively, so the dialect resolves `{"$FROM":"x"}` as a reference. Pre-existing, and **both** the old and the new predicates agree about it, so R1 did not move it.
- **A correction the re-check made to ITSELF, which is why the 143/145 note above stands as it does.** In its round-2 report it measured `workforce_test.go` at 145 where drain r1 had recorded 143, and wrote the difference off as "a counting-method difference". On re-measuring, the method reproduces exactly by three independent spellings: **145 is right, r1's 143 was simply wrong, and the method is not soft.** It should have flagged the discrepancy rather than excused it — recorded because an evaluator that corrects its own report is the posture this phase has relied on, and because a metric used to prove "nothing was weakened" is only worth having if a mismatch in it is treated as a finding.
- **What this section can and cannot claim.** RES-1 was implemented after the cap, so **no fresh adversarial pass reviewed it** — but it is a test-only addition that changes no production behaviour, and it carries its own reverting probe, which is the strongest claim a post-cap item in this phase has been able to make. NOTE-1 and NOTE-2 change nothing and are records.
